Topological 8d 𝒩 = 1 \mathcal{N}=1 Gauge Theory: Novel Floer Homologies, and A ∞ A_{\infty} -categories of Six, Five, and Four-Manifolds
Arif Er, Meng-Chwan Tan

Abstract
This work is a continuation of the program initiated in [ 15 ] . We show how one can define novel gauge-theoretic (holomorphic) Floer homologies of seven, six, and five-manifolds, from the physics of a topologically-twisted 8d 𝒩 = 1 \mathcal{N}=1 gauge theory on a Spin ( 7 ) (7) -manifold via its supersymmetric quantum mechanics interpretation. They are associated with G 2 G_{2} instanton, Donaldson-Thomas, and Haydys-Witten configurations on the seven, six, and five-manifolds, respectively. We also show how one can define hyperkähler Floer homologies specified by hypercontact three-manifolds, and symplectic Floer homologies of instanton moduli spaces. In turn, this will allow us to derive Atiyah-Floer type dualities between the various gauge-theoretic Floer homologies and symplectic intersection Floer homologies of instanton moduli spaces. Via a 2d gauged Landau-Ginzburg model interpretation of the 8d theory, one can derive novel Fukaya-Seidel type A ∞ A_{\infty} -categories that categorify Donaldson-Thomas, Haydys-Witten, and Vafa-Witten configurations on six, five, and four-manifolds, respectively – thereby categorifying the aforementioned Floer homologies of six and five-manifolds, and the Floer homology of four-manifolds from [ 15 ] – where an Atiyah-Floer type correspondence for the Donaldson-Thomas case can be established. Last but not least, topological invariance of the theory suggests a relation amongst these Floer homologies and Fukaya-Seidel type A ∞ A_{\infty} -categories for certain Spin ( 7 ) (7) -manifolds. Our work therefore furnishes purely physical proofs and generalizations of the conjectures by Donaldson-Thomas [ 10 ] , Donaldson-Segal [ 12 ] , Cherkis [ 6 ] , Hohloch-Noetzel-Salamon [ 21 ] , Salamon [ 26 ] , Haydys [ 20 ] , and Bousseau [ 5 ] , and more.



Introduction, Summary and Acknowledgements

Introduction

In
[
1
]
, Acharya-O’Loughlin-Spence studied an 8d
\mathcal{N}=1
topologically-twisted gauge theory on a certain eight-manifold where they predicted that one could physically derive from it, a Floer homology of a corresponding seven-manifold.
Incidentally, this gauge-theoretic Floer homology was also conjectured by Donaldson-Thomas in a program they initiated in
[
10
]
.
This program was continued by Donaldson-Segal in
[
12
]
, where they conjectured, with support from some preliminary computations, a gauge-theoretic Floer homology of a corresponding six-manifold.
Parallel to these efforts, a separate program was launched by Hohloch-Noetzel-Salamon in
[
21
,
26
]
to define a novel hyperkähleric Floer homology assigned to a three-four manifold pair, which they conjectured could be derived from the Donaldson-Thomas program in the adiabatic limit.

In this paper, we continue the program initiated in
[
15
]
, and aim to physically derive these mathematically conjectured Floer homologies, amongst other things.
To this end, we will study, on various decomposable eight-manifolds with Spin
(7)
holonomy in different topological limits, the aforementioned 8d
\mathcal{N}=1
topologically-twisted gauge theory whose BPS equation is the Spin
(7)
instanton equation.
As an offshoot, we would also be able to derive a web of mathematically-novel relations amongst these Floer homologies, their Atiyah-Floer dualities, and more.

The computational techniques we employ are mainly those of standard Kaluza-Klein reduction; generalizations of the topological reduction pioneered in
[
3
]
; recasting gauge theories as supersymmetric quantum mechanics (SQM) as pioneered in
[
4
]
; the physical realization of Floer homology groups via SQM in infinite-dimensional space as elucidated in
[
25
]
; and the physical realization of Fukaya-Seidel type
A_{\infty}
-categories via a soliton string theory in infinite-dimensional space as elucidated in
[
15
]
.

Let us now give a brief plan and summary of the paper.

A Brief Plan and Summary of the Paper

In

§

2
, we discuss general aspects of a topologically-twisted 8d
\mathcal{N}=1
theory on an eight-manifold with Spin
(7)
holonomy (a Spin
(7)
-manifold) with a “trivial twist”, where the gauge group
G
is taken to be a real, simple, compact Lie group.

In

§

3
, we let the Spin
(7)
-manifold be
\text{Spin}(7)=G_{2}\times\mathbb{R}
, where
G_{2}
is a closed and compact seven-manifold with
G_{2}
holonomy (a
G_{2}
-manifold).
We recast the aforementioned Spin
(7)
theory as a 1d SQM in the space
\mathfrak{A}_{7}
of irreducible gauge connections
A
on the
G_{2}
-manifold with action (
3.3
).
This will in turn allow us to express the partition function as (
3.11
):

\boxed{\mathcal{Z}_{\text{Spin}(7),G_{2}\times\mathbb{R}}(G)=\sum_{j}\mathcal{F}^{G}_{\text{Spin}(7)}(\Psi_{G_{2}}^{j})=\sum_{j}\text{HF}^{\text{Spin}(7)\text{-inst}}_{d_{j}}(G_{2},G)=\mathcal{Z}^{\text{Floer}}_{\text{Spin}(7)\text{-inst},G_{2}}(G)}

(1.1)

where
\text{HF}^{\text{Spin}(7)\text{-inst}}_{d_{j}}(G_{2},G)
is a
novel
Spin
(7)
instanton Floer homology class assigned to a
G_{2}
-manifold, of degree
d_{j}
, defined by Floer differentials described by the gradient flow equations (
3.7
):

\boxed{\derivative{A^\alpha}{t}=-g^{\alpha\beta}_{\mathfrak{A}_{7}}\partialderivative{V_7}{A^\beta}}

(1.2)

and Morse functional (
3.9
):

\boxed{V_{7}(A,\varphi)=\int_{G_{2}}\,\Tr\,\left(CS(A)\wedge\star\phi_{t}\right)}

(1.3)

The chains of the Spin
(7)
instanton Floer complex are generated by fixed critical points of
V_{7}
, which correspond to
time-invariant
G_{2}
instanton configurations
on the
G_{2}
-manifold given by time-independent solutions to the 7d equation (
3.10
):

\boxed{F\wedge\star\phi_{t}=0}

(1.4)

Note that
\text{HF}^{\text{Spin}(7)\text{-inst}}_{d_{j}}(G_{2},G)
was first mathematically conjectured to exist by Donaldson-Thomas
[
10
,
\lx@sectionsign 3
]
as a Floer homology generated by
G_{2}
instantons on a
G_{2}
-manifold whose flow lines are time-varying solutions to the Spin
(7)
instanton equation on
G_{2}\times\mathbb{R}
.
We have therefore furnished a physical proof of their mathematical conjecture.

In

§

4
, we let
G_{2}=CY_{3}\times S^{1}
, where
CY_{3}
is a closed and compact Calabi-Yau threefold, and perform a Kaluza-Klein (KK) dimensional reduction of Spin
(7)
theory on
G_{2}\times\mathbb{R}
by shrinking
S^{1}
to be infinitesimally small.
We obtain the corresponding 1d SQM theory in the space
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
of irreducible
(\mathcal{A},C)
fields on
CY_{3}
with action (
4.5
), where
\mathcal{A}\in\Omega^{(1,0)}(CY_{3},\text{ad}(G))
and
C\in\Omega^{0}(CY_{3},\text{ad}(G))
are a holomorphic gauge connection and real scalar, respectively, that is equivalent to the resulting 7d-Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
.
As before, this will allow us to express the partition function as (
4.7
):

\boxed{\mathcal{Z}_{\text{Spin}(7),CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}}(G)=\sum_{k}\mathcal{F}^{G}_{\text{7d-Spin}(7)}(\Psi_{CY_{3}}^{k})=\sum_{k}\text{HHF}^{G_{2}\text{-M}}_{d_{k}}(CY_{3},G)=\mathcal{Z}^{\text{Floer}}_{G_{2}\text{-M},CY_{3}}(G)}

(1.5)

where
\text{HHF}^{G_{2}\text{-M}}_{d_{k}}(CY_{3},G)
is a
novel
holomorphic
G_{2}
monopole (
G_{2}
-M) Floer homology class assigned to
CY_{3}
, of degree
d_{k}
, defined by Floer differentials described by the holomorphic gradient flow equation (
4.8
):

\boxed{\derivative{\mathcal{A}^\alpha}{t}=-g^{\alpha\bar{\beta}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{6}}\left(\partialderivative{V_6}{\mathcal{A}^\beta}\right)^{*}\qquad\derivative{C^\alpha}{t}=-g^{\alpha\bar{\beta}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{6}}\left(\partialderivative{V_6}{C^\beta}\right)^{*}}

(1.6)

and holomorphic Morse functional (
4.9
):

\boxed{V_{6}(\mathcal{A},C)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{CY_{3}}\Tr\left(CS(\mathcal{A})\wedge\bar{\star}\Lambda+2C\wedge\mathcal{F}^{(1,1)}\wedge\bar{\star}\omega\right)}

(1.7)

where
\Lambda
is a holomorphic three-form and
\omega
is the Kähler two-form of
CY_{3}
.
The chains of the holomorphic
G_{2}
-M Floer complex are generated by fixed critical points of
V_{6}
, which correspond to
time-invariant Donaldson-Thomas (DT) configurations on
CY_{3}
with the scalar being real
, given by time-independent solutions to the 6d equations (
4.10
):

\boxed{\omega\wedge\bar{\star}\mathcal{F}^{(1,1)}=0\qquad\mathcal{F}^{(2,0)}=0\qquad\mathcal{D}_{m}C=0}

(1.8)

Note that when
C=0
,
\text{HHF}^{G_{2}\text{-M}}_{d_{k}}(CY_{3},G)
becomes a holomorphic
G_{2}

instanton
Floer homology, generated by holomorphic vector bundles on
CY_{3}
(i.e., DT configurations on
CY_{3}
with
C=0
) whose flow lines correspond to time-varying solutions to the
G_{2}
instanton equation, which was mathematically conjectured to exist by Donaldson-Segal
[
12
,
\lx@sectionsign
4]
.
We have therefore furnished a physical proof and generalization (when
C\neq 0
) of their mathematical conjecture.

Furthermore, when the complexification of
CY_{3}
is undone and the results are instead expressed in real components, we will find that we have a
real
Floer homology of
CY_{3}
generated by fixed points of the
real-valued
Morse functional – the Chern-Simons-Higgs functional – whose flow lines correspond to time-varying solutions to the
G_{2}
monopole equation on
CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
.
This result has previously been speculated by Cherkis
[
6
,
\lx@sectionsign
7]
.
We have therefore furnished a physical proof of his speculation.

In

§

5
, we further specialize to the case where
CY_{3}=CY_{2}\times S^{1}\times S^{1}
, where
CY_{2}
is a closed and compact Calabi-Yau twofold, and perform another KK dimensional reduction of 7d-Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
by shrinking one of the
S^{1}
circles to be infinitesimally small.
We obtain the corresponding 1d SQM theory in the space
\mathfrak{A}_{5}
of irreducible
(\hat{\mathcal{A}},\hat{\mathcal{B}},\Gamma)
fields on
CY_{2}\times S^{1}
that is equivalent to the resulting 6d theory on
CY_{2}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
.
Here, (i)
\hat{\mathcal{A}}\in\Omega^{0}(S^{1},\text{ad}(G))\otimes\Omega^{(1,0)}(CY_{2},\text{ad}(G))
, (ii)
\hat{\mathcal{B}}\in\Omega^{0}(S^{1},\text{ad}(G))\otimes\Omega^{2,+}(CY_{2},\text{ad}(G))
, and (iii)
\Gamma\in\Omega^{1}(S^{1},\text{ad}(G))\otimes\Omega^{0}(CY_{2},\text{ad}(G))
are (i) a real scalar (holomorphic gauge connection), (ii) a real scalar (real self-dual two-form), and (iii) a real gauge connection (real scalar), respectively.
Again, this will also allow us to express the partition function as (
5.6
):

\boxed{\mathcal{Z}_{\text{Spin}(7),CY_{2}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}}(G)=\sum_{l}\mathcal{F}^{G}_{\text{6d-Spin}(7)}(\Psi_{CY_{2}\times S^{1}}^{l})=\sum_{l}\text{HHF}^{\text{DT}}_{d_{l}}(CY_{2}\times S^{1},G)=\mathcal{Z}^{\text{Floer}}_{\text{DT},CY_{2}\times S^{1}}(G)}

(1.9)

where
\text{HHF}^{\text{DT}}_{d_{l}}(CY_{2}\times S^{1},G)
is a
novel
holomorphic DT Floer homology class assigned to
CY_{2}\times S^{1}
, of degree
d_{l}
, defined by Floer differentials described by the holomorphic gradient flow equations (
5.7
):

\boxed{\derivative{\hat{\mathcal{A}}^\alpha}{t}=-g^{\alpha\bar{\beta}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{5}}\left(\partialderivative{V_5}{\hat{\mathcal{A}}^\beta}\right)^{*}\qquad\derivative{\hat{\mathcal{B}}^\alpha}{t}=-g^{\alpha\bar{\beta}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{5}}\left(\partialderivative{V_5}{\hat{\mathcal{B}}^\beta}\right)^{*}\qquad\derivative{\Gamma^\alpha}{t}=-g^{\alpha\bar{\beta}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{5}}\left(\partialderivative{V_5}{\Gamma^\beta}\right)^{*}}

(1.10)

and holomorphic Morse functional (
5.8
):

\boxed{V_{5}(\hat{\mathcal{A}},\hat{\mathcal{B}},\Gamma)=\int\frac{i}{2}d_{y}\left(\hat{\mathcal{A}}\wedge\bar{\star}\hat{\mathcal{A}}+\hat{\mathcal{B}}\wedge\bar{\star}\hat{\mathcal{B}}\right)-2i\hat{\mathcal{B}}\wedge\bar{\star}\hat{\mathcal{F}}-\Gamma\wedge\hat{\omega}\wedge\bar{\star}\left(\hat{\mathcal{F}}-\frac{1}{4}(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\times\hat{\mathcal{B}})\right)}

(1.11)

where
\hat{\omega}
is the Kähler two-form of
CY_{2}
.
The chains of the holomorphic DT Floer complex are generated by fixed critical points of
V_{5}
, which correspond to
time-invariant HW configurations on
CY_{2}\times S^{1}
with one of the linearly-independent components of the self-dual two-form field being zero
, given by time-independent solutions to the 5d equations (
5.9
):

\boxed{\begin{aligned} d_{y}\hat{\mathcal{A}}+\bar{\star}(\hat{\mathcal{D}}\bar{\star}\hat{\mathcal{B}})&=\hat{\mathcal{D}}\Gamma\\
d_{y}\hat{\mathcal{B}}+\frac{1}{2}(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\times\hat{\mathcal{B}})-2\hat{\mathcal{F}}&=[\hat{\mathcal{B}},\Gamma]\end{aligned}}

(1.12)

In

§

6
, we generalize to the case where
\text{Spin}(7)=CY_{2}\times HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
, with
HC_{3}
being a hypercontact three-manifold.
Topologically reducing Spin
(7)
theory along
CY_{2}
, we arrive at a 4d
\mathcal{N}=2
sigma model with target space the moduli space
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
of instantons on
CY_{2}
, whose BPS equation is the Cauchy-Riemann-Fueter equation on
HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
.
We then obtain the corresponding 1d SQM theory in the hypercontact three-space
\mathcal{M}(HC_{3},\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})
of smooth maps from
HC_{3}
to
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
.
Just as before, this will allow us to express the partition function as (
6.15
)

\boxed{\begin{aligned} \mathcal{Z}_{\text{Spin}(7),HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}}(G)&=\sum_{s}\mathcal{F}^{s}_{\text{4d-}\sigma,HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}}\\
&=\sum_{s}\text{HHKF}_{d_{s}}\left(HC_{3},\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})\right)=\mathcal{Z}^{\text{hyperkählerFloer}}_{HC_{3},\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}}\end{aligned}}

(1.13)

where
\text{HHKF}_{d_{s}}(HC_{3},\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2}))
is a
novel
hyperkähler Floer homology class of a hyperkähler manifold
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
and specified by a hypercontact three-manifold
HC_{3}
, of degree
d_{s}
, defined by Floer differentials described by the gradient flow equation (
6.16
):

\boxed{\derivative{X^\alpha}{t}=-g^{\alpha\beta}_{\mathcal{M}(HC_{3},\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})}\partialderivative{V_{\sigma}}{X^\beta}}

(1.14)

and Morse functional (
6.17
):

\boxed{V_{\sigma}(X)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{HC_{3}}\differential[3]{x}\left(\sum_{a}\partial_{a}(X\wedge\star X)J_{a}\right)}

(1.15)

where
J_{a}
for
a\in\{1,2,3\}
are the three complex structures of the hyperkähler
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
.
The chains of the hyperkähler Floer complex are generated by fixed critical points of
V_{\sigma}
, which correspond to
time-invariant Fueter maps from
HC_{3}
to
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
given by time-independent solutions of the 3d equation (
6.18
):

\boxed{\sum_{a}\partial_{a}X^{i}J_{a}=0}

(1.16)

Note that the existence of
\text{HHKF}_{d_{s}}(HC_{3},\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2}))
, derived from the topological reduction of the Spin
(7)
instanton equation on
CY_{2}\times HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
along
CY_{2}
, was first conjectured by Hohloch-Noetzel-Salamon
[
21
]

[
26
,
\lx@sectionsign 5
]
.
We have therefore furnished a physical proof and realization of Hohloch-Noetzel-Salamon’s mathematical conjecture.

In

§

7
, we specialize to several specific cases of
HC_{3}
.
First, in the case of
HC_{3}=T^{3}
, where we can interpret the hypercontact three-space
\mathcal{M}(T^{3},\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})
of maps from
T^{3}
to
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
as the triple loop space
L^{3}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
, we arrive at the following identification of a hyperkähler Floer homology of
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
and specified by
T^{3}
as a symplectic Floer homology in (
7.1
):

\boxed{\text{HHKF}_{d_{s}}\left(T^{3},\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})\right)=\text{HSF}^{\text{Fuet}}_{d_{s}}\left(L^{3}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}\right)}

(1.17)

where
\text{HSF}^{\text{Fuet}}_{d_{s}}(L^{3}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})
is a
novel
symplectic Floer homology class of
L^{3}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
generated by
time-invariant Fueter maps
from
T^{3}
to
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
.

Second, in the case where
HC_{3}=I\times T^{2}
, we recast the 4d sigma model as a 2d A-model on
I\times\mathbb{R}
with target space being the double loop space
L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
of maps from
T^{2}
to
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
.
We then obtain the corresponding 1d SQM theory in the space
\mathcal{T}(\mathscr{L}_{0},\mathscr{L}_{1})_{L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}}
of smooth trajectories between isotropic-coisotropic A-branes starting at
\mathscr{L}_{0}
and ending at
\mathscr{L}_{1}
in
L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
with action (
7.5
).
This will allow us to express the partition function as (
7.7
):

\boxed{\begin{aligned} \mathcal{Z}_{\text{Spin}(7),I\times T^{2}\times\mathbb{R}}(G)&=\sum_{s}\mathcal{F}^{s}_{\text{2d-}\sigma,I\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}}\\
&=\sum_{s}\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{d_{s}}\left(L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}},\mathscr{L}_{0},\mathscr{L}_{1}\right)=\mathcal{Z}^{\text{IntSympFloer}}_{\mathscr{L}_{0},\mathscr{L}_{1},L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}}\end{aligned}}

(1.18)

where
\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{d_{s}}(L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}},\mathscr{L}_{0},\mathscr{L}_{1})
is a
novel
symplectic intersection Floer homology class generated by the intersection points of
\mathscr{L}_{0}
and
\mathscr{L}_{1}
, of degree
d_{s}
, counted by the Floer differentials realized as flow lines of the SQM, whose gradient flow equations are defined by setting to zero the expression within the squared term in (
7.3
).
Doing so, we will arrive at the following identification of a hyperkähler Floer homology of
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
and specified by
I\times T^{2}
as a symplectic intersection Floer homology of
L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
in (
7.8
):

\boxed{\text{HHKF}_{d_{s}}\left(I\times T^{2},\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})\right)=\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{d_{s}}\left(L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}},\mathscr{L}_{0},\mathscr{L}_{1}\right)}

(1.19)

Lastly, in the case where
HC_{3}=I\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
, we recast the 4d sigma model as a 2d A
θ
-model on
I\times\mathbb{R}
with target space being the path space
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})
of maps from
\mathbb{R}
to the loop space
L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
, which in turn is the space of maps from
S^{1}
to the
\theta
-deformed
\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
.
We then obtain the corresponding 1d SQM theory in the space
\mathcal{T}(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1})_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})}
of smooth trajectories between A
θ
-branes starting at
\mathcal{P}_{0}(\theta)
and ending at
\mathcal{P}_{1}(\theta)
in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})
with action (
7.11
).
This will allow us to express the partition function as (
7.12
):

\boxed{\begin{aligned} \mathcal{Z}_{\text{Spin}(7),I\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}(G)&=\sum_{s}\mathcal{F}^{s}_{\text{2d-}\sigma,I\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})}\\
&=\sum_{s}\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{d_{s}}\left(\mathcal{M}\left(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}\right),\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1}\right)=\mathcal{Z}^{\text{IntSympFloer}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})}\end{aligned}}

(1.20)

where
\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{d_{s}}\big(\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}),\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1}\big)
is a
novel
symplectic intersection Floer homology class generated by the intersection points of
\mathcal{P}_{0}(\theta)
and
\mathcal{P}_{1}(\theta)
, of degree
d_{s}
, counted by the Floer differentials realized as flow lines of the SQM, whose gradient flow equations are defined by setting to zero the expression within the squared term in (
7.11
).
Doing so, we will arrive at the following identification of a hyperkähler Floer homology of
\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
and specified by
I\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
as a symplectic intersection Floer homology of
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})
in (
7.13
):

\boxed{\text{HHKF}_{d_{s}}\left(I\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R},\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})\right)=\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{d_{s}}\left(\mathcal{M}\left(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}\right),\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1}\right)}

(1.21)

In

§

8
, we consider Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
, and split it into two halves by performing a Tyurin degeneration of
CY_{3}
along a
CY_{2}
surface.
Doing so, when
M_{1}=S^{1}
, via the topological invariance of Spin
(7)
theory and the results of

§

7.2
, we will obtain a Spin
(7)
Atiyah-Floer type duality of
CY_{3}\times S^{1}
, between the gauge-theoretic Spin
(7)
instanton Floer homology of
CY_{3}\times S^{1}
and the symplectic intersection Floer homology of
L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
in (
8.4
):

\boxed{\text{HF}^{\text{Spin}(7)\text{-inst}}_{*}(CY_{3}\times S^{1},G)\cong\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{*}\left(L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}},\mathscr{L}_{0},\mathscr{L}_{1}\right)}

(1.22)

In turn, this will lead us to a 7d-Spin
(7)
Atiyah-Floer type duality of
CY_{3}
, between the gauge-theoretic holomorphic
G_{2}
monopole Floer homology of
CY_{3}
and the symplectic intersection Floer homology of
L\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
in (
8.8
):

\boxed{\text{HHF}^{G_{2}\text{-M}}_{*}(CY_{3},G)\cong\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{*}\left(L\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}},\mathcal{L}_{0},\mathcal{L}_{1}\right)}

(1.23)

where
\mathcal{L}_{*}
are isotropic-coisotropic A-branes in
L\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
.

In

§

9
, we consider the case where
\text{Spin}(7)=CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and recast Spin
(7)
theory as a 2d gauged Landau-Ginzburg (LG) model on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
with target space
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
.
In turn, this 2d gauged LG model can be recast as a 1d SQM theory in the path space
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{6})
of maps from
\mathbb{R}
to
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
.
From the SQM and its critical points that can be interpreted as LG
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-solitons in the 2d gauged LG model, we obtain (
9.33
):

\boxed{\text{Hom}(\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{DT}},\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{DT}})_{\pm}\Longleftrightarrow\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{p}}(p_{\text{DT},\pm}^{IJ})}

(1.24)

Here,
\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{p}}(p_{\text{DT},\pm}^{IJ})
is a Floer homology class, of degree
d_{p}
, generated by
p_{\text{DT},\pm}^{IJ}
, the intersection points of left and right thimbles representing LG
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-solitons that can be described as morphisms
\text{Hom}(\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{DT}},\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{DT}})_{\pm}
whose endpoints
\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{DT}}
correspond to
DT configurations on
CY_{3}
with
C=0
.
Furthermore, via this equivalent description of Spin
(7)
theory as a 2d gauged LG model, we can interpret the normalized 8d partition function as a sum over tree-level scattering amplitudes of LG
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-soliton strings given by the composition map of morphisms (
9.36
):

\boxed{\mu^{n_{k}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{6}}:\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n_{k}}\text{Hom}\left(\mathcal{E}^{I_{i}}_{\text{DT}},\mathcal{E}^{I_{i+1}}_{\text{DT}}\right)_{-}\longrightarrow\text{Hom}\left(\mathcal{E}^{I_{1}}_{\text{DT}},\mathcal{E}^{I_{n_{k}+1}}_{\text{DT}}\right)_{+}}

(1.25)

where
\text{Hom}(\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{DT}},\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{DT}})_{-}
and
\text{Hom}(\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{DT}},\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{DT}})_{+}
represent incoming and outgoing scattering LG
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-soliton strings, as shown in
Fig. 6
.

Note that (
1.24
) and (
1.25
) underlie a
novel
Fukaya-Seidel (FS) type
A_{\infty}
-category of DT configurations on
CY_{3}
with
C=0
(i.e., holomorphic vector bundles on
CY_{3}
).
That such an FS type
A_{\infty}
-category of holomorphic vector bundles on
CY_{3}
can be derived from Spin
(7)
instantons on
CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, was conjectured by Haydys
[
20
]
.
As such, we have furnished a purely physical proof of his mathematical conjecture.

Next, applying the results of

§

8
with
M_{1}=\mathbb{R}
, we have the one-to-one correspondence

\boxed{\text{Hom}\left(\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{DT}}(\theta),\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{DT}}(\theta)\right)_{\pm}\Longleftrightarrow\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{*}\left(\mathcal{M}\left(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}\right),\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1}\right)}

(1.26)

in (
9.41
), which is a
novel
Atiyah-Floer type correspondence for the FS type
A_{\infty}
-category of holomorphic vector bundles on
CY_{3}
!
Furthermore, we would also obtain (
9.45
):

\boxed{\text{Hom}\left(\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{DT}}(\theta),\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{DT}}(\theta)\right)_{\pm}\Longleftrightarrow\text{Hom}\left(\text{Hom}\left[\mathcal{L}^{I}_{0}(\theta),\mathcal{L}^{I}_{1}(\theta)\right],\text{Hom}\left[\mathcal{L}^{J}_{0}(\theta),\mathcal{L}^{J}_{1}(\theta)\right]\right)_{\pm}}

(1.27)

Lastly, via (
1.26
) and (
1.27
), we would arrive at the one-to-one correspondence

\boxed{\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{*}\left(\mathcal{M}\left(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}\right),\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1}\right)\Longleftrightarrow\text{Hom}\left(\text{Hom}\left[\mathcal{L}^{I}_{0}(\theta),\mathcal{L}^{I}_{1}(\theta)\right],\text{Hom}\left[\mathcal{L}^{J}_{0}(\theta),\mathcal{L}^{J}_{1}(\theta)\right]\right)_{\pm}}

(1.28)

in (
9.46
), between a symplectic intersection Floer homology and a Hom-category of morphisms!

In

§

10
, we let
CY_{3}=CY_{2}\times S^{1}\times S^{1}
, and perform a KK dimensional reduction of Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
by shrinking one of the
S^{1}
circles to be infinitesimally small.
The resulting 7d-Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{2}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
is recast as a 2d gauged LG model on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
with target space
\mathscr{A}_{5}
of irreducible
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{C})
fields on
CY_{2}\times S^{1}
, where
\mathcal{A}\in\Omega^{0}(S^{1},\text{ad}(G))\otimes\Omega^{(1,0)}(CY_{2},\text{ad}(G))
and
\mathcal{C}\in\Omega^{0}(S^{1},\text{ad}(G_{\mathbb{C}}))\otimes\Omega^{0}(CY_{2},\text{ad}(G_{\mathbb{C}}))
are a real scalar (holomorphic gauge connection) and real scalar (real scalar), respectively, with
G_{\mathbb{C}}
being the corresponding complex Lie group.
In turn, this 2d gauged LG model can be recast as a 1d SQM theory in the path space
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathscr{A}_{5})
of maps from
\mathbb{R}
to
\mathscr{A}_{5}
.
From the SQM and its critical points that can be interpreted as LG
\mathscr{A}_{5}^{\theta}
-solitons in the 2d gauged LG model, we obtain (
10.21
):

\boxed{\text{Hom}(\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{HW}},\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{HW}})_{\pm}\Longleftrightarrow\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{q}}(p^{IJ}_{\text{HW},\pm})}

(1.29)

Here,
\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{q}}(p^{IJ}_{\text{HW},\pm})
is a Floer homology class, of degree
d_{q}
, generated by
p^{IJ}_{\text{HW},\pm}
, the intersection points of left and right thimbles representing LG
\mathscr{A}_{5}^{\theta}
-solitons that can be described as morphisms
\text{Hom}(\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{HW}},\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{HW}})_{\pm}
whose endpoints
\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{HW}}
correspond to
HW configurations on
CY_{2}\times S^{1}
with two linearly-independent components of the self-dual two-form field being zero
.
Again, via the equivalent description of 7d-Spin
(7)
theory as a 2d gauged LG model, we can interpret the normalized 7d partition function as a sum over tree-level scattering amplitudes of LG
\mathscr{A}_{5}^{\theta}
-soliton strings given by the composition map of morphisms (
10.24
):

\boxed{\mu^{n_{l}}_{\mathscr{A}_{5}}:\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n_{l}}\text{Hom}\left(\mathcal{E}^{I_{i}}_{\text{HW}},\mathcal{E}^{I_{i+1}}_{\text{HW}}\right)_{-}\longrightarrow\text{Hom}\left(\mathcal{E}^{I_{1}}_{\text{HW}},\mathcal{E}^{I_{n_{l}+1}}_{\text{HW}}\right)_{+}}

(1.30)

where
\text{Hom}(\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{HW}},\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{HW}})_{-}
and
\text{Hom}(\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{HW}},\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{HW}})_{+}
represent incoming and outgoing scattering LG
\mathscr{A}_{5}^{\theta}
-soliton strings.

Together, (
1.29
) and (
1.30
) underlie a
novel
FS type
A_{\infty}
-category of
CY_{2}\times S^{1}
that categorifies HW configurations on
CY_{2}\times S^{1}
with two linearly-independent components of the self-dual two-form field being zero!

In

§

11
, we perform yet another KK dimensional reduction of 7d-Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{2}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
by shrinking the remaining
S^{1}
circle to be infinitesimally small.
The resulting 6d-Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
is then recast as a 2d gauged LG model on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
with target space
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
of irreducible
(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})
fields on
CY_{2}
, where
\mathcal{A}\in\Omega^{(1,0)}(CY_{2},\text{ad}(G))
and
\mathcal{B}\in\Omega^{2,+}(CY_{2},\text{ad}(G))
are a holomorphic gauge connection and real self-dual two-form, respectively.
In turn, this 2d gauged LG model can be recast as a 1d SQM theory in the path space
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
of maps from
\mathbb{R}
to
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
.
From the SQM and its critical points that can be interpreted as LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-solitons in the 2d gauged LG model, we obtain (
11.20
):

\boxed{\text{Hom}(\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{VW}},\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{VW}})_{\pm}\Longleftrightarrow\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{r}}(p^{IJ}_{\text{VW},\pm})}

(1.31)

Here,
\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{r}}(p^{IJ}_{\text{VW},\pm})
is a Floer homology class, of degree
d_{r}
, generated by
p^{IJ}_{\text{VW},\pm}
, the intersection points of left and right thimbles representing LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-solitons that can be described as morphisms
\text{Hom}(\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{VW}},\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{VW}})_{\pm}
whose endpoints
\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{VW}}
correspond to
Vafa-Witten (VW) configurations on
CY_{2}
with the scalar and one of the linearly-independent components of the self-dual two-form field being zero
.
Once again, via the equivalent description of 6d-Spin
(7)
theory as a 2d gauged LG model, we can interpret the normalized 6d partition function as a sum over tree-level scattering amplitudes of LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-soliton strings given by the composition map of morphisms (
11.23
):

\boxed{\mu^{n_{m}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}:\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n_{m}}\text{Hom}\left(\mathcal{E}^{I_{i}}_{\text{VW}},\mathcal{E}^{I_{i+1}}_{\text{VW}}\right)_{-}\longrightarrow\text{Hom}\left(\mathcal{E}^{I_{1}}_{\text{VW}},\mathcal{E}^{I_{n_{m}+1}}_{\text{VW}}\right)_{+}}

(1.32)

where
\text{Hom}(\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{VW}},\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{VW}})_{-}
and
\text{Hom}(\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{VW}},\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{VW}})_{+}
represent incoming and outgoing scattering LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-soliton strings.

Together, (
1.31
) and (
1.32
) underlie a
novel
FS type
A_{\infty}
-category of
CY_{2}
that categorifies VW configurations on
CY_{2}
with the scalar and one of the linearly-independent components of the self-dual two-form field being zero!

In

§

12
, we first elucidate the implications of the topological invariance of Spin
(7)
theory on the Floer homologies obtained in

§

3
–

§

8
.
The results are given in (
12.1
)—(
12.9
), and they can be summarized as follows.

(1.33)

where

(1.34)

\boxed{\hbox to259.19pt{\vbox to78.93pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 129.59349pt\lower-39.46516pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

Note that the relation between hyperkähleric
\text{HHKF}_{d_{s}}(HC_{3},\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2}))
and gauge-theoretic
\text{HF}^{\text{Spin}(7)\text{-inst}}_{d_{j}}(HC_{3}\times CY_{2},G)
in (
1.34
) was conjectured by Hohloch-Noetzel-Salamon
[
21
]

[
26
,
\lx@sectionsign
5]
.
Furthermore, this relation was also conjectured by Salamon
[
26
,
\lx@sectionsign
5]
to be analogous to an Atiyah-Floer duality.
Indeed, we do see, from (
1.36
) and (
1.38
), that the two Floer homologies are related to each other by an Atiyah-Floer type duality between a gauge-theoretic and a symplectic intersection Floer homology.
We have therefore furnished a physical proof of their mathematical conjectures.

Second, we elucidate the implications of the topological invariance of Spin
(7)
theory on the FS type
A_{\infty}
-categories obtained in

§

9
–

§

11
.
The results are given in (
12.10
)–(
12.12
), and they can be summarized as follows.

where
CY_{3}

\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{DT}}(\theta)
’s in the topmost entry of (

) are defined on.

1.25
), the leftmost entry of (

).

(VII)

\boxed{\begin{gathered}\text{Hom}\left(\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{DT}}(\theta),\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{DT}}(\theta)\right)_{\pm}\\
\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 10.00002pt\;\,\displaystyle\left\Updownarrow\vphantom{\int}\right.\scriptstyle{CY_{3}=CY_{3}^{\prime}\bigcup_{CY_{2}}CY_{3}^{\prime\prime}}\\
\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{*}\left(\mathcal{M}\left(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}\right),\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1}\right)\\
\displaystyle\left\Updownarrow\vphantom{\int}\right.\\
\text{Hom}\left(\text{Hom}\left[\mathcal{L}^{I}_{0}(\theta),\mathcal{L}^{I}_{1}(\theta)\right],\text{Hom}\left[\mathcal{L}^{J}_{0}(\theta),\mathcal{L}^{J}_{1}(\theta)\right]\right)_{\pm}\end{gathered}}

(1.44)

Third, from (
12.14
) and (
9.45
), we would obtain (
12.15
):

\boxed{\begin{gathered}\text{Hom}\left(\text{Hom}\left[L^{I}_{0}(\theta),L^{I}_{1}(\theta)\right],\text{Hom}\left[L^{J}_{0}(\theta),L^{J}_{1}(\theta)\right]\right)_{\pm}\\
\displaystyle\left\Updownarrow\vphantom{\Big(}\right.\\
\text{Hom}\left(\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{DT}}(\theta),\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{DT}}(\theta)\right)_{\pm}\\
\displaystyle\left\Updownarrow\vphantom{\Big(}\right.\\
\text{Hom}\left(\text{HHF}^{G_{2}\text{-inst},\theta}(CY_{3},G),\text{HHF}^{G_{2}\text{-inst},\theta}(CY_{3},G)\right)_{\pm}\end{gathered}}

(1.45)

In other words, we have a correspondence amongst (i) a Hom-category of morphisms between Lagrangian submanifolds of
\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
, (ii) an FS type
A_{\infty}
-category of
\theta
-deformed holomorphic vector bundles on
CY_{3}
, and (iii) a holomorphic
\theta
-generalized
G_{2}
instanton Floer homology.
In particular, at
\theta=0
, we would have a correspondence amongst (i) a Hom-category of morphisms between Lagrangian submanifolds of
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
(which, for
CY_{2}
a complex algebraic surface, span the subspace of holomorphic vector bundles on
CY_{2}
that can be extended to all of
CY_{3}=CY_{3}^{\prime}\bigcup_{CY_{2}}CY_{3}^{\prime\prime}
), (ii) an FS type
A_{\infty}
-category of holomorphic vector bundles on
CY_{3}
, and (iii) a holomorphic
G_{2}
instanton Floer homology of
CY_{3}
.
As such a correspondence was conjectured by Bousseau
[
5
,
\lx@sectionsign
2.8]
, we have therefore furnished a physical proof and generalization (for
\theta\neq 0
) of his mathematical conjecture.

Fourth, we summarize the results uncovered thus far in
Fig. 1
, where we obtain a web of relations.
Therein, the sizes of the
\widehat{S}^{1}
circles and the
\widehat{CY_{2}}
’s are variable;

dashed lines
indicate an equivalence that is due to dimensional/topological reduction;

undashed lines
indicate an equivalence that is not due to any dimensional/topological reduction;

double lines
indicate a correspondence;

bold rectangles
indicate a result that has not been conjectured;
and
regular rectangles
indicate a result that has been conjectured.

Lastly, by reviewing the various gauge-theoretic Floer homologies and FS type
A_{\infty}
-categories obtained hitherto and in
[
15
]
, we observe that higher categorical structures can be introduced by taking an increasing number of spatial directions to be
\mathbb{R}
.
Moreover, we also find that to configurations on a
D
-manifold,
M_{D}
, one can associate a Floer homology of
M_{D}
0-category realized by the partition function of a gauge theory on
M_{D}\times\mathbb{R}
, which, in turn, can be categorified into an FS type
A_{\infty}
-category of
M_{D}
1-category realized by the partition function of a gauge theory on
M_{D}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
.
This scheme of categorification is depicted in
Fig. 2
.
Therein,
dotted lines
are relations representing a categorification;

dash-dotted lines
are relations between categories due to dimensional reduction;
and the
\text{Fuet}^{\text{BPS-eqn}}(M_{D},G)
’s are Fueter type 2-categories of
M_{D}
that categorify the various FS type
A_{\infty}
1-categories.
Such 2-categories are gauge-theoretic generalizations of the Fueter 2-categories recently developed by Bousseau
[
5
]
and Doan-Rezchikov
[
8
]
, and will appear in a sequel paper
[
16
]
.

A Topological 8d
\mathcal{N}=1
Gauge Theory on a Spin
(7)
-manifold

In this section, we will consider a certain “trivially-twisted” topological 8d
\mathcal{N}=1
gauge theory on an eight-manifold with Spin
(7)
holonomy, and gauge group a real, simple, compact Lie group
G
, where the BPS equation that its path integral localizes onto is the Spin
(7)
instanton equation.
We will make use of this theory to obtain our desired results in later sections.

2.1
The Field Composition of the Theory

The topological theory that we will consider in this paper is defined on an eight-manifold with Spin
(7)
holonomy that is equipped with a closed Hodge self-dual structure four-form.
Such manifolds are known in the literature as Spin
(7)
-manifolds (equipped with a closed Spin
(7)
structure)
[
22
, Prop. 10.5.3]
.
The topological theory admits a “trivial twist”, i.e., the twisted theory is equivalent to the original untwisted theory
[
1
,
14
]
.
The bosonic field content of the theory is a gauge connection
A_{\mu}\in\Omega^{1}(\text{Spin}(7),\text{ad}(G))
and complex scalars
\varphi,\bar{\varphi}\in\Omega^{0}(\text{Spin}(7),\text{ad}(G))
.
The fermionic field content of the theory is a scalar
\eta\in\Omega^{0}(\text{Spin}(7),\text{ad}(G))
, a one-form
\psi\in\Omega^{1}(\text{Spin}(7),\text{ad}(G))
, and a self-dual two-form
\chi\in\Omega^{2,+}(\text{Spin}(7),\text{ad}(G))
.
Here,
\text{ad}(G)
is the adjoint bundle of the underlying principal
G
-bundle.

The presence of a scalar fermion field in the theory indicates the existence of a nilpotent scalar supersymmetry generator
\mathcal{Q}
in the theory.
The supersymmetry transformations of the topological theory under
\mathcal{Q}
are
[
1
]

\displaystyle\delta A_{\mu}

\displaystyle=i\psi_{\mu}\,,

(2.1)

\displaystyle\delta\varphi

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\delta\bar{\varphi}

\displaystyle=2i\eta\,,

\displaystyle\delta\eta

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}[\varphi,\bar{\varphi}]\,,

\displaystyle\delta\psi_{\mu}

\displaystyle=-D_{\mu}\varphi\,,

\displaystyle\delta\chi_{\mu\nu}

\displaystyle=F^{+}_{\mu\nu}\,,

where
\mu\in\{0,\dots,7\}
are the indices on the Spin
(7)
-manifold;
F^{+}_{\mu\nu}=\frac{1}{2}(F_{\mu\nu}+\frac{1}{2}\phi_{\mu\nu\rho\pi}F^{\rho\pi})
is the self-dual part of the field strength
F_{\mu\nu}
, with
\phi
being the closed Hodge self-dual
\text{Spin}(7)
-structure; and self-duality of
\Phi\in\{\chi,F^{+}\}
means
\Phi_{\mu\nu}=\frac{1}{6}\phi_{\mu\nu\rho\pi}\Phi^{\rho\pi}
.
Our choice of
\phi
will be that in
[
1
]
, where its non-zero components, denoted by
[\rho\pi\mu\nu]\equiv\phi_{\rho\pi\mu\nu}
, are

\displaystyle[0145]=[0167]=[2345]=[2367]

\displaystyle=[0246]=[1357]=[0123]=[4567]=1\,,

(2.2)

\displaystyle[0257]=[1346]=[0347]

\displaystyle=[0356]=[1247]=[1256]=-1\,.

By introducing auxiliary fields, the off-shell supersymmetry variation
\mathcal{Q}
will be nilpotent up to gauge transformations generated by
\varphi
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\delta^{2}A

\displaystyle\propto D\varphi\,,

(2.3)

\displaystyle\delta^{2}\Psi

\displaystyle\propto[\Psi,\varphi]\,,

where
\Psi
represents all the fields that are not
A
.
As we wish to study the theory where the relevant moduli spaces are well-behaved (i.e., no reducible connections), we shall consider the case where
\varphi
has no zero-modes.

2.2
Spin
(7)
Theory and its BPS Equations

The full
\mathcal{Q}
-exact topological action is
[
1
]

\displaystyle S_{\text{Spin}(7)}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\text{Spin}(7)}\differential[8]{x}\Tr\bigg(
& \frac{1}{2} \left| F^+_{\mu\nu} \right|^2
- \frac{1}{2} D_\mu\varphi D^{\mu} \bar{\varphi}
- \frac{1}{8} [\varphi, \bar{\varphi}]^2
- i \eta D^\mu\psi_\mu+ 2i D_\mu\psi_\nu\chi^{\mu\nu}
\\
& - \frac{i}{2} \varphi\{\eta, \eta\}
- \frac{i}{4} \varphi\left\lbrace\chi_{\mu\nu}, \chi^{\mu\nu} \right\rbrace- \frac{i}{2} \bar{\varphi} \left\lbrace\psi_\mu, \psi^\mu\right\rbrace\bigg)\,.

(2.4)

Setting the variations of the fermions in (
2.1
) to zero, we obtain the BPS equation of the 8d theory as
1
1

1

As we are only considering the case where
\varphi
has no zero-modes, we can take it to be zero in the variations of the fermions.

\displaystyle F^{+}_{\mu\nu}=0\,.

(2.5)

This is an instanton equation on the Spin
(7)
-manifold (commonly known in the literature as the Spin
(7)
instanton equation), which is the 8d analogue of the Donaldson-Witten equation
[
1
]
.
Configurations of
A_{\mu}
satisfying (
2.5
) constitute a moduli space
\mathcal{M}_{\text{Spin}(7)}
that the path integral of the 8d theory localizes onto, where the action in (
2.4
) is minimized.
We shall henceforth refer to this 8d theory with action
S_{\text{Spin}(7)}
as the Spin
(7)
theory, whose moduli space is the moduli space of irreducible Spin
(7)
instantons.

A Floer Homology of Seven-Manifolds

In this section, we shall define, purely physically, a novel Spin
(7)
instanton Floer homology of a closed and compact
G_{2}
-manifold (i.e., a seven-manifold with
G_{2}
holonomy) via the
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology of Spin
(7)
theory, through a supersymmetric quantum mechanics (SQM) interpretation of the 8d gauge theory.
In turn, this would serve as a physical proof of Donaldson-Thomas’ mathematical conjecture
[
10
]
.

3.1
Spin
(7)
Theory as a 1d SQM

We would like to first re-express the 8d
\mathcal{N}=1
gauge theory on
\text{Spin}(7)=M_{7}\times\mathbb{R}
as a 1d SQM model in the space
\mathfrak{A}_{7}
of irreducible gauge connections on
M_{7}
.
To this end, we shall employ the methods pioneered in
[
4
]
and further elucidated in
[
25
]
.

We begin by noting that for
\text{Spin}(7)=M_{7}\times\mathbb{R}
, where
M_{7}
is a closed and compact seven-manifold, it necessarily has to be a
G_{2}
-manifold
[
1
,
6
,
2
,
17
]
.
2
2

2

G_{2}
-manifolds are equipped with a three-form structure
\tilde{\phi}_{t}
that defines the Spin
(7)
-structure of a Spin
(7)
-manifold on
G_{2}\times\mathbb{R}
as
\phi=dt\wedge_{8}\tilde{\phi_{t}}+\star\tilde{\phi}_{t}
, where
t
is the direction along
\mathbb{R}
, “
\wedge_{8}
” is the exterior product on the Spin
(7)
-manifold, and “
\star
” is the Hodge star operator on the
G_{2}
-manifold.

Then, (
2.4
) on
\text{Spin}(7)=G_{2}\times\mathbb{R}

becomes

\displaystyle S_{\text{Spin}(7)}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{G_{2}\times\mathbb{R}}\differential{t}\differential[7]{x}\Tr\bigg(
& 2 \left| F^+_{ti} \right|^2
- \frac{1}{2} D_t \varphi D^t \bar{\varphi}
- \frac{1}{2} D_i \varphi D^i \bar{\varphi}
- \frac{1}{8} [\varphi, \bar{\varphi}]^2
- i \eta D_t \psi_t
- i \eta D^i \psi_i
\\
& + 2i (D_t \psi_i - D_i \psi_t) \chi^{ti}
+ 2i D_i \psi_j \chi^{ij}
- \frac{i}{2} \varphi\{\eta, \eta\}
\\
& - \frac{i}{2} \varphi\{\chi_{ti}, \chi^{ti}\}
- \frac{i}{4} \varphi\{\chi_{ij}, \chi^{ij}\}
- \frac{i}{2} \bar{\varphi} \{\psi_t, \psi_t\}
- \frac{i}{2} \bar{\varphi} \{\psi_i, \psi^i\}
\bigg)\,,

(3.1)

where
t=x^{0}
is the temporal direction along
\mathbb{R}
, and
x^{i}
for
i\in\{1,\dots,7\}
are the remaining directions along the
G_{2}
-manifold.

Expanding out the action in (
3.1
) and collecting the terms without
A_{t}
and
\varphi
, the action becomes
3
3

3

We can ignore the terms with
A_{t}
and
\varphi
, since they will be integrated out to furnish the terms that contribute to the Christoffel connection in the kinetic terms of the fermions as well as the four-fermi curvature term, when recast as a 1d SQM model afterwards
[
15
,
4
]
.

S_{\text{Spin}(7)}=\frac{1}{2e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\differential[]\int_{G_{2}}\differential[7]{x}\Tr\left(\left|\dot{A}_{i}+\frac{1}{2}\phi_{tijk}F^{jk}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,,

(3.2)

where the “
\dots
” contain the fermion terms in the action, and
\dot{A}_{\mu}=\partial_{t}A_{\mu}
.

After suitable rescalings, we can recast (
3.2
) as a 1d SQM model, where its action will now read

S_{\text{SQM},G_{2}\text{-inst}}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\differential{t}\left(\left|\dot{A}^{\alpha}+g^{\alpha\beta}_{\mathfrak{A}_{7}}\partialderivative{V_7}{A^\beta}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,.

(3.3)

Here,
A^{\alpha}
and
(\alpha,\beta)
are coordinates and indices on the space
\mathfrak{A}_{7}
of irreducible
A_{i}
fields on the
G_{2}
-manifold;
4
4

4

Since we will ultimately consider only gauge-inequivalent configurations,
\mathfrak{A}_{7}
is more precisely the space of irreducible
A_{i}
fields on the
G_{2}
-manifold modulo gauge equivalence.
Similar such spaces to appear in later sections should also be understood as spaces of fields modulo gauge equivalence.

g_{\mathfrak{A}_{7}}
is the metric on
\mathfrak{A}_{7}
;
V_{7}(A)
is the potential function; and the ‘
G_{2}
-inst’ label in the subscript will be made clear shortly.

3.2
A Spin
(7)
Instanton Floer Homology of
G_{2}
-manifolds

In a TQFT, the Hamiltonian
H
vanishes in the
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology, whence this means that for any state
|\mathcal{O}\rangle
that is nonvanishing in the
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology, we have

H\ket{\mathcal{O}}=\anticommutator{\mathcal{Q}}{\cdots}\ket{\mathcal{O}}=\mathcal{Q}(\cdots\ket{\mathcal{O}})=\mathcal{Q}\ket{\mathcal{O}'}=\anticommutator{\mathcal{Q}}{\mathcal{O}'}\ket{0}=\ket{\acomm{\mathcal{Q}}{\mathcal{O}'}}\sim 0\,.

(3.4)

In other words, the
|\mathcal{O}\rangle
’s which span the relevant
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology of states in Spin
(7)
theory are actually ground states that are therefore time-invariant.
In particular, for Spin
(7)
theory on
\text{Spin}(7)=G_{2}\times\mathbb{R}
with
\mathbb{R}
as the time coordinate, its relevant spectrum of states is associated only with the
G_{2}
-manifold.

With
\text{Spin}(7)=G_{2}\times\mathbb{R}
, the
G_{2}
-manifold is the far boundary of the Spin
(7)
-manifold and one needs to specify “boundary conditions” on the
G_{2}
-manifold to compute the path integral.
We can do this by first defining a restriction of the fields to the
G_{2}
-manifold, which we shall denote as
\Psi_{G_{2}}
, and then specifying boundary values for these restrictions.
Doing this is equivalent to inserting in the path integral, an operator functional
F_{7}(\Psi_{G_{2}})
that is nonvanishing in the
{\mathcal{Q}}
-cohomology (so that the path integral will continue to be topological).
This means that the corresponding partition function of Spin
(7)
theory can be computed as
[
33
, eqn. (4.12)]
5
5

5

The partition function of Spin
(7)
theory on
G_{2}\times\mathbb{R}
can be defined if it were a balanced TQFT like Vafa-Witten theory.
This requires the virtual dimension of its moduli space to be zero, i.e., the difference between the number of fermion zero-modes of the theory to be zero.
Note that fermion zero-modes are static and are thus time-invariant, i.e., they have no
t
dependence.
Hence, they are actually insensitive to the geometry of the
t
-direction, which is
\mathbb{R}
in our case.
So, if we were to replace
\mathbb{R}
with
S^{1}
, the fermion zero-modes would not be affected; in particular, their numbers, and thus difference, would not change.
On
G_{2}\times S^{1}
, this difference between fermion zero-modes is the index of the elliptic operator associated to the (linearized) Spin
(7)
instanton equation (modulo gauge equivalence) on
G_{2}\times S^{1}

[
23
]
.
Therefore, for an appropriate choice of a
G_{2}
-manifold and
G
such that the index is zero, Spin
(7)
theory on
G_{2}\times\mathbb{R}
is a balanced TQFT and thus, its partition function can be defined.
We shall henceforth assume such a choice of a
G_{2}
-manifold and
G
; specifically, we choose a
G_{2}
-manifold and
G
such that, when evaluated over the fundamental class of
G_{2}\times S^{1}
,
p_{1}(G_{2})\text{ch}_{2}(\text{ad}(G))=24\text{ch}_{4}(\text{ad}(G))
(where
p_{1}(G_{2})
is the first Pontrjagin class of the
G_{2}
-manifold and
\text{ch}_{k}(\text{ad}(G))
is the degree-
2k
component of the Chern character of
\text{ad}(G)
).

\expectationvalue{1}_{F_{7}(\Psi_{G_{2}})}=\int_{\mathcal{M}_{\text{Spin}(7)}}F_{7}(\Psi_{G_{2}})\,e^{-S_{\text{Spin}(7)}}\,.

(3.5)

Since we have demonstrated in the previous subsection that Spin
(7)
theory on
G_{2}\times\mathbb{R}
can be expressed as a 1d SQM model in
\mathfrak{A}_{7}
, we can thus write the partition function as

\mathcal{Z}_{\text{Spin}(7),G_{2}\times\mathbb{R}}(G)=\expectationvalue{1}_{F_{7}(\Psi_{G_{2}})}=\sum_{j}\mathcal{F}^{G}_{\text{Spin}(7)}(\Psi_{G_{2}}^{j})\,.

(3.6)

Here,
\mathcal{F}^{G}_{\text{Spin}(7)}(\Psi_{G_{2}}^{j})
, in the
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology of Spin
(7)
theory, is the
j^{\text{th}}
contribution to the partition function that depends on the expression of
F_{7}(\Psi_{G_{2}})
in the fields on the
G_{2}
-manifold, evaluated over the corresponding solutions to the Spin
(7)
instanton equation in (
2.5
) restricted to
G_{2}
;
and the summation in ‘
j
’ is over all presumably isolated and non-degenerate configurations on the
G_{2}
-manifold in
\mathfrak{A}_{7}
that the equivalent SQM localizes onto.
6
6

6

This presumption that the configurations are isolated and non-degenerate will be justified in
footnote 7
.

Let us now ascertain what the
\mathcal{F}^{G}_{\text{Spin}(7)}(\Psi_{G_{2}}^{j})
’s correspond to.
To this end, we have to first determine the configurations that the SQM localizes onto.
These are configurations that minimize the SQM action (
3.3
), i.e., configurations that set the squared term therein to zero.
They are therefore given by

\boxed{\derivative{A^\alpha}{t}=-g^{\alpha\beta}_{\mathfrak{A}_{7}}\partialderivative{V_7}{A^\beta}}

(3.7)

where the squaring argument
[
33
]
means that both the LHS and RHS are
simultaneously
set to zero.
In other words, the configurations that the SQM localizes onto are fixed (i.e., time-invariant) critical points of the potential
V_{7}
in
\mathfrak{A}_{7}
.

G_{2}
Instanton Configurations as Critical Points of the 1d SQM

To determine the explicit form of
V_{7}
, note that the squared term in (
3.3
) originates from the squared term in (
3.2
).
Indeed, setting the expression within the squared term in (
3.2
) to zero minimizes the underlying 8d action, and this is consistent with setting the expression within the squared term in (
3.3
) to zero to minimize the equivalent SQM action.
Therefore, we can deduce the explicit form of
V_{7}
by comparing (
3.7
) with (
3.2
).
Specifically, setting to zero the expression within the squared term in (
3.2
) would give us

\derivative{A_i}{t}=-\frac{1}{2}\phi_{tijk}F^{jk}\,.

(3.8)

Comparing (
3.8
) with (
3.7
), we find that

\boxed{V_{7}(A,\varphi)=\int_{G_{2}}\,\Tr\,\left(CS(A)\wedge\star\phi_{t}\right)}

(3.9)

Here,
CS(A)
is the Chern-Simons three-form in
A
;
\phi_{t}
is the three-form
G_{2}
-structure of the
G_{2}
-manifold, whose non-zero components
(\phi_{t})_{ijk}
correspond to the choice of the Spin
(7)
-structure
\phi
in (
2.2
) with the first index being ‘
0
’; and “
\wedge
”, “
\star
” are the exterior product and Hodge star operator on the
G_{2}
-manifold, respectively.
Thus, the summation in ‘
j
’ in (
3.6
) is over all isolated and non-degenerate critical points of (
3.9
) in
\mathfrak{A}_{7}
that are also fixed.
7
7

7

As we will explain next, the aforementioned critical points correspond to
G_{2}
instanton configurations on the
G_{2}
-manifold.
For them to be isolated, the actual dimension of their moduli space needs to be zero.
This is indeed possible under appropriate transversality assumptions of the
G_{2}
instantons being acyclic
[
31
,
\lx@sectionsign
3]
.
We shall henceforth choose our
G_{2}
-manifolds to satisfy these transversality assumptions (in addition to the condition spelt out in
footnote 5
).
As for their non-degeneracy, a suitable perturbation of
V_{7}(A)
, which can be effected by introducing physically-trivial
\mathcal{Q}
-exact terms to the action, would ensure this
[
15
, footnote 5]
.
We would like to thank D. Joyce for discussions on this point.

Critical points of
V_{7}(A)
are configurations in
\mathfrak{A}_{7}
that set the RHS of (
3.7
) to zero, which, in turn, correspond to configurations on the
G_{2}
-manifold that set the RHS of (
3.8
) to zero.
Such configurations span the space of solutions to the 7d
G_{2}
instanton equation, for which we shall henceforth refer to as
G_{2}
instanton configurations.

In summary, the partition function (
3.6
) is an algebraic sum of
fixed

G_{2}
instanton configurations on the
G_{2}
-manifold in
\mathfrak{A}_{7}
.

The Spin
(7)
Instanton Floer Homology

Notice that (
3.7
) is a gradient flow equation and it governs the classical trajectory of the 1d SQM model from one time-invariant
G_{2}
instanton configuration to another on the
G_{2}
-manifold, in
\mathfrak{A_{7}}
.
Hence, just as in
[
15
,
4
,
25
]
, the equivalent 1d SQM model will physically realize a gauge-theoretic Floer homology.

Specifically, the
time-invariant
G_{2}
instanton configurations on the
G_{2}
-manifold in
\mathfrak{A}_{7}
, i.e., time-independent solutions to the 7d equation

\boxed{F\wedge\star\phi_{t}=0}

(3.10)

will generate the chains of a Floer complex with
Morse functional

V_{7}(A)
in (
3.9
).
The Spin
(7)
instanton flow lines, described by time-varying solutions to the
gradient flow equation
(
3.7
), are the Floer differentials such that the number of outgoing flow lines at each time-invariant configuration obeying (
3.10
) is the degree
d_{j}
of the corresponding chain in the Floer complex.

In other words, we can also write (
3.6
) as

\boxed{\mathcal{Z}_{\text{Spin}(7),G_{2}\times\mathbb{R}}(G)=\sum_{j}\mathcal{F}^{G}_{\text{Spin}(7)}(\Psi_{G_{2}}^{j})=\sum_{j}\text{HF}^{\text{Spin}(7)\text{-inst}}_{d_{j}}(G_{2},G)=\mathcal{Z}^{\text{Floer}}_{\text{Spin}(7)\text{-inst},G_{2}}(G)}

(3.11)

where each
\mathcal{F}^{G}_{\text{Spin}(7)}(\Psi_{G_{2}}^{j})
can be identified with a
novel
Floer homology class
\text{HF}^{\text{Spin}(7)\text{-inst}}_{d_{j}}(G_{2},G)
, that we shall henceforth name a Spin
(7)
instanton Floer homology class, assigned to the
G_{2}
-manifold defined by (
3.7
), (
3.9
), (
3.10
), and the description above.

A Physical Proof of Donaldson-Thomas’ Mathematical Conjecture

Note that
\text{HF}^{\text{Spin}(7)\text{-inst}}_{d_{j}}(G_{2},G)
was first mathematically conjectured to exist by Donaldson-Thomas
[
10
,
\lx@sectionsign
3]
as a Floer homology generated by
G_{2}
instantons on a
G_{2}
-manifold, whose flow lines are time-varying solutions to the Spin
(7)
instanton equation on
G_{2}\times\mathbb{R}
.
We have therefore furnished a physical proof of their mathematical conjecture.

A Holomorphic Floer Homology of Six-Manifolds

In this section, we will specialize to
G_{2}=M_{6}\times S^{1}
, where
M_{6}
is a closed and compact Calabi-Yau threefold (
CY_{3}
), and perform a Kaluza-Klein (KK) dimensional reduction of Spin
(7)
theory by shrinking the
S^{1}
circle to be infinitesimally small.
This will allow us to physically derive, from its topologically-invariant
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology, a holomorphic
G_{2}
monopole Floer homology of
CY_{3}
. In turn, this would serve as a physical proof of Donaldson-Segal’s mathematical conjecture
[
12
]
and Cherkis’ speculation
[
6
]
, and a generalization of the former.

4.1
A KK Reduction of Spin
(7)
Theory along
S^{1}
and the Corresponding SQM

For
\text{Spin}(7)=M_{6}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
to be a Spin
(7)
-manifold,
M_{6}\times S^{1}
has to be a closed and compact
G_{2}
-manifold.
This is possible if
M_{6}
were to be a closed and compact Calabi-Yau threefold (
CY_{3}
)
[
32
]
.
8
8

8

CY_{3}
’s are equipped with a Kähler two-form
\omega
and holomorphic three-form
\Lambda
that defines a
G_{2}
three-form structure
\tilde{\phi}_{t}
of a
G_{2}
-manifold on
CY_{3}\times S^{1}
as
\tilde{\phi}_{t}=ds\wedge\omega+\text{Re}(\Lambda)
, where
s
is the direction along
S^{1}
and “
\wedge
” is the exterior product on the
G_{2}
-manifold.

We will consider this case, and start with Spin
(7)
theory on
\text{Spin}(7)=CY_{3}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
.

Let
x^{1}
be the coordinate along the
S^{1}
circle.
Upon KK reduction along this
S^{1}
circle, the component of the gauge field along it, i.e.,
A_{1}
, will be interpreted as a scalar field on
CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
.
The
\mathcal{Q}
-variation of the bosons are then

\delta A_{t}=i\psi_{t}\,,\qquad\delta C=i\varrho\,,\qquad\delta A_{a}=i\psi_{a}\,,

(4.1)

where
x^{a}
for
a\in\{2,\dots,7\}
are coordinates on
CY_{3}
, and
(A_{1},\psi_{1})
have been relabeled as
(C,\varrho)\in\Omega^{0}(CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R},\text{ad}(G))
for later convenience.

The BPS Equations of 7d-Spin
(7)
Theory

The conditions that minimize Spin
(7)
theory when we KK reduce along
S^{1}
are effectively obtained by KK reduction of the conditions that minimize the action (
3.2
)
[
15
,
\lx@sectionsign
4]
, i.e., by the KK reduction of the BPS equation of Spin
(7)
theory.
They are given by

\dot{C}+\frac{1}{2}\phi_{t1ab}F^{ab}=0\,,\qquad\dot{A}_{a}+\phi_{t1ab}D^{b}C+\frac{1}{2}\phi_{tabc}F^{bc}=0\,.

(4.2)

These are the BPS equations of the theory that results from the KK reduction of Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{3}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
along
S^{1}
.
Notice also that these equations can be re-expressed as a monopole equation on
G_{2}=CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
(known in the literature as a
G_{2}
monopole (
G_{2}
-M) equation
[
7
,
17
]
, or as an octonionic monopole equation
[
6
]
) in temporal gauge, with time being along the
\mathbb{R}
direction.

Let use define complex coordinates
(z^{m},{\bar{z}}^{\bar{m}})
for
CY_{3}
as
z^{1}=x^{2}+ix^{3}
,
z^{2}=x^{4}+ix^{5}
, and
z^{3}=x^{6}+ix^{7}
, where their complex conjugates are
\bar{z}^{\bar{1}}
,
\bar{z}^{\bar{2}}
, and
\bar{z}^{\bar{3}}
.
This will allow us to define
\mathcal{A}\in\Omega^{(1,0)}(CY_{3},\text{ad}(G))
and
\bar{\mathcal{A}}\in\Omega^{(0,1)}(CY_{3},\text{ad}(G))
, a holomorphic and anti-holomorphic gauge connection, respectively, on
CY_{3}
.
The components of
\mathcal{A}
work out to be
\mathcal{A}_{1}=(A_{2}-iA_{3})/2
,
\mathcal{A}_{2}=(A_{4}-iA_{5})/2
, and
\mathcal{A}_{3}=(A_{6}-iA_{7})/2
, where their complex conjugates are
\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{1}}
,
\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{2}}
, and
\bar{\mathcal{A}}_{\bar{3}}
.
With this, (
4.2
) can be equivalently expressed as
9
9

9

We only need to consider the holomorphic expressions, as the anti-holomorphic expressions are obtained through complex conjugations.

\dot{C}=-\omega_{m\bar{n}}\mathcal{F}^{m\bar{n}}\,,\qquad\dot{\mathcal{A}}_{m}=-\omega_{m\bar{n}}\bar{\mathcal{D}}^{\bar{n}}C-\frac{1}{4}\varepsilon_{mpq}\mathcal{F}^{pq}\,,

(4.3)

where
(\omega_{m\bar{n}},\varepsilon_{mpq})
are components of the Kähler two-form and holomorphic three-form of
CY_{3}
, respectively;
\mathcal{F}^{pq}=\partial^{p}\mathcal{A}^{q}-\partial^{q}\mathcal{A}^{p}+[\mathcal{A}^{p},\mathcal{A}^{q}]
are the components of the
(2,0)
-form field strength in
\mathcal{A}
;
\mathcal{F}^{m\bar{n}}=\partial^{m}\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{\bar{n}}-\bar{\partial}^{\bar{m}}\mathcal{A}^{n}+[\mathcal{A}^{m},\bar{\mathcal{A}}^{\bar{n}}]
are the components of the
(1,1)
-form field strength in
\mathcal{A}
and
\bar{\mathcal{A}}
; and
\bar{\mathcal{D}}=\bar{\partial}+[\bar{\mathcal{A}},\cdot]
is the anti-holomorphic covariant derivative.

In other words, Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{3}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
, upon a KK reduction along
S^{1}
to a 7d theory (henceforth referred to as 7d-Spin
(7)
theory) on
CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
, localizes onto configurations that obey (
4.3
).

7d-Spin
(7)
Theory as a 1d SQM in
\mathfrak{A}_{6}

The 7d action can thus be written as

\displaystyle S_{\text{7d-Spin}(7)}=\frac{1}{2e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\differential{t}\int_{CY_{3}}\absolutevalue{\dd{z}}^{6}\Tr\Bigg(
& 4 \left| \dot{\mathcal{A}}_m
+ \omega_{m\bar{n}} \bar{\mathcal{D}}^{\bar{n}} C
+ \frac{1}{4} \varepsilon_{mpq} \mathcal{F}^{pq}
\right|^2
+ \left| \dot{C}
+ \omega_{m\bar{n}} \mathcal{F}^{m\bar{n}}
\right|^2
+ \dots\Bigg)\,.

(4.4)

After suitable rescalings, the equivalent SQM action can be obtained from (
4.4
) as

S_{\text{SQM,DT}}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\differential{t}\left(\left|\dot{\mathcal{A}}^{\alpha}+g^{\alpha\bar{\beta}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{6}}\left(\partialderivative{V_6}{\mathcal{A}^\beta}\right)^{*}\right|^{2}+\left|\dot{C}^{\alpha}+g^{\alpha\bar{\beta}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{6}}\left(\partialderivative{V_6}{C^\beta}\right)^{*}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,,

(4.5)

where
(\mathcal{A}^{\alpha},C^{\alpha})
and
(\alpha,\beta)
are holomorphic coordinates and indices on the space
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
of irreducible
(\mathcal{A}_{m},C)
fields on
CY_{3}
;
g_{\mathfrak{A}_{6}}
is the metric on
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
;
V_{6}(\mathcal{A},C)
is the holomorphic potential function; and the ‘DT’ label in the subscript will be made clear very shortly.

Localizing Onto Time-invariant Donaldson-Thomas Configurations on
CY_{3}

By the squaring argument
[
4
]
applied to (
4.5
), the configurations that the equivalent SQM localizes onto are those that set the LHS’s and RHS’s of (
4.3
)
simultaneously
to zero.
10
10

10

On our choice of a closed and compact
CY_{3}
, setting to zero the RHS of the second equation of (
4.3
) and applying the Bianchi identity, we find that both terms within are independently zero, i.e.,
\mathcal{D}_{m}C=0=\frac{1}{4}\varepsilon_{mpq}\mathcal{F}^{pq}

[
17
, Lemma 53]
.

Such configurations in
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
correspond to time-invariant configurations that span the space of solutions to the 6d Donaldson-Thomas (DT) equations on
CY_{3}

[
27
]
with the scalar being real.
11
11

11

6d DT equations on
CY_{3}
contain a holomorphic gauge connection and a
complex
scalar
[
27
,
7
]
.
However, what we get by setting to zero the RHS’s of (
4.3
) are equations involving a holomorphic gauge connection and a
real
scalar (corresponding to the real gauge field
A_{1}
from the KK reduction).
Solutions to such DT equations on
CY_{3}
where the complex scalar is actually real are known in the literature as Calabi-Yau monopoles on
CY_{3}

[
17
]
.

We shall, in the rest of this section, refer to such configurations as DT configurations on
CY_{3}
.

In summary, the equivalent SQM localizes onto time-invariant DT configurations on
CY_{3}
in
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
.

4.2
A Holomorphic
G_{2}
Monopole Floer Homology of
CY_{3}

Since the resulting 7d theory on
CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
can be interpreted as a 1d SQM in
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
, its partition function can, like in (
3.6
), be written as

\mathcal{Z}_{\text{Spin}(7),CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}}(G)=\expectationvalue{1}_{F_{6}(\Psi_{CY_{3}})}=\sum_{k}\mathcal{F}^{G}_{\text{7d-Spin}(7)}(\Psi_{CY_{3}}^{k})\,,

(4.6)

where
\mathcal{F}^{G}_{\text{7d-Spin}(7)}(\Psi_{CY_{3}}^{k})
, in the
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology of 7d-Spin
(7)
theory, is the
k^{\text{th}}
contribution to the partition function that depends on the expression of
F_{6}(\Psi_{CY_{3}})
in the bosonic fields on
CY_{3}
, and the summation in ‘
k
’ is over all isolated and non-degenerate DT configurations on
CY_{3}
in
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
that the equivalent SQM localizes onto.
12
12

12

This presumption that the configurations will be isolated and non-degenerate is justified because the (
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology of) Spin
(7)
theory is topological in all directions and therefore invariant when we shrink the
S^{1}
.
Thus, if
CY_{3}
(where
CY_{3}\times S^{1}=G_{2}
) is chosen such as to satisfy the transversality assumptions of
footnote 7
,
\mathcal{Z}_{\text{Spin}(7),CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}}
will be a discrete and non-degenerate sum of contributions, just like
\mathcal{Z}_{\text{Spin}(7),G_{2}\times\mathbb{R}}
.
We shall henceforth assume such a choice of
CY_{3}
whence the presumption would hold.

Let us now ascertain what the
\mathcal{F}^{G}_{\text{7d-Spin}(7)}(\Psi_{CY_{3}}^{k})
’s correspond to.
Repeating here the analysis in

§

3.2
with (
4.5
) as the action for the equivalent SQM model, we find that we can also write (
4.6
) as

\boxed{\mathcal{Z}_{\text{Spin}(7),CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}}(G)=\sum_{k}\mathcal{F}^{G}_{\text{7d-Spin}(7)}(\Psi_{CY_{3}}^{k})=\sum_{k}\text{HHF}^{G_{2}\text{-M}}_{d_{k}}(CY_{3},G)=\mathcal{Z}^{\text{Floer}}_{G_{2}\text{-M},CY_{3}}(C)}

(4.7)

where each
\mathcal{F}^{G}_{\text{7d-Spin}(7)}(\Psi_{CY_{3}}^{k})
can be identified with a
novel
gauge-theoretic
holomorphic
Floer homology class
\text{HHF}^{G_{2}\text{-M}}_{d_{k}}(CY_{3},G)
, that we shall henceforth name a holomorphic
G_{2}
monopole Floer homology class, of degree
d_{k}
, assigned to
CY_{3}
.

Specifically, the
time-invariant DT configurations on
CY_{3}
in
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
that obey the simultaneous vanishing of the LHS and RHS of the holomorphic
gradient flow equations

\boxed{\derivative{\mathcal{A}^\alpha}{t}=-g^{\alpha\bar{\beta}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{6}}\left(\partialderivative{V_6}{\mathcal{A}^\beta}\right)^{*}\qquad\derivative{C^\alpha}{t}=-g^{\alpha\bar{\beta}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{6}}\left(\partialderivative{V_6}{C^\beta}\right)^{*}}

(4.8)

will generate the chains of the holomorphic
G_{2}
monopole Floer complex with
holomorphic Morse functional

\boxed{V_{6}(\mathcal{A},C)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{CY_{3}}\Tr\left(CS(\mathcal{A})\wedge\bar{\star}\Lambda+2C\wedge\mathcal{F}^{(1,1)}\wedge\bar{\star}\omega\right)}

(4.9)

in
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
, where
CS(\mathcal{A})
is the Chern-Simons three-form in
\mathcal{A}
and
\Lambda
is a holomorphic
(3,0)
-form with components
\Lambda_{mpq}=\varepsilon_{mpq}
.
The
G_{2}
monopole flow lines, described by time-varying solutions to (
4.8
), are the Floer differentials such that the degree
d_{k}
of the corresponding chain in the holomorphic
G_{2}
monopole Floer complex is counted by the outgoing flow lines at each time-invariant DT configuration on
CY_{3}
in
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
.
Such a configuration corresponds to a time-independent solution to the 6d equations

\boxed{\omega\wedge\bar{\star}\mathcal{F}^{(1,1)}=0\qquad\mathcal{F}^{(2,0)}=0\qquad\mathcal{D}_{m}C=0}

(4.10)

A Physical Proof of Donaldson-Segal’s Mathematical Conjecture and Cherkis’ Speculation, and a Generalization of the Former

Note that if we were to restrict to the case where
C=0
, we would instead physically realize
\text{HHF}^{G_{2}\text{-inst}}_{d_{k}}(CY_{3},G)
, a holomorphic
G_{2}

instanton
Floer homology of
CY_{3}
generated by DT configurations on
CY_{3}
with
C=0
,
13
13

13

On a closed and compact
CY_{3}
, DT configurations on
CY_{3}
with
C=0
are also known as Donaldson-Uhlenbeck-Yau (DUY) configurations
[
2
]
, Hermitian-Yang-Mills connections
[
32
,
29
,
9
]
, or holomorphic vector bundles on
CY_{3}

[
11
,
29
,
9
]
.

whose flow lines correspond to time-varying solutions to the
G_{2}
instanton equation, i.e., (
4.3
) with
C=0
.
Such a Floer homology was first predicted to exist in the mathematical literature by Donaldson-Thomas
[
10
]
, and later conjectured more concretely by Donaldson-Segal
[
12
,
\lx@sectionsign
4]
as a Floer homology generated by holomorphic vector bundles on
CY_{3}
(described in
footnote 13
), whose flow lines correspond to time-varying solutions to the
G_{2}
instanton equation on
CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
.
We have therefore furnished a physical proof and generalization (when
C\neq 0
) of Donaldson-Segal’s mathematical conjecture.

On the other hand, in the physical literature, Cherkis
[
6
,
\lx@sectionsign
7]
speculated that octonionic monopoles (i.e.,
G_{2}
monopoles) on
CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
would allow one to physically realize a
real
Floer homology of
CY_{3}
generated by Calabi-Yau monopoles (described in
footnote 11
), whose
real
Morse functional is the Chern-Simons-Higgs functional, and flow lines are time-varying solutions to the octonionic monopole equation.
Although his results were for a
real
Floer homology generated by
real
configurations on
CY_{3}
and a
real
Morse functional, it is straightforward to verify that through a complexification of
CY_{3}
in his case, our results are produced.
We have therefore furnished a physical proof of Cherkis’ speculation.

A Holomorphic Floer Homology of Five-Manifolds

In this section, we will further specialize to
CY_{3}=M_{4}\times S^{1}\times S^{1}
, where
M_{4}
is a closed and compact Calabi-Yau twofold (
CY_{2}
), and perform another KK dimensional reduction of Spin
(7)
theory by shrinking one of the
S^{1}
circles to be infinitesimally small.
This will allow us to physically derive, from its topologically-invariant
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology, a novel holomorphic DT Floer homology of
CY_{2}\times S^{1}
.

5.1
A KK Reduction of Spin
(7)
Theory along
S^{1}\times S^{1}
and the Corresponding SQM

For
\text{Spin}(7)=M_{5}\times S^{1}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
to be a Spin
(7)
-manifold, the closed and compact
M_{5}\times S^{1}
can be
CY_{3}
.
This is possible if
M_{5}=CY_{2}\times S^{1}
, where
CY_{2}
is a closed and compact Calabi-Yau twofold
[
17
]
.
We will consider this case, and start with 7d-Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{2}\times S^{1}_{\mathfrak{b}}\times S^{1}_{\mathfrak{a}}\times\mathbb{R}
.
Taking
x^{2}
and
x^{3}
to be the coordinates along
S^{1}_{\mathfrak{a}}
and
S^{1}_{\mathfrak{b}}
, respectively, a further KK reduction of 7d-Spin
(7)
theory along
S^{1}_{\mathfrak{a}}
amounts to setting
\partial_{2}\rightarrow 0
, and relabelling
A_{2}=\tilde{C}\in\Omega^{0}(S^{1}_{\mathfrak{b}},\text{ad}(G))\otimes\Omega^{0}(CY_{2},\text{ad}(G))
, and
A_{3}=\Gamma\in\Omega^{1}(S^{1}_{\mathfrak{b}},\text{ad}(G))\otimes\Omega^{0}(CY_{2},\text{ad}(G))
.
14
14

14

Topological invariance of 7d-Spin
(7)
theory under yet another KK reduction along
S^{1}_{\mathfrak{a}}
implies that all the fields of the theory resulting from the KK reduction, with the exception of
\Gamma
and its fermionic supersymmetric partner, can be interpreted as scalars along
S^{1}_{\mathfrak{b}}
.
On the other hand, this also means that
\Gamma
can be interpreted as a scalar on
CY_{2}
.

Note that we can assign the scalars
(C,\tilde{C})
to the linearly-independent components of
B\in\Omega^{0}(S^{1}_{\mathfrak{b}},\text{ad}(G))\otimes\Omega^{2,+}(CY_{2},\text{ad}(G))
as
B_{45}=-\tilde{C}
,
B_{46}=C
, and
B_{47}=0
.

Using a new set of complex coordinates
(\hat{z}^{p},\hat{\bar{z}}^{\bar{p}})
for
CY_{2}
defined as
\hat{z}^{1}=x^{4}+ix^{7}
,
\hat{z}^{2}=x^{5}+ix^{6}
, where their complex conjugates are
\hat{\bar{z}}^{\bar{1}}
and
\hat{\bar{z}}^{\bar{2}}
, we are able to complexify the fields above.
In particular, we can define
\hat{\mathcal{A}}\in\Omega^{0}(S^{1}_{b},\text{ad}(G))\otimes\Omega^{(1,0)}(CY_{2},\text{ad}(G))
, where its components are
\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{1}=(A_{4}-iA_{7})/2
, and
\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{2}=(A_{5}-iA_{6})/2
.
We can also define
\hat{\mathcal{B}}\in\Omega^{0}(S^{1}_{b},\text{ad}(G))\otimes\Omega^{2,+}(CY_{2},\text{ad}(G))
, a real scalar (
real
self-dual two-form) on
S^{1}
(
CY_{2}
), whose three linearly-independent components are
\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{12}=(\tilde{C}+iC)/2
,
\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{\bar{1}\bar{2}}=(\tilde{C}-iC)/2
, and
\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{1\bar{1}}=0
.
15
15

15

Note that
\hat{\mathcal{B}}
can equally be interpreted as a one-form on an auxiliary
\mathbb{R}^{3}
as
\mathscr{B}=\mathscr{B}_{i}d\mathscr{X}^{i}
, where each of the three components of
\mathscr{B}
corresponds to each of the three linearly-independent components of
\hat{\mathcal{B}}
.
We will be making use of this correspondence between
\hat{\mathcal{B}}
and
\mathscr{B}
shortly.

Thus, upon KK reduction along
S^{1}_{\mathfrak{a}}
, (
4.2
) becomes

\displaystyle\dot{\hat{\mathcal{A}}}_{p}

\displaystyle=-i\left(\partial_{y}\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{p}+\hat{\mathcal{D}}^{q}\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{qp}-\hat{\mathcal{D}}_{p}\Gamma\right)\,,

(5.1)

\displaystyle\dot{\hat{\mathcal{B}}}_{pq}

\displaystyle=i\left(\partial_{y}\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{pq}-2\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{pq}-[\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{pq},\Gamma]\right)\,,

\displaystyle\dot{\Gamma}

\displaystyle=\hat{\omega}^{p\bar{q}}\left(\hat{\mathcal{F}}_{p\bar{q}}-\frac{1}{4}\left(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\times\hat{\mathcal{B}}\right)_{p\bar{q}}\right)\,,

where
\hat{\omega}^{p\bar{q}}
is the Kähler two-form of
CY_{2}
;
x^{3}
is relabeled as
y
; and
\hat{\mathcal{B}}\times\hat{\mathcal{B}}
is a shorthand defined as
\hat{\mathcal{B}}\times\hat{\mathcal{B}}\coloneq 4\star_{3}(\mathscr{B}\wedge_{3}\mathscr{B})=2\varepsilon_{ijk}[\mathscr{B}^{i},\mathscr{B}^{j}]d\mathscr{X}^{k}
, with “
\wedge_{3}
” and “
\star_{3}
” being the exterior product and Hodge star operators, respectively, in the auxiliary
\mathbb{R}^{3}
of
\mathscr{B}
fields introduced in
footnote 15
.
16
16

16

The only non-zero component of
\hat{\mathcal{B}}\times\hat{\mathcal{B}}
(since
\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{1\bar{1}}=0
) is
(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\times\hat{\mathcal{B}})_{1\bar{1}}\propto[\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{12},\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{\bar{1}\bar{2}}]
.

These are the BPS equations of the theory that results from the KK reduction along the
S^{1}_{\mathfrak{a}}
circle.
Notice also that these equations can be expressed as DT equations on
CY_{3}=CY_{2}\times S^{1}_{\mathfrak{b}}\times\mathbb{R}
in temporal gauge, with time being along the
\mathbb{R}
direction.

In other words, 7d-Spin
(7)
theory, upon a second KK reduction along
S^{1}_{\mathfrak{a}}
to a 6d theory (henceforth referred to as 6d-Spin
(7)
theory) on
CY_{2}\times S^{1}_{\mathfrak{b}}\times\mathbb{R}
, localizes onto configurations that obey (
5.1
).

6d-Spin
(7)
Theory as a 1d SQM in
\mathfrak{A}_{5}

Just as was done in the previous sections, this means that the 6d action can be written as
17
17

17

The expressions and results that follow will remain the same if we had chosen to reduce along
S^{1}_{\mathfrak{b}}
instead of
S^{1}_{\mathfrak{a}}
, up to a redefinition of the components of the fields.
Thus, the subscripts of the
S^{1}_{*}
circles will henceforth be omitted.

\displaystyle S_{\text{6d-Spin}(7)}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\differential{t}\int_{CY_{2}\times S^{1}}\absolutevalue{\dd{\hat{z}}}^{4}\differential{y}\Tr\Bigg(
4 \left|
\dot{\hat{\mathcal{A}}}_p
+ i \left( \partial_y \hat{\mathcal{A}}_p
+ \hat{\mathcal{D}}^{q} \hat{\mathcal{B}}_{qp}
- \hat{\mathcal{D}}_p \Gamma\right)
\right|^2
\\
& + 4 \left|
\dot{\hat{\mathcal{B}}}_{pq}
- i \left( \partial_y \hat{\mathcal{B}}_{pq}
- 2 \hat{\mathcal{F}}_{pq}
- [\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{pq}, \Gamma]
\right)
\right|^2
+ \left|
\dot{\Gamma}
- \hat{\omega}^{p\bar{q}} \left( \hat{\mathcal{F}}_{p\bar{q}}
- \frac{1}{4} \left( \hat{\mathcal{B}} \times\hat{\mathcal{B}} \right)_{p\bar{q}}
\right)
\right|^2
+ \dots\Bigg)\,.

(5.2)

After suitable rescalings, the equivalent SQM action can be obtained from (
5.2
) as

S_{\text{SQM,HW}}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\differential{t}\left(\left|\dot{\hat{\mathcal{A}}}^{\alpha}+g^{\alpha\bar{\beta}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{5}}\left(\partialderivative{V_5}{\hat{\mathcal{A}}^\beta}\right)^{*}\right|^{2}+\left|\dot{\hat{\mathcal{B}}}^{\alpha}+g^{\alpha\bar{\beta}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{5}}\left(\partialderivative{V_5}{\hat{\mathcal{B}}^\beta}\right)^{*}\right|^{2}+\left|\dot{\Gamma}^{\alpha}+g^{\alpha\bar{\beta}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{5}}\left(\partialderivative{V_5}{\Gamma^\beta}\right)^{*}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,,

(5.3)

where
(\hat{\mathcal{A}}^{\alpha},\hat{\mathcal{B}}^{\alpha},\Gamma^{\alpha})
and
(\alpha,\beta)
are holomorphic coordinates and indices on the space
\mathfrak{A}_{5}
of irreducible
(\hat{\mathcal{A}}_{p},\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{pq},\Gamma)
fields on
CY_{2}\times S^{1}
;
g_{\mathfrak{A}_{5}}
is the metric on
\mathfrak{A}_{5}
;
V_{5}(\hat{\mathcal{A}},\hat{\mathcal{B}},\Gamma)
is the holomorphic potential function; and the ‘HW’ label in the subscript will be made clear very shortly.

Localizing Onto Time-invariant Haydys-Witten Configurations on
CY_{2}\times S^{1}

Applying the squaring argument to (
5.3
), the configurations of the equivalent SQM localizes onto are those that set the LHS and RHS of (
5.1
)
simultaneously
to zero.
In the real coordinates of
CY_{2}\times S^{1}
, such configurations in
\mathfrak{A}_{5}
are time-invariant configurations that span the space of solutions to
18
18

18

Recall that
\Gamma
is actually the gauge connection along the
y
-direction, i.e.,
A_{y}
, from its definition at the start of this section.

F_{ya}+D^{b}B_{ba}=0\,,\qquad F_{ab}^{+}-\frac{1}{2}D_{y}B_{ab}-\frac{1}{4}[B_{ac},B_{bd}]g^{cd}=0\,,

(5.4)

where
(a,b)
are indices on
CY_{2}
, with one of the three linearly-independent components of the self-dual two-form field
B
being zero, and
[B_{ac},B_{bd}]g^{cd}\eqcolon(B\times B)_{ab}
.
These are the 5d Haydys-Witten (HW) equations on
CY_{2}\times S^{1}

[
20
,
34
,
15
]
(where the Reeb vector is along the
S^{1}
direction).
We shall, in the rest of this section, refer to such configurations as HW configurations on
CY_{2}\times S^{1}
.

In summary, the equivalent SQM localizes onto time-invariant HW configurations on
CY_{2}\times S^{1}
in
\mathfrak{A}_{5}
.

5.2
A Holomorphic Donaldson-Thomas Floer Homology of
CY_{2}\times S^{1}

Since the resulting 6d theory on
CY_{2}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
can be interpreted as a 1d SQM in
\mathfrak{A}_{5}
, its partition function can, like in (
3.6
), be written as

\mathcal{Z}_{\text{Spin}(7),CY_{2}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}}(G)=\expectationvalue{1}_{F_{5}(\Psi_{CY_{2}\times S^{1}})}=\sum_{l}\mathcal{F}^{G}_{\text{6d-Spin}(7)}(\Psi_{CY_{2}\times S^{1}}^{l})\,,

(5.5)

where
\mathcal{F}^{G}_{\text{6d-Spin}(7)}(\Psi_{CY_{2}\times S^{1}}^{l})
, in the
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology of 6d-Spin
(7)
theory, is the
l^{\text{th}}
contribution to the partition function that depends on the expression of
F_{5}(\Psi_{CY_{2}\times S^{1}})
in the bosonic fields of
CY_{2}\times S^{1}
, and the summation in ‘
l
’ is over all isolated and non-degenerate HW configurations on
CY_{2}\times S^{1}
in
\mathfrak{A}_{5}
that the equivalent SQM localizes onto.
19
19

19

This presumption that the configurations will be isolated and non-degenerate is justified because (the
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology of) Spin
(7)
theory is topological in all directions and therefore invariant when we further shrink an
S^{1}
circle from 7d-Spin
(7)
theory.
Thus, if
CY_{2}
(where
CY_{2}\times T^{3}=G_{2}
) is chosen such as to satisfy the transversality assumptions of
footnote 7
,
\mathcal{Z}_{\text{Spin}(7),CY_{2}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}}
will be a discrete and non-degenerate sum of contributions, just like
\mathcal{Z}_{\text{Spin}(7),G_{2}\times\mathbb{R}}
.
We shall henceforth assume such a choice of
CY_{2}
whence the presumption would hold.

Let us now ascertain what the
\mathcal{F}^{G}_{\text{6d-Spin}(7)}(\Psi_{CY_{2}\times S^{1}}^{l})
’s correspond to.
Repeating here again the analysis in

§

3.2
with (
5.3
) as the action for the equivalent SQM model, we find that we can also write (
5.5
) as
