\boxed{\mathcal{Z}_{\text{Spin}(7),CY_{2}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}}(G)=\sum_{l}\mathcal{F}^{G}_{\text{6d-Spin}(7)}(\Psi_{CY_{2}\times S^{1}}^{l})=\sum_{l}\text{HHF}^{\text{DT}}_{d_{l}}(CY_{2}\times S^{1},G)=\mathcal{Z}^{\text{Floer}}_{\text{DT},CY_{2}\times S^{1}}(G)}

(5.6)

where each
\mathcal{F}^{G}_{\text{6d-Spin}(7)}(\Psi_{CY_{2}\times S^{1}}^{l})
can be identified with a
novel
gauge-theoretic
holomorphic
Floer homology class
\text{HHF}^{\text{DT}}_{d_{l}}(CY_{2}\times S^{1},G)
, that we shall henceforth name a holomorphic DT Floer homology class, of degree
d_{l}
, assigned to
CY_{2}\times S^{1}
.

Specifically, the
time-invariant HW configurations on
CY_{2}\times S^{1}
in
\mathfrak{A}_{5}
that obey the simultaneous vanishing of the LHS and RHS of the holomorphic
gradient flow equations

\boxed{\derivative{\hat{\mathcal{A}}^\alpha}{t}=-g^{\alpha\bar{\beta}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{5}}\left(\partialderivative{V_5}{\hat{\mathcal{A}}^\beta}\right)^{*}\qquad\derivative{\hat{\mathcal{B}}^\alpha}{t}=-g^{\alpha\bar{\beta}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{5}}\left(\partialderivative{V_5}{\hat{\mathcal{B}}^\beta}\right)^{*}\qquad\derivative{\Gamma^\alpha}{t}=-g^{\alpha\bar{\beta}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{5}}\left(\partialderivative{V_5}{\Gamma^\beta}\right)^{*}}

(5.7)

will generate the chains of the holomorphic DT Floer complex with holomorphic

Morse functional

\boxed{V_{5}(\hat{\mathcal{A}},\hat{\mathcal{B}},\Gamma)=\int\frac{i}{2}d_{y}\left(\hat{\mathcal{A}}\wedge\bar{\star}\hat{\mathcal{A}}+\hat{\mathcal{B}}\wedge\bar{\star}\hat{\mathcal{B}}\right)-2i\hat{\mathcal{B}}\wedge\bar{\star}\hat{\mathcal{F}}-\Gamma\wedge\hat{\omega}\wedge\bar{\star}\left(\hat{\mathcal{F}}-\frac{1}{4}(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\times\hat{\mathcal{B}})\right)}

(5.8)

in
\mathfrak{A}_{5}
, where “
\wedge
” is the exterior product in
CY_{2}\times S^{1}
, and “
\star
” is the Hodge star operator in the complex
CY_{2}
.
The holomorphic DT flow lines, described by time-varying solutions to (
5.7
), are the Floer differentials such that the degree
d_{l}
of the corresponding chain in the holomorphic DT Floer complex is counted by the outgoing flow lines at each time-invariant HW configuration on
CY_{2}\times S^{1}
in
\mathfrak{A}_{5}
.
Such a configuration corresponds to a time-independent solution to the 5d equations

\boxed{\begin{aligned} d_{y}\hat{\mathcal{A}}+\bar{\star}(\hat{\mathcal{D}}\bar{\star}\hat{\mathcal{B}})&=\hat{\mathcal{D}}\Gamma\\
d_{y}\hat{\mathcal{B}}+\frac{1}{2}(\hat{\mathcal{B}}\times\hat{\mathcal{B}})-2\hat{\mathcal{F}}&=[\hat{\mathcal{B}},\Gamma]\end{aligned}}

(5.9)

with a vanishing component of
\hat{\mathcal{B}}
, i.e.,
\hat{\mathcal{B}}_{p\bar{p}}=0
.

There is one remaining
S^{1}
circle that we may perform KK reduction along to repeat the procedure of these past three sections.
However, we will stop here as such a case was previously studied more generally in
[
15
,
\lx@sectionsign
3]
as an HW Floer homology of four-manifolds.
It is indeed straightforward to show that KK reduction of 6d-Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{2}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
along the remaining
S^{1}
circle will physically realize an HW Floer homology of
CY_{2}
.

A Hyperkähler Floer Homology Specified by Hypercontact Three-Manifolds

Up until now, the most specific decomposition of the Spin
(7)
-manifold considered has been
\text{Spin}(7)=CY_{2}\times T^{3}\times\mathbb{R}
.
We will relax this specification and instead, consider the case where
\text{Spin}(7)=CY_{2}\times HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
, with
HC_{3}
being a hypercontact three-manifold.
By performing a topological reduction along
CY_{2}
using a technique similar to what Bershadsky-Johansen-Sadov-Vafa (BJSV) had developed in
[
3
]
, we will get a 4d sigma model whose target space is the moduli space of instantons on
CY_{2}
.
Recasting this 4d sigma model as an SQM in the hypercontact three-space will allow us to physically derive, from its topologically-invariant
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology, a hyperkähler Floer homology. In turn, this would serve as a physical proof of Hohloch-Noetzel-Salamon’s mathematical conjecture
[
21
]

[
26
,
\lx@sectionsign
5]
.

6.1
A Topological Reduction of Spin
(7)
Theory along
CY_{2}
and the Corresponding 4d Sigma Model

Any closed and orientable three-manifold admits a hypercontact structure, and is thus a hypercontact three-manifold; this includes examples such as
T^{3}
and
S^{3}

[
18
,
21
]
.
Recall however, that
CY_{2}\times HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
must admit a Spin
(7)
structure.
This means that our choice of
HC_{3}
must be such that
b_{1}(HC_{3})>2
.
20
20

20

For
CY_{2}\times HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
to admit a Spin
(7)
structure,
CY_{2}\times HC_{3}
must admit a
G_{2}
structure,
for which it is known that
b_{1}(HC_{3})>2

[
26
, Remark 5.1 (ii)]
.

With that said, let us begin by first performing a topological reduction along
CY_{2}
.
21
21

21

Here, the compact
CY_{2}
’s are non-trivial, such as K3.

To effect the topological reduction of Spin
(7)
theory along
CY_{2}
, we have to scale the Spin
(7)
-manifold metric
g_{\text{Spin}(7)}
appropriately.
This can be done by writing it as a block diagonal with a vanishing scale parameter
\epsilon
, i.e.,
g_{\text{Spin}(7)}=\epsilon^{1/2}g_{CY_{2}}\oplus\epsilon^{-1/2}g_{HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}}
.
By doing so, in the vanishing limit of
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
, only
g_{HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}}
will remain and the theory surviving the reduction process will be a 4d theory on
HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
.
22
22

22

This is similar to the BJSV topological reduction process
[
3
]
, where given a manifold
M=M_{X}\times M_{Y}
, instead of topologically reducing
M_{X}
and leaving
M_{Y}
alone, we are simultaneously topologically reducing and enlarging
M_{X}
and
M_{Y}
, respectively.
Both processes would result in the same physical phenomenon, where the size of
M_{Y}
is infinitely larger than
M_{X}
in the vanishing limit of
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
.
Indeed, we are able to reproduce the results that BJSV arrived at in
[
3
]
using this topological reduction process.

Topological Reduction Along
CY_{2}

Using the
\epsilon
-scaled metric in (
2.4
), in the vanishing limit of
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
, terms with positive powers of
\epsilon
in the action can be ignored as they will vanish, whilst terms with no power of
\epsilon
are of interest as they will survive the reduction process.
These terms can be identified as those with a single contraction in both
CY_{2}
and
HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
in (
2.4
) (and thus (
3.1
)), i.e.,

\displaystyle S_{\text{Spin}(7),\epsilon^{0}}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{CY_{2}\times HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}}\differential{t}\differential[7]{x}\Tr\bigg(
& 2 |F_{tM}^+|^2
+ 2i (D_t \psi_M - D_M \psi_t )\chi^{tM}
+ 2i (D_a \psi_M - D_M \psi_a) \chi^{aM}
\\
& - \frac{i}{2} \varphi\left\lbrace\chi_{tM}, \chi^{tM} \right\rbrace- \frac{i}{2} \varphi\left\lbrace\chi_{aM}, \chi^{aM} \right\rbrace\bigg)\,,

(6.1)

where
M\in\{4,\dots,7\}
is the index on
CY_{2}
and
a\in\{1,2,3\}
is the index on
HC_{3}
.
On the other hand, terms with negative powers of
\epsilon
will blow up in the vanishing limit of
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
, and thus need to be set to zero to ensure the finiteness of the action.
These terms are identified with those that have more than one contraction in
CY_{2}
in (
2.4
), which (for the bosonic sector of the action) work out to be

F_{MN}^{+}=0\,.

(6.2)

Keep in mind that the self-duality of the
F^{+}
’s in (
6.1
) and (
6.2
) is still to be understood as a self-duality on the Spin
(7)
-manifold, i.e.,
F_{tM}^{+}=\frac{1}{2}(F_{tM}+\phi_{tMaN}F^{aN})
and
F_{MN}^{+}=\frac{1}{2}(F_{MN}+\frac{1}{2}\phi_{MNPQ}F^{PQ}+\frac{1}{2}\phi_{MNab}F^{ab})
, with the Spin
(7)
-structure in (
2.2
).

When the vanishing limit of
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
is finally taken to topologically reduce along
CY_{2}
, we can take
\partial_{M}\rightarrow 0
, and will find that the gauge connections with indices on
HC_{3}
are no longer dynamical in the resulting 4d theory and thus, can be integrated out.
The finiteness condition of (
6.2
) will then simply be the ASD
G
-instanton equation on
CY_{2}
, i.e.,

F_{CY_{2}}^{+}=0\,.

(6.3)

Note that the bosonic fields surviving the topological reduction process (after integrating out the gauge fields) in (
6.1
), will consist only of connections on
CY_{2}
which are also scalars on the 4d worldvolume.
Imposing the finiteness condition (
6.3
) on the resulting 4d theory means that we can interpret the bosonic fields
\Phi
as maps
\Phi:HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
from the 4d worldvolume
HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
to the moduli space
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
of ASD
G
-instantons on
CY_{2}
(henceforth referred to as the moduli space of instantons on
CY_{2}
), i.e., solutions to (
6.3
) (modulo gauge equivalence).
In other words, we will have a 4d
\mathcal{N}=2
topological sigma model on
HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
with target space
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
.

The 4d Sigma Model Action

Let us now determine the action of the 4d sigma model.
First, in the vanishing limit of
\epsilon\rightarrow 0
, (
6.1
) becomes

S_{\text{4d-}\sigma}=\frac{1}{2e^{2}}\int_{HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}}\differential{t}\differential[3]{x}\bigg(\partial_{t}A_{M}\partial^{t}A^{M}+\partial_{a}A_{M}\partial^{a}A^{M}-\partial_{t}A_{M}\phi^{taMN}\partial_{a}A_{N}+\dots\bigg)\,.

(6.4)

Next, by using the cotangent basis
\kappa_{iM}
of
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
, we can write the boson in (
6.4
) as

A_{M}=X^{i}\kappa_{iM}\,,

(6.5)

where
X^{i}
and
i\in 4\mathbb{N}
are coordinates and indices on the hyperkähler
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
.
This means that (
6.4
) can therefore finally be written as

S_{\text{4d-}\sigma}=\frac{1}{2e^{2}}\int_{HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}}\differential{t}\differential[3]{x}g_{i\bar{\jmath}}\bigg(\partial_{t}X^{i}\partial^{t}X^{\bar{\jmath}}+\partial_{a}X^{i}\partial^{a}X^{\bar{\jmath}}+\dots\bigg)+\tau\int_{HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}}\sum_{a=1}^{3}\Omega_{a}\wedge\Phi^{*}(\omega_{a})\,,

(6.6)

where
g_{i\bar{\jmath}}
is the metric on
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
;

\Omega_{a}
and
\omega_{a}
are the Kähler two-forms of
HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
and
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
, respectively, with respect to their three complex structures;
23
23

23

HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
and
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
are both hyperkähler, and thus support three complex structures
I_{1}
,
I_{2}
, and
I_{3}\equiv-I_{1}I_{2}
for the former, and
J_{1}
,
J_{2}
, and
J_{3}=-J_{1}J_{2}
for the latter, satisfying the quaternionic relations
I_{a}^{2}=J_{a}^{2}=-1
.
These hyperkähler manifolds also have a corresponding Kähler two-form for each of the three complex structures,
\Omega_{a}
for the former, and
\omega_{a}
for the latter.
The reason for this unusual orientation of (
I_{3}
,
J_{3}
) with an additional negative sign is purely for the ease of notation.

and the last term is a topological term containing the pullback from
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
onto the worldvolume with some constant
\tau
.

In other words, topological reduction of Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{2}\times HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
along
CY_{2}
results in a 4d
\mathcal{N}=2
sigma model on
HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
with target space
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
, and action (
6.6
).

6.2
A 1d SQM in the Hypercontact Three-space of Instantons on
CY_{2}

The BPS equations of the 4d Sigma Model

First, note that the BPS equations of the 4d sigma model with action (
6.6
), will descend from (
2.5
) for the fields that survive the topological reduction of Spin
(7)
theory along
CY_{2}
.
The explicit components of the BPS equations are
24
24

24

In the following expressions, there are no gauge fields in the worldvolume theory as they are integrated out. We have also lowered the indices of the fields.

\displaystyle\partial_{t}A_{4}-\partial_{1}A_{5}-\partial_{2}A_{6}+\partial_{3}A_{7}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(6.7)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}A_{5}+\partial_{1}A_{4}+\partial_{2}A_{7}+\partial_{3}A_{6}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}A_{6}-\partial_{1}A_{7}+\partial_{2}A_{4}-\partial_{3}A_{5}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\partial_{t}A_{7}+\partial_{1}A_{6}-\partial_{2}A_{5}-\partial_{3}A_{4}

\displaystyle=0\,.

Let us define the action of the three complex structures
J_{a}
of the hyperkähler
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
(introduced in
footnote 23
) on the target space cotangent bases as

\displaystyle J_{1}\kappa_{i4}

\displaystyle=\kappa_{i5}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad J_{1}\kappa_{i6}

\displaystyle=\kappa_{i7}\,,

(6.8)

\displaystyle J_{2}\kappa_{i4}

\displaystyle=\kappa_{i6}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad J_{2}\kappa_{i5}

\displaystyle=-\kappa_{i7}\,,

\displaystyle J_{3}\kappa_{i4}

\displaystyle=-\kappa_{i7}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad J_{3}\kappa_{i5}

\displaystyle=-\kappa_{i6}\,.

Then, by expressing (
6.7
) in terms of the
X^{i}
coordinates via (
6.5
), it becomes

\partial_{t}X^{i}=-\partial_{1}X^{i}J_{1}-\partial_{2}X^{i}J_{2}-\partial_{3}X^{i}J_{3}\,.

(6.9)

If we also define the action of the three complex structures
I_{a}
of the hyperkähler
HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
worldvolume (also introduced in
footnote 23
) on the worldvolume cotangent bases as

\displaystyle I_{1}\partial_{t}

\displaystyle=-\partial_{1}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad I_{1}\partial_{2}

\displaystyle=\partial_{3}\,,

(6.10)

\displaystyle I_{2}\partial_{t}

\displaystyle=-\partial_{2}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad I_{2}\partial_{1}

\displaystyle=-\partial_{3}\,,

\displaystyle I_{3}\partial_{t}

\displaystyle=-\partial_{3}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad I_{3}\partial_{1}

\displaystyle=\partial_{2}\,,

we can write (
6.9
) more compactly as

dX^{i}-\sum_{a=1}^{3}I_{a}dX^{i}J_{a}=0\,,

(6.11)

where ‘
d
’ is the exterior derivative on the
HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
worldvolume.
This is the Cauchy-Riemann-Fueter equation for the aholomorphic map
X^{i}:HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
.
25
25

25

Aholomorphic maps are also sometimes referred to in the literature as triholomorphic, quaternionic, or q-holomorphic maps
[
19
]
.

The 1d SQM Action

Next, using the BPS equation in (
6.9
), we can re-express the action of the 4d sigma model with target space
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
in (
6.6
) as

S_{\text{4d-}\sigma}=\frac{1}{2e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\differential{t}\int_{HC_{3}}\differential[3]{x}\left(\left|\partial_{t}X^{i}+\sum_{a=1}^{3}\partial_{a}X^{i}J_{a}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,,

(6.12)

where the “
\dots
” will now contain the fermionic
and
topological terms of (
6.6
).
Lastly, after suitable rescalings, we obtain the equivalent SQM action from (
6.12
) as

S_{\text{SQM},\text{4d-}\sigma}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\differential{t}\left(\left|\derivative{X^\alpha}{t}+g^{\alpha\beta}_{\mathcal{M}(HC_{3},\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})}\partialderivative{V_{\sigma}}{X^\beta}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,,

(6.13)

where
X^{\alpha}
and
(\alpha,\beta)
are coordinates and indices on the hypercontact three-space
\mathcal{M}(HC_{3},\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})
of instantons on
CY_{2}
, which is the space of smooth maps from
HC_{3}
to
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
;

g_{\mathcal{M}(HC_{3},\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})}
is the metric on
\mathcal{M}(HC_{3},\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})
;
and
V_{\sigma}(X^{\alpha})
is the potential function.

Localizing onto Time-invariant Fueter Maps from
HC_{3}
to
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})

Applying again the squaring argument
[
4
]
with (
6.13
) as the equivalent SQM action, the configurations that the equivalent SQM localizes onto are those that set the LHS and RHS of (
6.9
)
simultaneously
to zero.
In other words, the equivalent SQM localizes onto time-invariant Fueter maps from
HC_{3}
to
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
.

6.3
A Hyperkähler Floer Homology of Instantons on
CY_{2}
and Specified by
HC_{3}

As before, since the resulting 4d theory on
HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
can be expressed as a 1d SQM in the space
\mathcal{M}(HC_{3},\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})
, its partition function can, like in (
3.6
), be written as

\mathcal{Z}_{\text{Spin}(7),HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}}(G)=\sum_{s}\mathcal{F}^{s}_{\text{4d-}\sigma,HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}}\,,

(6.14)

where
\mathcal{F}^{s}_{\text{4d-}\sigma,HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}}
, in the
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology of the 4d-sigma model, is the
s^{\text{th}}
contribution to the partition function, and the summation in ‘
s
’ is over all isolated and non-degenerate time-invariant Fueter maps from
HC_{3}
to
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
in
\mathcal{M}(HC_{3},\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})
that the equivalent SQM localizes onto.
26
26

26

This presumption that the configurations will be isolated and non-degenerate is justified because (the
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology of) Spin
(7)
theory is topological in all directions and therefore invariant when we topologically reduce along
CY_{2}
from Spin
(7)
theory.
Thus, if
HC_{3}
and
CY_{2}
(where
HC_{3}\times CY_{2}=G_{2}
) are chosen such as to satisfy the transversality assumptions of
footnote 7
,
\mathcal{Z}_{\text{Spin}(7),HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}}
will be a discrete and non-degenerate sum of contributions, just like
\mathcal{Z}_{\text{Spin}(7),G_{2}\times\mathbb{R}}
.
We shall henceforth assume such a choice of
HC_{3}
and
CY_{2}
whence the presumption would hold.

Via a similar analysis to that in

§

3.2
with (
6.13
) as the action for the equivalent 1d SQM model, we find that we can also write (
6.14
) as

\boxed{\begin{aligned} \mathcal{Z}_{\text{Spin}(7),HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}}(G)&=\sum_{s}\mathcal{F}^{s}_{\text{4d-}\sigma,HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}}\\
&=\sum_{s}\text{HHKF}_{d_{s}}\left(HC_{3},\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})\right)=\mathcal{Z}^{\text{hyperkählerFloer}}_{HC_{3},\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}}\end{aligned}}

(6.15)

where each
\mathcal{F}^{s}_{\text{4d-}\sigma,HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}}
can be identified with a
novel
Floer homology class

\text{HHKF}_{d_{s}}(HC_{3},\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2}))
, of degree
d_{s}
, that we shall henceforth name a hyperkähler Floer homology class of the hyperkähler
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
and specified by the hypercontact three-manifold
HC_{3}
.

Specifically, the
time-invariant Fueter maps from
HC_{3}
to
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
in
\mathcal{M}(HC_{3},\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})
that obey the simultaneous vanishing of the LHS and RHS of the
gradient flow equation

\boxed{\derivative{X^\alpha}{t}=-g^{\alpha\beta}_{\mathcal{M}(HC_{3},\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})}\partialderivative{V_{\sigma}}{X^\beta}}

(6.16)

will generate the chains of the hyperkähler Floer complex with Morse functional

\boxed{V_{\sigma}(X)=\frac{1}{2}\int_{HC_{3}}\differential[3]{x}\left(\sum_{a}\partial_{a}(X\wedge\star X)J_{a}\right)}

(6.17)

in
\mathcal{M}(HC_{3},\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})
, where “
\wedge
” and “
\star
” are the exterior product and Hodge star operator on
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
, respectively.
The aholomorphic flow lines, described by
time-varying
solutions to (
6.16
), are the Floer differentials such that the degree
d_{s}
of the corresponding chain in the hyperkähler Floer complex is counted by the outgoing flow lines at each time-invariant Fueter map in
\mathcal{M}(HC_{3},\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})
.
Such a Fueter map corresponds to a time-independent solution to the 3d equation

\boxed{\sum_{a}\partial_{a}X^{i}J_{a}=0}

(6.18)

A Physical Proof of Hohloch-Noetzel-Salamon’s Mathematical Conjecture

The existence of a Floer homology of hypercontact three-manifolds, whose chain complexes are generated by time-invariant Fueter maps from
HC_{3}
to
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
, and Floer differentials that count solutions to the time-varying Cauchy-Riemann-Fueter equation for aholomorphic maps from
HC_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
to
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
, was conjectured by Hohloch-Noetzel-Salamon in
[
21
]

[
26
,
\lx@sectionsign
5]
, where the term “hyperkähler Floer homology” was first coined.
We have therefore furnished a physical proof of their mathematical conjecture.

Symplectic Floer Homologies of Instanton Moduli Spaces

In this section, we will specialize to several specific (not necessarily closed)
HC_{3}
.
In particular, we will be specializing to
HC_{3}
’s that contain combinations of
S^{1}
circles,
I
intervals, and
\mathbb{R}
lines.
This will allow us to interpret the hyperkähler Floer homologies of
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
and specified by these
HC_{3}
’s as symplectic, or symplectic intersection Floer homologies of some loop space, path space, or both, of
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
.

7.1
A Symplectic Floer Homology in the Triple Loop Space of Instantons on
CY_{2}

Let us look at the closed case of
HC_{3}=T^{3}
.
First, notice that the space
\mathcal{M}(T^{3},\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})
of maps from
T^{3}
to
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
) can be interpreted as the triple loop space
L^{3}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
of instantons on
CY_{2}
.
This means that the 1d SQM in
\mathcal{M}(T^{3},\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})
with action (
6.13
) can also be interpreted as a 1d SQM in
L^{3}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
. Therefore, we can interpret each
\text{HHKF}_{d_{s}}(T^{3},\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2}))
as a Floer homology class in
L^{3}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
, i.e.,

\boxed{\text{HHKF}_{d_{s}}\left(T^{3},\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})\right)=\text{HSF}^{\text{Fuet}}_{d_{s}}\left(L^{3}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}\right)}

(7.1)

where each
\text{HSF}^{\text{Fuet}}_{d_{s}}(L^{3}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})
is a
novel
symplectic Floer homology class, of degree
d_{s}
, in
L^{3}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
.
27
27

27

The term “symplectic” is used here in the sense that
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
is symplectic.

Specifically, the
time-invariant Fueter maps from
T^{3}
to
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
in
L^{3}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
obeying the simultaneous vanishing of LHS and RHS of (
6.16
) (with
\mathcal{M}(T^{3},\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})=L^{3}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
) will generate the chains of the symplectic Floer complex with Morse functional (
6.17
) (with
HC_{3}=T^{3}
).
The aholomorphic flow lines, described by
time-varying
solutions to (
6.16
), are the Floer differentials such that the degree
d_{s}
of the corresponding chain in the symplectic Floer complex is counted by the outgoing flow lines at each time-invariant Fueter map in
L^{3}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
.
Such a map corresponds to a time-independent solution to (
6.18
).

In other words, a hyperkähler Floer homology of
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
and specified by
T^{3}
can be interpreted as a symplectic Floer homology of
L^{3}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
.

7.2
A Symplectic Intersection Floer Homology in the Double Loop Space of Instantons on
CY_{2}

The 4d Sigma Model as a 2d A-model with Branes
\mathscr{L}_{0}
and
\mathscr{L}_{1}

Let us consider the case where one of the
S^{1}
circles is an interval
I
so
HC_{3}=I\times T^{2}
.
We will take
x^{3}
to be the coordinate on
I
, and relabel it as
r
for convenience.
Doing so, the action (
6.12
) can be expressed as

S_{\text{4d-}\sigma}=\frac{1}{2e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times I}\differential{t}\differential{r}\int_{T^{2}}\differential[2]{x}\left(\left|\partial_{t}X^{i}+(J_{3}\partial_{r}X^{i}+J_{1}\partial_{1}X^{i}+J_{2}\partial_{2}X^{i})\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,.

(7.2)

After suitable rescalings, it can be recast as
28
28

28

Here, we have made use of Stokes’ theorem and the fact that the
S^{1}
circles of
T^{2}
have no boundary to note that
\partial_{\{1,2\}}X^{i}
should vanish in their integration over
T^{2}
.

S_{\text{2d-}\sigma}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\differential{t}\int_{I}\differential{r}\left(\left|\partial_{t}X^{c}+J_{3}\partial_{r}X^{c}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,,

(7.3)

where
X^{c}
and
c
are coordinates and indices on the double loop space
L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
of maps from
T^{2}
to
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
.
This is a 2d sigma model on
I\times\mathbb{R}
with target space
L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
.

The 2d sigma model that we have obtained above describes open strings with worldsheet
I\times\mathbb{R}
propagating in
L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
, starting and ending on branes
\mathscr{L}_{0}
and
\mathscr{L}_{1}
.
To better describe this 2d sigma model, let us complexify the worldsheet, i.e., introduce complex coordinates
w=t+J_{3}r
.
Then, its BPS equation, easily read off from (
7.3
), can be expressed as

\partial_{\bar{w}}X^{c}=0\,.

(7.4)

These are holomorphic maps from the worldsheet to the target space.
As such, our 2d sigma model is an A-model, and
\mathscr{L}_{0}
and
\mathscr{L}_{1}
are isotropic-coisotropic A-branes in
L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
.
29
29

29

In the case of finite-dimensional symplectic manifolds, isotropic-coisotropic branes are Lagrangian, and thus middle-dimensional
[
24
]
.

In other words, the 4d sigma model on
I\times T^{2}\times\mathbb{R}
with target space
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
is equivalent to a 2d A-model on
I\times\mathbb{R}
with target space
L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
and branes
\mathscr{L}_{0}
and
\mathscr{L}_{1}
.

The 2d A-model as a 1d SQM in the Interval Space of
L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}

From the action of the A-model (
7.3
), we can, after suitable rescalings, obtain the equivalent SQM action as

S_{\text{SQM},\text{2d-}\sigma}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}}\differential{t}\left(\left|\derivative{X^\alpha}{t}+g^{\alpha\beta}_{I\rightarrow L^{2}}\partialderivative{V_{I \rightarrow L^2}}{X^\beta}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,,

(7.5)

where
X^{\alpha}
and
(\alpha,\beta)
are coordinates and indices on the interval space
\mathcal{T}(\mathscr{L}_{0},\mathscr{L}_{1})_{L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}}
of smooth trajectories from
\mathscr{L}_{0}
to
\mathscr{L}_{1}
in
L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
;

g_{I\rightarrow L^{2}}
is the metric on
\mathcal{T}(\mathscr{L}_{0},\mathscr{L}_{1})_{L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}}
;
and
V_{I\rightarrow L^{2}}(X^{\alpha})
is the potential function.
In other words, we equivalently have, from the 2d A-model, an SQM in
\mathcal{T}(\mathscr{L}_{0},\mathscr{L}_{1})_{L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}}
.

Localizing Onto Constant Intervals in
\mathcal{T}(\mathscr{L}_{0},\mathscr{L}_{1})_{L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}}
, or Intersection Points of
\mathscr{L}_{0}
and
\mathscr{L}_{1}

By the squaring argument
[
4
]
applied to (
7.5
), the configurations that the equivalent SQM localizes onto are those that set to zero
simultaneously
the LHS and RHS of the expression within the squared term in (
7.3
).
In other words, the equivalent SQM localizes onto time-invariant constant intervals in
\mathcal{T}(\mathscr{L}_{0},\mathscr{L}_{1})_{L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}}
.
In turn, these correspond to stationary trajectories between branes in
\mathcal{T}(\mathscr{L}_{0},\mathscr{L}_{1})_{L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}}
, i.e., intersection points of
\mathscr{L}_{0}
and
\mathscr{L}_{1}
.

A Symplectic Intersection Floer Homology of
L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}

Since the resulting 2d A-model on
I\times\mathbb{R}
with target space
L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
and action (
7.3
) can be interpreted as a 1d SQM with action (
7.5
), its partition function can, like in (
3.6
), be written as

\mathcal{Z}_{\text{Spin}(7),I\times T^{2}\times\mathbb{R}}(G)=\sum_{s}\mathcal{F}^{s}_{\text{2d-}\sigma,I\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}}\,,

(7.6)

where
\mathcal{F}^{s}_{\text{2d-}\sigma,I\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}}
, in the
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology, is the
s^{\text{th}}
contribution to the partition function, and the summation in ‘
s
’ is over all isolated and non-degenerate intersection points of
\mathscr{L}_{0}
and
\mathscr{L}_{1}
that the equivalent SQM localizes onto.
30
30

30

These points can always be made isolated and non-degenerate by adding physically-inconsequential
\mathcal{Q}
-exact terms to the SQM action which will (i) correspond to a deformation to the 2d A-model worldsheet such that the
\mathscr{L}_{0}
and
\mathscr{L}_{1}
branes can be moved to intersect only at isolated points, and (ii) deform the SQM potential accordingly such that its critical points are non-degenerate.

This then allows us, via a similar analysis to that in

§

3.2
with (
7.5
) as the action for the equivalent 1d SQM, to express (
7.6
) as

\boxed{\begin{aligned} \mathcal{Z}_{\text{Spin}(7),I\times T^{2}\times\mathbb{R}}(G)&=\sum_{s}\mathcal{F}^{s}_{\text{2d-}\sigma,I\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}}\\
&=\sum_{s}\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{d_{s}}\left(L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}},\mathscr{L}_{0},\mathscr{L}_{1}\right)=\mathcal{Z}^{\text{IntSympFloer}}_{\mathscr{L}_{0},\mathscr{L}_{1},L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}}\end{aligned}}

(7.7)

where each
\mathcal{F}^{s}_{\text{2d-}\sigma,I\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}}
can be identified with a
novel
symplectic intersection Floer homology class
\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{d_{s}}(L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}},\mathscr{L}_{0},\mathscr{L}_{1})
, of degree
d_{s}
, in
\mathcal{T}(\mathscr{L}_{0},\mathscr{L}_{1})_{L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}}
.

Specifically, intersections of the
\mathscr{L}_{0}
and
\mathscr{L}_{1}
branes in
L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
that correspond to the simultaneous vanishing of the LHS and RHS of the squared term in (
7.5
), will generate the chains of the symplectic intersection Floer complex in
\mathcal{T}(\mathscr{L}_{0},\mathscr{L}_{1})_{L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}}
.
The holomorphic flow lines, described by
time-varying
solutions to (
7.4
), are the Floer differentials such that the degree
d_{s}
of the corresponding chain in the symplectic intersection Floer complex is counted by the outgoing flow lines at each intersection of
\mathscr{L}_{0}
and
\mathscr{L}_{1}
in
L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
.

Hyperkähler Floer Homology of
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
and Specified by
I\times T^{2}
as a Symplectic Intersection Floer Homology of
L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}

Recall that this 2d A-model is equivalent to the 4d sigma model on
I\times T^{2}\times\mathbb{R}
with target space
\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
.
We know from

§

6.3
that the latter will physically realize a hyperkähler Floer homology of
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
and specified by
I\times T^{2}
.
This therefore allows us to interpret each
\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{d_{s}}(L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}},\mathscr{L}_{0},\mathscr{L}_{1})
as a hyperkähler Floer homology class, i.e.,

\boxed{\text{HHKF}_{d_{s}}\left(I\times T^{2},\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})\right)=\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{d_{s}}\left(L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}},\mathscr{L}_{0},\mathscr{L}_{1}\right)}

(7.8)

where each
\text{HHKF}_{d_{s}}(I\times T^{2},\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2}))
is a hyperkähler Floer homology class, of degree
d_{s}
, of the hyperkähler
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
and specified by the hypercontact three-manifold
I\times T^{2}
.

In other words, a hyperkähler Floer homology of
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
and specified by
I\times T^{2}
can be interpreted as a symplectic intersection Floer homology of
L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
.

7.3
A Symplectic Intersection Floer Homology in the Path Space of Loops of Instantons on
CY_{2}

The 4d Sigma Model as a
\theta
-generalized 2d A-model with
\theta
-generalized Branes
\mathcal{P}_{0}(\theta)
and
\mathcal{P}_{1}(\theta)

Let us consider the case where an
S^{1}
circle of
T^{2}
is replaced with an
\mathbb{R}
line, so
HC_{3}=I\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
.
We will take
x^{1}
to be the coordinate on
\mathbb{R}
, and relabel it as
\tau
for convenience.
Doing so, the action (
7.2
) can be expressed as

S_{\text{4d-}\sigma}=\frac{1}{2e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times I}\differential{\tau}\differential{r}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times S^{1}}\differential{t}\differential{x^2}\left(\left|\partial_{\tau}X^{i}-(J_{1}\partial_{t}X^{i}-J_{2}\partial_{r}X^{i}+J_{3}\partial_{2}X^{i})\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,.

(7.9)

Notice that we can make use of the rotational symmetry of
\mathbb{R}^{2}
to rotate the 4d sigma model about the
I
interval.
This can be achieved by performing a quaternionic conjugation with respect to the
J_{1}
complex structure by an angle
\theta
.
31
31

31

Note that the complex structure of the worldvolume is uniquely determined by the complex structure of the target space.
Therefore, performing a quaternionic conjugation with respect to the
J_{1}
complex structure of the target space is equivalent to performing a quaternionic conjugation with respect to the
I_{1}
complex structure of the
\mathbb{R}^{2}
plane in the worldvolume.

Doing so, the action (
7.9
) becomes

S_{\text{4d-}\sigma}=\frac{1}{2e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times I}\differential{\tau}\differential{r}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times S^{1}}\differential{t}\differential{x^2}\left(\left|\partial_{\tau}X^{i}-(J^{\theta}_{1}\partial_{t}X^{i}-J^{\theta}_{2}\partial_{r}X^{i}+J^{\theta}_{3}\partial_{2}X^{i})\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,,

(7.10)

where
J^{\theta}_{a}=e^{-J_{1}\theta/2}J_{a}e^{J_{1}\theta/2}
is a
\theta
-rotated complex structure with respect to
J_{1}
.
The BPS equation of the
\theta
-rotated theory, easily read off from (
7.10
), can be understood to be a
\theta
-deformed Cauchy-Riemann-Fueter equation of the aholomorphic maps
X^{i}:I\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
, where the target space also picks up the effect of the rotation.

After suitable rescalings, the
\theta
-rotated 4d sigma model can be recast as
32
32

32

Just as in
footnote 28
, we make use of Stokes’ theorem and the fact that
S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
have no boundary to note that
\partial_{\{t,2\}}X^{i}
should vanish in their integration over
S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
.

S_{\text{2d-}\sigma}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}\times I}\differential{\tau}\differential{r}\left(\left|\partial_{\tau}X^{e}+J^{\theta}_{2}\partial_{r}X^{e}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,,

(7.11)

where
X^{e}
and
e
are coordinates and indices on the
\theta
-rotated path space
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})
of maps from
\mathbb{R}
to the loop space
L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
of maps from
S^{1}
to
\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
.
This is a 2d sigma model on
I\times\mathbb{R}
with target space
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})
.

At
\theta=0
,
\pi/2
, and
\pi
, this 2d sigma model is a 2d A-model on
I\times\mathbb{R}
with target space
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})
, describing open strings on the worldsheet propagating in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})
.
In general, as
\theta
interpolates between
0
,
\pi/2
, and
\pi
, we can understand the 2d sigma model to be a
\theta
-generalized 2d A-model with
\theta
-generalized branes
\mathcal{P}_{0}(\theta)
and
\mathcal{P}_{1}(\theta)
, which are regular isotropic-coisotropic A-branes when
\theta=0
,
\pi/2
, and
\pi
.
Such a
\theta
-generalized A-model with
\theta
-generalized branes was also observed in a similar setting in
[
15
,
\lx@sectionsign
6.6]
, where it was given the name “A
θ
-model” with “A
θ
-branes”.

A Symplectic Intersection Floer Homology of
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})

We will next follow the analysis of

§

7.2
, with (
7.11
) as the action for the equivalent 2d sigma model.
Doing so, we will find that the 2d A
θ
-model can then be further recast as a 1d SQM in the interval space
\mathcal{T}(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1})_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})}
of smooth trajectories from
\mathcal{P}_{0}(\theta)
to
\mathcal{P}_{1}(\theta)
in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})
.
Thus, its partition function can therefore be expressed as
33
33

33

In the final expression, the ‘
\theta
’ label is omitted as the physical theory is actually equivalent for all values of
\theta
.

\boxed{\begin{aligned} \mathcal{Z}_{\text{Spin}(7),I\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}(G)&=\sum_{s}\mathcal{F}^{s}_{\text{2d-}\sigma,I\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})}\\
&=\sum_{s}\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{d_{s}}\left(\mathcal{M}\left(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}\right),\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1}\right)=\mathcal{Z}^{\text{IntSympFloer}}_{\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})}\end{aligned}}

(7.12)

where each
\mathcal{F}^{s}_{\text{2d-}\sigma,I\times\mathbb{R}\rightarrow\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})}
can be identified with a
novel
symplectic intersection Floer homology class
\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{d_{s}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}),\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1})
, of degree
d_{s}
, in
\mathcal{T}(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1})_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})}
.

Specifically, intersections of the
\mathcal{P}_{0}(\theta)
and
\mathcal{P}_{1}(\theta)
branes in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})
that correspond to the simultaneous vanishing of the LHS and RHS of the squared term in (
7.11
), will generate the chains of the symplectic intersection Floer complex in
\mathcal{T}(\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1})_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})}
.
The holomorphic flow lines, described by
\tau
-varying
solutions to the expression within the squared term in (
7.11
), are the Floer differentials such that the degree
d_{s}
of the corresponding chain in the symplectic intersection Floer complex is counted by the outgoing flow lines at each intersection of
\mathcal{P}_{0}(\theta)
and
\mathcal{P}_{1}(\theta)
in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})
.

Hyperkähler Floer Homology of
\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
and Specified by
I\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
as a Symplectic Intersection Floer Homology of
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})

Recall again that this 2d A
θ
-model is equivalent to the 4d sigma model on
I\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
with target space
\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
.
We know, again, from

§

6.3
that the latter will physically realize a hyperkähler Floer homology of
\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
and specified by
I\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
.
This therefore allows us to interpret each
\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{d_{s}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}),\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1})
as a hyperkähler Floer homology class, i.e.,

\boxed{\text{HHKF}_{d_{s}}\left(I\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R},\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})\right)=\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{d_{s}}\left(\mathcal{M}\left(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}\right),\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1}\right)}

(7.13)

where each
\text{HHKF}_{d_{s}}(I\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R},\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2}))
is a hyperkähler Floer homology class, of degree
d_{s}
, of the hyperkähler
\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
and specified by the hypercontact three-manifold
I\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
.

In other words, a hyperkähler Floer homology of
\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
and specified by
I\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
can be interpreted as a symplectic intersection Floer homology of
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})
.

Atiyah-Floer Type Dualities

In this section, we will consider Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
, and perform a Tyurin degeneration of
CY_{3}
along a
CY_{2}
surface
[
13
]
.
Then, by exploiting the topological invariance of the underlying Spin
(7)
theory under a shrinking of
CY_{2}
and an
S^{1}
circle, we will find that the results of the previous sections on Floer homologies naturally lead to the derivation of novel Atiyah-Floer type dualities.
In particular, we will derive a (i) Spin
(7)
Atiyah-Floer type duality of
CY_{3}\times S^{1}
, and (ii) 7d-Spin
(7)
Atiyah-Floer type duality of
CY_{3}
.

8.1
Splitting Spin
(7)
Theory on
CY_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}

A Tyurin Degeneration of
CY_{3}

Let us begin with Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
, where
M_{1}
is a one-manifold.
The “splitting” will be done on the
CY_{3}
submanifold along a
CY_{2}
surface.
34
34

34

The
CY_{2}
surface that we are considering in this section will exclude trivial
CY_{2}=T^{4}
surfaces.

This is accomplished by performing a Tyurin degeneration of
CY_{3}
along a
CY_{2}
surface, i.e.,
CY_{3}=CY_{3}^{\prime}\bigcup_{CY_{2}}CY_{3}^{\prime\prime}
as shown in
3(a)
, whence we can view
CY_{3}^{\prime}
and
CY_{3}^{\prime\prime}
as non-trivial fibrations of
CY_{2}
over a disk
D
, where
CY_{2}
goes to zero size at the boundary of the disk.

\mathcal{S}
CY_{3}^{\prime}
CY_{3}^{\prime\prime}

(a)

CY_{3}
as a connected sum of Calabi-Yau threefolds
CY_{3}^{\prime}
and
CY_{3}^{\prime\prime}
along the surface
\mathcal{S}=CY_{2}
.

\mathscr{B}
\text{Spin}(7)^{\prime}

Spin
(7)
-manifold splits into Spin

and Spin
(7)^{\prime\prime}
along their common boundary

These figures are higher-dimensional generalizations of
[

, Fig. 2]
.

The metric on
CY_{3}^{\prime}
and
CY_{3}^{\prime\prime}
can then be written as

\differential[2]{\mathsf{s}}_{CY_{3}}=(\differential{r})^{2}+r^{2}(\differential{\vartheta})^{2}+f(r,\vartheta)(g_{CY_{2}})_{MN}\differential{x^M}\differential{x^N}\,,

(8.1)

where
x^{M}
and
(M,N)
are coordinates and indices on
CY_{2}
;

r
and
\vartheta
are, respectively, the radius and angle on
D
, which, in turn, can be viewed as a non-trivial
S^{1}
fibration of an interval
I
that is closed at one end;
and

is a scalar function of

and
\vartheta
.

Splitting the Spin
(7)
-manifold

From such a splitting of
CY_{3}
, this means that the Spin
(7)
-manifold is split as
\text{Spin}(7)=\text{Spin}(7)^{\prime}\bigcup_{\mathscr{B}}\text{Spin}(7)^{\prime\prime}
, where
\text{Spin}(7)^{\prime}=CY_{3}^{\prime}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
,
\text{Spin}(7)^{\prime\prime}=CY_{3}^{\prime\prime}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
, and
\mathscr{B}
is their common boundary.
This is illustrated in
3(b)
.

We can now exploit the topological invariance of Spin
(7)
theory to freely perform a Weyl rescaling of the corresponding Tyurin-degenerated metrics on
CY_{3}^{\prime}
and
CY_{3}^{\prime\prime}
, such that the metric on Spin
(7)^{\prime}
and Spin
(7)^{\prime\prime}
can be expressed as

\differential{\mathsf{s}}^{2}_{\mathrm{Spin}(7)}=\frac{1}{f(r,\vartheta)}\left[(\differential{t})^{2}+(\differential{s})^{2}+(\differential{r})^{2}+r^{2}(\differential{\vartheta})^{2}\right]+(g_{CY_{2}})_{MN}\differential{x}^{M}\differential{x}^{N}\,,

(8.2)

where
t
and
s
are the coordinates on
\mathbb{R}
and
M_{1}
, respectively.
The prefactor of
f(r,\vartheta)^{-1}
is effectively a scaling factor on
D^{\prime}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
and
D^{\prime\prime}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
, whence their topologies are left unchanged.
Since the theory is topological, we can replace the discs
D^{\prime}
and
D^{\prime\prime}
with their topological equivalents
I^{\prime}\times S^{1}
and
I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{1}
.
Thus, we can regard the Spin
(7)
-manifold as
\text{Spin}(7)=\text{Spin}(7)^{\prime}\bigcup_{\mathscr{B}}\text{Spin}(7)^{\prime\prime}
, where
\text{Spin}(7)^{\prime}=CY_{2}\times I^{\prime}\times S^{1}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
,
\text{Spin}(7)^{\prime\prime}=CY_{2}\times I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{1}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
, and
\mathscr{B}=CY_{2}\times S^{1}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
is their common boundary.

Hence, Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{3}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
can be regarded as a union of two Spin
(7)
theories, one on
CY_{2}\times I^{\prime}\times S^{1}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
and another on
CY_{2}\times I^{\prime\prime}\times S^{1}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
, along their common boundary
\mathscr{B}=CY_{2}\times S^{1}\times M_{1}\times\mathbb{R}
.

8.2
A Spin
(7)
Atiyah-Floer Type Duality of
CY_{3}\times S^{1}

Let us now take
M_{1}=S^{1}
.
From

§

7.2
, we know that Spin
(7)
theory on
\text{Spin}(7)^{\prime}=CY_{2}\times I^{\prime}\times T^{2}\times\mathbb{R}
and
\text{Spin}(7)^{\prime\prime}=CY_{2}\times I^{\prime\prime}\times T^{2}\times\mathbb{R}
, when topologically reduced along
CY_{2}
, can be interpreted as a 4d sigma model on
I^{\prime}\times T^{2}\times\mathbb{R}
and
I^{\prime\prime}\times T^{2}\times\mathbb{R}
with target space
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
and action (
7.2
).
In turn, these 4d sigma models can be interpreted as 2d sigma models on
I^{\prime}\times\mathbb{R}
and
I^{\prime\prime}\times\mathbb{R}
with action (
7.3
).
That is to say, Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{2}\times I^{\prime}\times T^{2}\times\mathbb{R}
and
CY_{2}\times I^{\prime\prime}\times T^{2}\times\mathbb{R}
, when topologically reduced along
CY_{2}
, can also be interpreted as 2d A-models on
I^{\prime}\times\mathbb{R}
and
I^{\prime\prime}\times\mathbb{R}
, with branes
(\mathscr{L}_{0},\mathscr{L}_{1/2})
and
(\mathscr{L}_{1/2},\mathscr{L}_{1})
in
L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
, respectively.

Spin
(7)
Theory on
CY_{3}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
as a 2d A-model on
I\times\mathbb{R}
with Branes
(\mathscr{L}_{0},\mathscr{L}_{1})

Therefore, the union of a Spin
(7)
theory on a Spin
(7)^{\prime}
-manifold and another on a Spin
(7)^{\prime\prime}
-manifold along their common boundary
\mathscr{B}
, to get a Spin
(7)

, can be interpreted as the union of a 2d A-model on

with branes
(\mathscr{L}_{0},\mathscr{L}_{1/2})
and another on

with branes

, to get a 2d A-model on

with branes
(\mathscr{L}_{0},\mathscr{L}_{1})
.
This union of the 2d A-models is illustrated in

.

In other words, Spin

with branes

.
This means that we can equate their partition functions in (
3.11
) and (
