), respectively, to get

\sum_{j}\text{HF}^{\text{Spin}(7)\text{-inst}}_{d_{j}}(CY_{3}\times S^{1},G)=\sum_{s}\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{d_{s}}\left(L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}},\mathscr{L}_{0},\mathscr{L}_{1}\right)\,.

(8.3)

A Spin
(7)
Atiyah-Floer Type Duality of
CY_{3}\times S^{1}

Let us now ascertain if there is a one-to-one correspondence between
(j,d_{j})
and
(s,d_{s})
, which will in turn imply that there is a degree-to-degree isomorphism between the Spin
(7)
instanton Floer homology of
CY_{3}\times S^{1}
and the symplectic intersection Floer homology of
L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
.

To ascertain if there is a one-to-one correspondence between ‘
j
’ and ‘
s
’, first, note that each ‘
j
’ refers to a time-invariant critical point of
V_{7}
in
\mathfrak{A}_{7}
, corresponding to a time-invariant solution to the 8d BPS equation on
CY_{3}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
in (
3.8
).
Second, note that each ‘
s
’ refers to a time-invariant critical point of
V_{I\rightarrow L^{2}}
in
\mathcal{T}(\mathscr{L}_{0},\mathscr{L}_{1})_{L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}}
, corresponding to a time-invariant solution to the 4d BPS equation on
I\times T^{2}\times\mathbb{R}
given by setting the LHS and RHS of the expression within the squared term in (
7.2
) simultaneously to zero.
Third, note that the 4d BPS equation is a direct topological reduction of the 8d BPS equation along
CY_{2}\subset CY_{3}
, whence there is a one-to-one correspondence between the time-invariant solutions of the former and the latter.
Altogether, this means that there is a one-to-one correspondence between ‘
j
’ and ‘
s
’.

To ascertain if there is a one-to-one correspondence between ‘
d_{j}
’ and ‘
d_{s}
’, first, note that the flow lines between time-invariant critical points of
V_{7}
in
\mathfrak{A}_{7}
realizing the Floer differential of
\text{HF}^{\text{Spin(7)}\text{-inst}}_{*}
, which counts ‘
d_{j}
’, correspond to time-varying solutions to the gradient flow equations (
3.7
).
This, in turn, corresponds to time-varying solutions to the 8d BPS equation on
CY_{3}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
in (
3.8
).
Second, note that the flow lines between time-invariant critical points of
V_{I\rightarrow L^{2}}
in
\mathcal{T}(\mathscr{L}_{0},\mathscr{L}_{1})_{L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}}
realizing the Floer differentials of
\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{*}
, which counts ‘
d_{s}
’, correspond to time-varying solutions to the gradient flow equations defined by setting to zero the expression within the squared term in (
7.5
).
This, in turn, corresponds to time-varying solutions to the 4d BPS equations on
I\times T^{2}\times\mathbb{R}
defined by setting to zero the expression within the squared term in (
7.2
).
Third, note again that the 4d BPS equations are a direct topological reduction of the 8d BPS equation along
CY_{2}\subset CY_{3}
, whence there is a one-to-one correspondence between solutions of the former and the latter.
Altogether, this means that there is a one-to-one correspondence between ‘
d_{j}
’ and ‘
d_{s}
’.

In other words, we do indeed have a one-to-one correspondence between
(j,d_{j})
and
(s,d_{s})
in (
8.3
), whence we would have the following degree-to-degree isomorphism between the Spin
(7)
instanton Floer homology classes of
CY_{3}\times S^{1}
and the symplectic intersection Floer homology classes of
L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}

\boxed{\text{HF}^{\text{Spin}(7)\text{-inst}}_{*}(CY_{3}\times S^{1},G)\cong\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{*}\left(L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}},\mathscr{L}_{0},\mathscr{L}_{1}\right)}

(8.4)

We thus have an Atiyah-Floer type duality, that we shall henceforth name a Spin
(7)
Atiyah-Floer type duality of
CY_{3}\times S^{1}
!

8.3
A 7d-Spin
(7)
Atiyah-Floer Type Duality of
CY_{3}

Let us now perform a KK reduction of Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{3}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
by shrinking the
S^{1}
circle to be infinitesimally small.
We know that, according to

§

4
, the LHS of (
8.3
) becomes (
4.7
), i.e.,

\sum_{j}\text{HF}^{\text{Spin}(7)\text{-inst}}_{d_{j}}(CY_{3}\times S^{1},G)\xrightarrow{S^{1}\rightarrow 0}\sum_{k}\text{HHF}^{G_{2}\text{-M}}_{d_{k}}(CY_{3},G)\,,

(8.5)

whilst the RHS of (
8.3
) will simply become
37
37

37

This can be seen by performing a straightforward KK reduction along an
S^{1}
circle of the 4d sigma model on
I\times T^{2}\times\mathbb{R}
from

§

7.2
.
The resulting 3d sigma model on
I\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
can be recast as a 2d A-model on
I\times\mathbb{R}
, this time with target space
L\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
.
Following the analysis of

§

7.2
, we will physically realize a symplectic intersection Floer homology of
L\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
generated by intersections of isotropic-coisotropic A-branes
\mathcal{L}_{*}
in
L\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
.

\sum_{s}\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{d_{s}}\left(L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}},\mathscr{L}_{0},\mathscr{L}_{1}\right)\xrightarrow{S^{1}\rightarrow 0}\sum_{u}\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{d_{u}}\left(L\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}},\mathcal{L}_{0},\mathcal{L}_{1}\right)\,,

(8.6)

where the branes
\mathcal{L}_{0}
and
\mathcal{L}_{1}
are isotropic-coisotropic A-branes in
L\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
.

In other words, we will have, from the KK reduction of Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{3}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}
,

\sum_{k}\text{HHF}^{G_{2}\text{-M}}_{d_{k}}(CY_{3},G)=\sum_{u}\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{d_{u}}\left(L\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}},\mathcal{L}_{0},\mathcal{L}_{1}\right)\,.

(8.7)

By application of the same arguments that led us from (
8.3
) to (
8.4
), we will find that (
8.7
) will mean that

\boxed{\text{HHF}^{G_{2}\text{-M}}_{*}(CY_{3},G)\cong\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{*}\left(L\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}},\mathcal{L}_{0},\mathcal{L}_{1}\right)}

(8.8)

In other words, we have an Atiyah-Floer type duality, that we shall henceforth name a 7d-Spin
(7)
Atiyah-Floer type duality of
CY_{3}
!

A Fukaya-Seidel Type
A_{\infty}
-category of Six-Manifolds

In this section, we will consider the case where
\text{Spin}(7)=CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and recast Spin
(7)
theory as either a 2d gauged Landau-Ginzburg (LG) model on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
or a 1d LG SQM in path space.
Following the program in
[
15
,
\lx@sectionsign
9]
, we will, via the
\text{Spin}(7)
partition function, be able to physically realize a novel Fukaya-Seidel (FS) type
A_{\infty}
-category of
CY_{3}
whose objects correspond to DT configurations on
CY_{3}
.
In doing so, we will furnish a physical proof of Haydys’ mathematical conjecture
[
20
]
.
Furthermore, by exploiting one of the Atiyah-Floer type dualities in

§

8
, we will find that this FS type
A_{\infty}
-category physically manifests a Hom-category.

9.1
Spin
(7)
Theory on
CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
as a 2d Model on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
or SQM in Path Space

For
\text{Spin}(7)=M_{6}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
to be a Spin
(7)
-manifold,
M_{6}\times\mathbb{R}
has to be a
G_{2}
-manifold.
This is possible if
M_{6}
is a closed and compact
CY_{3}

[
6
,
1
,
17
]
.
We will consider this case, and study Spin
(7)
theory on
\text{Spin}(7)=CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

By choosing
x^{0}=t
and
x^{1}=\tau
as the directions of
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, and collecting the terms without
\varphi
, (
2.4
) becomes

S_{\text{Spin}(7),CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}\differential{t}\differential{\tau}\differential[6]{x}\Tr\Big(
|F^+_{t \tau}|^2
+ |F^+_{t i}|^2
+ |F^+_{\tau i}|^2
+ \dots\Big)\,,

(9.1)

where “
\dots
” contain the fermion terms in (
2.4
), and
x^{i}
for
i\in\{2,\dots,7\}
are coordinates on
CY_{3}
.
We will now like to recast Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
with action (
9.1
) as a 2d model on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

Spin
(7)
Theory on
CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
as a 2d Model

To this end, first note that by expanding the self-dual
F^{+}
’s in (
9.1
), we are able to re-express the action as

\displaystyle S_{\text{Spin}(7),CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}=\frac{1}{4e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\differential{t}\differential{\tau}\int_{CY_{3}}\differential[6]{x}\Tr\bigg(
& \left|F_{t\tau} + \frac{1}{2} \phi_{t\tau ij} F^{ij} \right|^2
+ \left|F_{\tau i} + \phi_{\tau i t j} F^{t j} \right|^2
\\
& + \left|F_{t i}
+ \phi_{t i \tau j} F^{\tau j}
+ \frac{1}{2} \phi_{t i j k} F^{jk}
\right|^2
+ \dots\bigg)\,,

(9.2)

where the conditions that minimize the action (
9.2
) (and thus (
9.1
)) are easily identified to be

\displaystyle F_{t\tau}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\phi_{t\tau ij}F^{ij}\,,

(9.3)

\displaystyle F_{ti}+\phi_{ti\tau j}F^{\tau j}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\phi_{tijk}F^{jk}\,,

\displaystyle F_{\tau i}+\phi_{\tau itj}F^{tj}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\phi_{\tau ijk}F^{jk}\,.

Second, using the
(z^{1},z^{2},z^{3})
holomorphic coordinates on
CY_{3}
as defined in

§

4
, (
9.3
) becomes

\displaystyle F_{\tau t}

\displaystyle=\omega_{m\bar{n}}\mathcal{F}^{m\bar{n}}\,,

(9.4)

\displaystyle 2(D_{t}\mathcal{A}_{m}-\partial_{m}A_{t})-2i(D_{\tau}\mathcal{A}_{m}-\partial_{m}A_{\tau})

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{mpq}\mathcal{F}^{pq}\,,

\displaystyle 2(D_{\tau}\mathcal{A}_{m}-\partial_{m}A_{\tau})+2i(D_{t}\mathcal{A}_{m}-\partial_{m}A_{t})

\displaystyle=-\frac{i}{2}\varepsilon_{mpq}\mathcal{F}^{pq}\,.

Third, noticing that the last two equations are actually identical up to an overall factor of
i
, and that we are physically free to rotate
\mathbb{R}^{2}
about the origin, the linearly-independent equations of (
9.4
) become

\displaystyle F_{\tau t}

\displaystyle=\omega_{m\bar{n}}\mathcal{F}^{m\bar{n}}\,,

(9.5)

\displaystyle 2(D_{\tau}\mathcal{A}_{m}-\partial_{m}A_{\tau})+2i(D_{t}\mathcal{A}_{m}-\partial_{m}A_{t})

\displaystyle=-\frac{ie^{i\theta}}{2}\varepsilon_{mpq}\mathcal{F}^{pq}\,,

where
\theta
is the angle of rotation.
This allows us to write (
9.2
) as

S_{\text{Spin}(7),CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}=\frac{1}{4e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\differential{t}\differential{\tau}\int_{CY_{3}}\absolutevalue{\dd{z}}^{6}\Tr\Big(
|F_{\tau t} + \kappa|^2
+ 8 |D_\tau\mathcal{A}_m + i D_t \mathcal{A}_m + u_m|^2
+ \dots\Big)\,,

(9.6)

where

\kappa=-\omega_{m\bar{n}}\mathcal{F}^{m\bar{n}}\,,\qquad u_{m}=-\partial_{m}A_{\tau}-i\partial_{m}A_{t}+\frac{ie^{i\theta}}{4}\varepsilon_{mpq}\mathcal{F}^{pq}\,.

(9.7)

Lastly, after suitable rescalings, we can recast (
9.6
) as a 2d model, where the action is
38
38

38

To arrive at the following expression for the action, we have (i) employed Stokes’ theorem and the fact that
CY_{3}
has no boundary to omit terms with
\partial_{m}A_{\{t,\tau\}}
as they will vanish when integrated over
CY_{3}
, and (ii) integrated out an auxiliary scalar field
\mathfrak{H}_{6}(\kappa)=\omega_{a\bar{b}}\mathcal{F}^{a\bar{b}}
corresponding to the scalar
\kappa
, whose contribution to the action is
|\mathfrak{H}_{6}(\kappa)|^{2}
.

S_{\text{2d},\mathfrak{A}_{6}}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\differential{t}\differential{\tau}\Big(|F_{\tau t}|^{2}+|D_{\tau}\mathcal{A}^{a}+iD_{t}\mathcal{A}^{a}+u^{a}|^{2}+\dots\Big)\,.

(9.8)

Here,
\mathcal{A}^{a}
and
a
are holomorphic coordinates and indices on the space
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
of irreducible
\mathcal{A}_{m}
fields on
CY_{3}
; and

u^{a}=\frac{ie^{i\theta}}{4}\varepsilon^{abc}\mathcal{F}_{bc}

(9.9)

will correspond to
u_{m}
in (
9.7
).

In other words, Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
can be regarded as a 2d gauged sigma model along the
(t,\tau)
-directions with target space
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
and action (
9.8
).
We will now further recast this 2d gauged sigma model as a 1d SQM.

The 2d Model on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
with Target Space
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
as a 1d SQM

Singling out
\tau
as the direction in “time”, the equivalent SQM action can be obtained from (
9.8
) after suitable rescalings as
39
39

39

In the resulting SQM, as
A_{\tau}
has no field strength and is thus non-dynamical, it will be integrated out to furnish the Christoffel connection for the fermions in the SQM
[
15
]
, leaving us with an SQM without
A_{\tau}
.
We have also omitted a term
|\partial_{\tau}A_{t}|^{2}
in the following expression as it will just lead to the trivial condition
\partial_{\tau}A_{t}=0
.

S_{\text{SQM},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{6})}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int\differential{\tau}\left(\left|\partial_{\tau}\mathcal{A}^{\alpha}+g^{\alpha\beta}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{6})}\partialderivative{h_6}{\mathcal{A}^\beta}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,,

(9.10)

where
\mathcal{A}^{\alpha}
and
(\alpha,\beta)
are holomorphic coordinates and indices on the path space
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{6})
of maps from
\mathbb{R}
to
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
;
g_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{6})}
is the metric on
\mathcal{M}({\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{6}})
; and
h_{6}(\mathcal{A})
is the potential function.

In other words, Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
can also be regarded as a 1d SQM along
\tau
in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{6})
whose action is (
9.10
).

9.2
Non-constant Paths, Solitons, and DT Configurations

\theta
-deformed, Non-constant Paths in the SQM

The squaring argument
[
4
]
applied to (
9.10
) tells us that the equivalent SQM localizes onto configurations that set both the LHS and RHS of the expression within the squared term
simultaneously
to zero, i.e., the SQM localizes onto
\tau
-invariant critical points of
h_{6}(\mathcal{A})
that obey

\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{\alpha}=-\frac{e^{i\theta}}{4}\varepsilon^{\alpha\beta\gamma}\mathcal{F}_{\beta\gamma}\,.

(9.11)

These are
\tau
-invariant,
\theta
-deformed
, non-constant paths in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{6})
.

\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-solitons in the 2d Gauged Model

By comparing (
9.10
) with (
9.8
), we find that such
\tau
-invariant,
\theta
-deformed, non-constant paths in the SQM defined by (
9.11
), will correspond, in the 2d gauged sigma model with target space
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
, to configurations defined by

[A_{\tau},\mathcal{A}^{a}]+iD_{t}\mathcal{A}^{a}+u^{a}=0\,.

(9.12)

Via (
9.9
), we can write this as

\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}^{a}=-[A_{t},\mathcal{A}^{a}]+i[A_{\tau},\mathcal{A}^{a}]-\frac{e^{i\theta}}{4}\varepsilon^{abc}\mathcal{F}_{bc}\,.

(9.13)

These are
\tau
-invariant,
\theta
-deformed solitons along the
t
-direction in the 2d gauged sigma model, which also satisfy the condition

F_{\tau t}=0=\omega^{a\bar{b}}\mathcal{F}_{a\bar{b}}\,,

(9.14)

where
\omega^{a\bar{b}}\mathcal{F}_{a\bar{b}}=\mathfrak{H}_{6}(\kappa)
is the auxiliary scalar field in
footnote 38
.

We shall henceforth refer to such
\tau
-invariant,
\theta
-deformed solitons in the 2d gauged sigma model with target space
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
, defined by (
9.13
) and (
9.14
), as
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-solitons.

\tau
-independent,
\theta
-deformed Spin
(7)
Configurations in Spin
(7)
Theory

In turn, by comparing (
9.8
) with (
9.6
), we find that the 2d configurations defined by (
9.12
), will correspond, in Spin
(7)
theory, to 8d configurations defined by

[A_{\tau},A_{m}]+iD_{t}\mathcal{A}_{m}+u_{m}=0\,.

(9.15)

Via (
9.7
), we can write this as

\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}_{m}=\mathcal{D}_{m}\mathcal{A}_{t}-i\mathcal{D}_{m}A_{\tau}-\frac{e^{i\theta}}{4}\varepsilon_{mpq}\mathcal{F}^{pq}\,.

(9.16)

These are
\tau
-independent,
\theta
-deformed Spin
(7)
configurations on
CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
which also satisfy the conditions

\partial_{t}A_{\tau}=[A_{\tau},A_{t}]\,,\qquad\omega_{m\bar{n}}\mathcal{F}^{m\bar{n}}=0\,.

(9.17)

Spin
(7)
Configurations,
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-solitons, and Non-constant Paths

In short, these
\tau
-independent,
\theta
-deformed
Spin
(7)
configurations on
CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
that are defined by (
9.16
) and (
9.17
), will correspond to the
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-solitons defined by (
9.13
) and (
9.14
), which, in turn, will correspond to the
\tau
-invariant,
\theta
-deformed, non-constant paths in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{6})
defined by (
9.11
).

\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-soliton Endpoints Corresponding to
\theta
-deformed DT Configurations on
CY_{3}

Consider now the fixed endpoints of the
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-solitons at
t=\pm\infty
, where we also expect the finite-energy 2d gauge fields
A_{t},A_{\tau}
to decay to zero.
They are given by (
9.13
) and (
9.14
) with
\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}_{a}=0
and
A_{t},A_{\tau}\rightarrow 0
, i.e.,

\displaystyle e^{i\theta}\varepsilon^{abc}\mathcal{F}_{bc}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(9.18)

\displaystyle\omega^{a\bar{b}}\mathcal{F}_{a\bar{b}}

\displaystyle=0\,.

In turn, they will correspond, in Spin
(7)
theory, to
(t,\tau)
-independent,

\theta
-deformed configurations that obey

\displaystyle e^{i\theta}\varepsilon_{mpq}\mathcal{F}^{pq}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(9.19)

\displaystyle\omega_{m\bar{n}}\mathcal{F}^{m\bar{n}}

\displaystyle=0\,.

Notice that (
9.19
) can also be obtained from (
9.16
) and (
9.17
) with
\partial_{t}\mathcal{A}_{m}=0
and
A_{t},A_{\tau}\rightarrow 0
.

The equations in (
9.19
) are a
\theta
-deformed version of Donaldson-Uhlenbeck-Yau (DUY) equations on
CY_{3}
.
At
\theta=0,\pi
, they become the regular DUY equations on
CY_{3}
, which are simply DT equations on
CY_{3}
with the scalar being zero.
Configurations spanning the space of solutions to these equations shall, in the rest of this section, be referred to as DT configuration on
CY_{3}
.

In other words, the
(t,\tau)
-independent,
\theta
-deformed Spin
(7)
configurations corresponding to the endpoints of the
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-solitons, are
\theta
-deformed DT configurations on
CY_{3}
.
We will also assume choices of
CY_{3}
satisfying
footnote 12
whereby such configurations are isolated and non-degenerate.
40
40

40

At
\theta=0
, the moduli space of such configurations is the moduli space of undeformed DT configurations on
CY_{3}
.
For such a choice of
CY_{3}
, this moduli space will be made of isolated and non-degenerate points.
Therefore, at
\theta=0
, the endpoints of the
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-solitons will be isolated and non-degenerate.
As the physical theory is symmetric under a variation of
\theta
, this observation about the endpoints of the
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-solitons will continue to hold true for any value of
\theta
. Hence, the presumption that the moduli space of
\theta
-deformed DT configurations on
CY_{3}
will be made of isolated and non-degenerate points, is justified.

In short, from the equivalent 1d SQM of Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, the theory localizes onto
\tau
-invariant,
\theta
-deformed, non-constant paths in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{6})
, which, in turn, will correspond to
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-solitons in the 2d gauged sigma model whose endpoints correspond to
\theta
-deformed DT configurations on
CY_{3}
.

9.3
The 2d Model on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
and an Open String Theory in
\mathfrak{A}_{6}

Flow Lines of the SQM as BPS Worldsheets of the 2d Model

The classical trajectories or flow lines of the equivalent SQM are governed by the gradient flow equation (defined by setting to zero the expression within the squared term in (
9.10
)), i.e.,

\derivative{\mathcal{A}^\alpha}{\tau}=-g^{\alpha\beta}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{6})}\partialderivative{h_6}{\mathcal{A}^\beta}\,,

(9.20)

and they go from one
\tau
-invariant critical point of
h_{6}
to another in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{6})
.
In the 2d gauged sigma model with target space
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
, these flow lines will correspond to worldsheets that have, at
\tau=\pm\infty
,
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-solitons.
41
41

41

The
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-soliton can translate in the
\tau
-direction due to its “center of mass” motion, and because it is
\tau
-invariant, it is effectively degenerate. This reflects the fact that generically, each critical point of
h_{6}
is degenerate and does not correspond to a point but a real line
\mathbb{R}
in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},{\mathfrak{A}}_{6})
. Nonetheless, one can perturb
h_{6}
via the addition of physically-inconsequential
\mathcal{Q}
-exact terms to the SQM action, and collapse the degeneracy such that the critical points really correspond to points in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},{\mathfrak{A}}_{6})
. This is equivalent to factoring out the center of mass degree of freedom of the
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-soliton, and fixing it at
\tau=\pm\infty
.

These solitons shall be denoted as
\gamma_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{6})
, and are defined by (
9.13
) with finite-energy gauge fields
A_{t},A_{\tau}\rightarrow 0
, i.e.,

\derivative{\mathcal{A}^a}{t}=-\frac{e^{i\theta}}{4}\varepsilon^{abc}\mathcal{F}_{bc}\,.

(9.21)

Their endpoints
\gamma(\pm\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{6})
at
t=\pm\infty
are defined by

e^{i\theta}\varepsilon^{abc}\mathcal{F}_{bc}=0\,,

(9.22)

which is simply (
9.21
) with
d_{t}\mathcal{A}^{a}=0
.

Note that the flow lines are governed by the gradient flow equations, which are actually the BPS equations of the 1d SQM.
This means that the worldsheets that they will correspond to are governed by the BPS equations of the equivalent 2d gauged sigma model with target space
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
(defined by setting to zero the expression within the squared terms in (
9.8
)), i.e.,

\displaystyle F_{\tau t}

\displaystyle=0\,,

(9.23)

\displaystyle D_{\tau}\mathcal{A}^{a}+iD_{t}\mathcal{A}^{a}+u^{a}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{H}_{6}(\kappa)

\displaystyle=0\,,

or more explicitly,

\displaystyle\derivative{A_\tau}{t}-\derivative{A_t}{\tau}+[A_{t},A_{\tau}]

\displaystyle=0\,,

(9.24)

\displaystyle\frac{D\mathcal{A}^{a}}{D\tau}+i\frac{D\mathcal{A}^{a}}{Dt}

\displaystyle=-\frac{ie^{i\theta}}{4}\varepsilon^{abc}\mathcal{F}_{bc}\,,

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\omega^{a\bar{b}}\mathcal{F}_{a\bar{b}}\,.

In
[
15
,
\lx@sectionsign
9.3]
, we coined such worldsheets corresponding to the classical trajectories of 2d gauged sigma models, as BPS worldsheets. We shall do the same here.

BPS Worldsheets with Boundaries Corresponding to
\theta
-deformed DT Configurations on
CY_{3}

The boundaries of the BPS worldsheets are traced out by the endpoints of the
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-solitons as they propagate in
\tau
.
As we have seen at the end of

§

9.2
, at
\theta=0
, these endpoints correspond to DT configurations on
CY_{3}
.
If there are ‘
k
’ such configurations
\{\mathcal{E}^{1}_{\text{DT}}(0),\mathcal{E}^{2}_{\text{DT}}(0),\dots,\mathcal{E}^{k}_{\text{DT}}(0)\}
, we can further specify the undeformed
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{0}
-solitons at
\tau=\pm\infty
as
\gamma^{IJ}_{\pm}(t,0,\mathfrak{A}_{6})
, where
I,J\in\{1,\dots,k\}
indicates that its left and right endpoints, given by
\gamma^{I}(-\infty,0,\mathfrak{A}_{6})
and
\gamma^{J}(+\infty,0,\mathfrak{A}_{6})
, would correspond to the configurations
\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{DT}}(0)
and
\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{DT}}(0)
, respectively.
As the physical theory is symmetric under a variation of
\theta
, this would be true at any value of
\theta
.
In other words, we can also further specify any
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-soliton at
\tau=\pm\infty
as
\gamma^{IJ}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{6})
, where its left and right endpoints, given by
\gamma^{I}(-\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{6})
and
\gamma^{J}(+\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{6})
, would correspond to
\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{DT}}(\theta)
and
\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{DT}}(\theta)
, respectively, with the
\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{DT}}(\theta)
’s being
k
number of
\theta
-deformed DT configurations on
CY_{3}
.

Since the
\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{DT}}(\theta)
’s are
\tau
-independent and therefore, have the same values for all
\tau
, we have BPS worldsheets of the kind shown in
Fig. 5
.

\gamma^{IJ}_{+}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{6})
\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{DT}}(\theta)
\gamma^{IJ}_{-}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{6})
\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{DT}}(\theta)
t
\tau

Figure 5
:
BPS worldsheet with solitons
\gamma^{IJ}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{6})
and boundaries corresponding to
\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{DT}}(\theta)
and
\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{DT}}(\theta)
.

The 2d Model on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
and an Open String Theory in
\mathfrak{A}_{6}

Hence, one can understand the 2d gauged sigma model on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
with target space
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
to define an open string theory in
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
, with
effective
worldsheet and boundaries shown in
Fig. 5
, where
\tau
and
t
are the temporal and spatial directions, respectively.

9.4
Soliton String Theory, the
\text{Spin}(7)
Partition Function, and an FS Type
A_{\infty}
-category of DT Configurations on
CY_{3}

The 2d Model as a Gauged LG Model

Notice that we can also express the action of the 2d gauged sigma model with target space
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
in (
9.8
) as

\displaystyle S_{\text{LG},\mathfrak{A}_{6}}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int\differential{t}\differential{\tau}\left(\left|D_{\tau}\mathcal{A}^{a}+iD_{t}\mathcal{A}^{a}+\frac{ie^{i\theta}}{4}\varepsilon^{abc}\mathcal{F}_{bc}\right|^{2}+\left|F_{\tau t}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)

(9.25)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int\differential{t}\differential{\tau}\left(\left|D_{\tau}\mathcal{A}^{a}+iD_{t}\mathcal{A}^{a}-g^{a\bar{b}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{6}}\left(\frac{i\zeta}{2}\partialderivative{W_6}{\mathcal{A}^b}\right)^{*}\right|^{2}+\left|F_{\tau t}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int\differential{t}\differential{\tau}\left(\left|D_{\sigma}\mathcal{A}^{a}\right|^{2}+\left|\partialderivative{W_6}{\mathcal{A}^a}\right|^{2}+\left|F_{\tau t}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,,

where
\sigma
is the index on the worldsheet, and
\zeta=e^{-i\theta}
.
In other words, the 2d gauged sigma model with target space
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
can also be interpreted as a 2d gauged LG model in
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
with holomorphic superpotential
W_{6}(\mathcal{A})
.
Noting that the gradient vector field of
W_{6}(\mathcal{A})
is the
\theta
-independent part of
u^{a}
from (
9.9
), i.e.,
\mathcal{F}
, we find that the holomorphic superpotential
W_{6}(\mathcal{A})
must therefore be
CS(\mathcal{A})
, a
Chern-Simons function of
\mathcal{A}
.

By setting
d_{\tau}\mathcal{A}^{a}=0
and
A_{t},A_{\tau}\rightarrow 0
in the expression within the squared terms in (
9.25
), we can read off the LG
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-soliton equations corresponding to
\gamma^{IJ}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{6})
(that re-expresses (
9.21
)) as

\derivative{\mathcal{A}^a}{t}=-ig^{a\bar{b}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{6}}\left(\frac{i\zeta}{2}\partialderivative{W_6}{\mathcal{A}^{b}}\right)^{*}\,.

(9.26)

By setting
d_{t}\mathcal{A}^{a}=0
in (
9.26
), we get the LG
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-soliton endpoint equations corresponding to
\gamma^{IJ}(\pm\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{6})
(that re-expresses (
9.22
)) as

g^{a\bar{b}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{6}}\left(\frac{i\zeta}{2}\partialderivative{W_6}{\mathcal{A}^{b}}\right)^{*}=0\,.

(9.27)

Recall from the end of

§

9.2
that we are only considering certain
CY_{3}
such that the endpoints
\gamma^{IJ}(\pm\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{6})
are isolated and non-degenerate.
Therefore, from their definition in (
9.27
) which tells us that they are critical points of
W_{6}(\mathcal{A})
, we conclude that
W_{6}(\mathcal{A})
can be regarded as a holomorphic Morse function in
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
.

A consequence of being able to write the 2d model as a 2d gauged LG model with holomorphic superpotential
W_{6}
, is that it is known that such LG solitons map to straight line segments in the complex
W_{6}
-plane.
Specifically, an LG
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-soliton defined in (
9.26
) maps to a straight line segment
[W_{6}^{I}(\theta),W_{6}^{J}(\theta)]
in the complex
W_{6}
-plane that starts and ends at the critical values
W_{6}^{I}(\theta)\coloneq W_{6}(\gamma^{I}(-\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{6}))
and
W_{6}^{J}(\theta)\coloneq W_{6}(\gamma^{J}(+\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{6}))
, respectively, where its slope depends on
\theta
(via
\zeta
).
This fact will be useful shortly.
We shall also assume that
\text{Re}(W_{6}^{I}(\theta))<\text{Re}(W_{6}^{J}(\theta))
.

The Gauged LG Model as an LG SQM

Last but not least, after suitable rescalings, we can recast (
9.25
) as a 1d LG SQM (that re-expresses (
9.10
)), where its action will be given by
42
42

42

Just as in
footnote 39
, we have integrated out
A_{\tau}
and omitted the term containing
A_{t}
in the resulting SQM.

S_{\text{LG SQM},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{6})}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int\differential{\tau}\left(\left|\partial_{\tau}\mathcal{A}^{\alpha}+g^{\alpha\beta}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{6})}\partialderivative{H_6}{\mathcal{A}^\beta}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,,

(9.28)

where
H_{6}(\mathcal{A})
is the
real-valued
potential in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{6})
.

The LG SQM will localize onto configurations that
simultaneously
set to zero the LHS and RHS of the expression within the squared term in (
9.28
).
In other words, it will localize onto
\tau
-invariant critical points of
H_{6}(\mathcal{A})
that will correspond to the LG
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-solitons defined by (
9.26
).
For our choice of
CY_{3}
, the LG
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-solitons, just like their endpoints, will be isolated and non-degenerate.
Thus,
H_{6}(\mathcal{A})
can be regarded as a real-valued Morse functional in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{6})
.

Morphisms from
\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{DT}}(\theta)
to
\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{DT}}(\theta)
as Floer Homology Classes of Intersecting Thimbles

Note that we can also describe an LG
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-soliton in terms of the intersection of thimbles, as was done in
[
15
,
\lx@sectionsign
9.4]
.
One can understand such thimbles as submanifolds of a certain fiber space over the complex
W_{6}
-plane.
Solutions satisfying

\lim_{t\rightarrow-\infty}\gamma_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{6})=\gamma^{I}(-\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{6})

(9.29)

are known as left thimbles, and those satisfying

\lim_{t\rightarrow+\infty}\gamma_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{6})=\gamma^{J}(+\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{6})

(9.30)

are known as right thimbles.
Such a description makes it clear that they correspond, respectively, to the left and right endpoints of an LG
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-soliton solution
\gamma^{IJ}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{6})
.

Clearly, an LG
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-soliton solution, which would correspond to
\gamma^{IJ}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{6})
, must simultaneously be in a left and right thimble.
Thus, it can be represented as a transversal intersection of the left and right thimble in the fiber space over the line segment
[W^{I}_{6}(\theta),W^{J}_{6}(\theta)]
.
43
43

43

This intersection is guaranteed at some
\theta
, for which we can freely tune as the physical theory is symmetric under its variation.

Denoting such intersections as
S^{IJ}_{\text{DT}}
, each LG
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-soliton pair
\gamma^{IJ}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{6})
, whose left and right endpoints correspond to
\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{DT}}(\theta)
and
\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{DT}}(\theta)
on a BPS worldsheet as shown in
Fig. 5
, will correspond to a pair of intersection points
p^{IJ}_{\text{DT},\pm}(\theta)\in S^{IJ}_{\text{DT}}
.

Hence, as in earlier sections, the LG SQM in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{6})
with action (
9.28
) will physically realize a Floer homology that we shall name an
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
-LG Floer homology.
The chains of the
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
-LG Floer complex will be generated by LG
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-solitons which we can identify with
p_{\text{DT},\pm}^{**}(\theta)
, and the
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
-LG Floer differential will be realized by the flow lines governed by the gradient flow equation satisfied by
\tau
-varying configurations which set the expression within the squared term in (
9.28
) to zero.
In particular, the SQM partition function of the LG SQM in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{6})
will be given by
44
44

44

The ‘
\theta
’ label is omitted in the LHS of the following expression, as the physical theory is actually equivalent for all values of
\theta
.

\mathcal{Z}_{\text{LG SQM},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{6})}(G)=\sum_{I\neq J=1}^{k}\sum_{p^{IJ}_{\text{DT},\pm}\in S^{IJ}_{\text{DT}}}\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{p}}(p_{\text{DT},\pm}^{IJ}(\theta))\,,

(9.31)

where the contribution
\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{q}}(p_{\text{DT},\pm}^{IJ}(\theta))
can be identified with a homology class in an
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
-LG Floer homology generated by intersection points of thimbles.
These intersection points represent LG
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-solitons whose endpoints correspond to
\theta
-deformed DT configurations on
CY_{3}
.
The degree of each chain in the complex is
d_{p}
, and is counted by the number of outgoing flow lines from the fixed critical points of
H_{6}(\mathcal{A})
in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{6})
which can also be identified as
p_{\text{DT},\pm}^{IJ}(\theta)
.

Therefore,
\mathcal{Z}_{\text{LG SQM},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{6})}(G)
in (
9.31
) is a sum of LG
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-solitons defined by (
9.26
) with endpoints (
9.27
), or equivalently,
\gamma^{IJ}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{6})
-solitons defined by (
9.21
) with endpoints (
9.22
), whose start and end correspond to
\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{DT}}(\theta)
and
\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{DT}}(\theta)
, respectively.
In other words, we can write

\text{CF}_{\mathfrak{A}_{6}}\left(\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{DT}}(\theta),\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{DT}}(\theta)\right)_{\pm}=\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{p}}(p_{\text{DT},\pm}^{IJ}(\theta))\,,

(9.32)

where
\text{CF}_{\mathfrak{A}_{6}}(\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{DT}}(\theta),\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{DT}}(\theta))_{\pm}
is a vector representing a
\gamma^{IJ}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{6})
-soliton, such that
\text{Re}(W^{I}_{6}(\theta))<\text{Re}(W^{J}_{6}(\theta))
.

Recall that a soliton can be regarded as a morphism between its endpoints.
Specifically, the pair of
\gamma^{IJ}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{6})
-solitons can be regarded as a pair of morphisms
\text{Hom}(\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{DT}}(\theta),\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{DT}}(\theta))_{\pm}
from
\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{DT}}(\theta)
to
\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{DT}}(\theta)
.
Thus, we have the following one-to-one identification
45
45

45

The ‘
\theta
’ label is once again omitted in the following expression, as the physical theory is actually equivalent for all values of
\theta
.

\boxed{\text{Hom}(\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{DT}},\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{DT}})_{\pm}\Longleftrightarrow\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{p}}(p_{\text{DT},\pm}^{IJ})}

(9.33)

where the RHS is proportional to the identity class when
I=J
, and zero when
I\leftrightarrow J
(since the
\gamma^{IJ}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{6})
-soliton only moves in one direction from
\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{DT}}(\theta)
to
\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{DT}}(\theta)
as depicted in
Fig. 5
).

Soliton String Theory from the 2d LG Model

Just like the 2d gauged sigma model, the equivalent 2d gauged LG model will define an open string theory in
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
with effective worldsheets and boundaries shown in
Fig. 5
, where
\tau
and
t
are the temporal and spatial directions, respectively.

The dynamics of this open string theory in
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
will be governed by the BPS worldsheet equations determined by setting to zero the expression within the squared term in (
9.25
), where
\mathcal{A}^{a}
are scalars on the worldsheet corresponding to the holomorphic coordinates on
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
.
At an arbitrary instant in time whence
d_{\tau}\mathcal{A}^{a}=0
therein, the dynamics of
\mathcal{A}^{a}
along
t
will be governed by the soliton equation

\derivative{\mathcal{A}^a}{t}=-[A_{t}-iA_{\tau},\mathcal{A}^{a}]-ig^{a\bar{b}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{6}}\left(\frac{i\zeta}{2}\partialderivative{W_6}{\mathcal{A}^{b}}\right)^{*}\,.

(9.34)

Hence, just as a topological A-model can be interpreted as an instanton string theory whose corresponding dynamics of the
{\mathcal{A}}^{a}
fields along the spatial
t
-direction will be governed by the instanton equation
d{\mathcal{A}^{a}}/dt=0
, our LG model can be interpreted as a
soliton
string theory.

The Normalized Spin
(7)
Partition Function, LG
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-soliton String Scattering, and Maps of an
A_{\infty}
-structure

The spectrum of Spin
(7)
theory is given by the
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology of operators.
In particular, its normalized 8d partition function will be a sum over the free-field correlation functions of these operators.
46
46

46

Recall from
footnote 5
that Spin
(7)
theory is a balanced TQFT, whence the (normalized) 8d Spin
(7)
partition function can be computed by bringing down interaction terms to absorb fermion pair zero-modes in the path integral measure.
These interaction terms can be regarded as operators of the free-field theory that are necessarily in the
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology (since the non-vanishing partition function ought to remain
\mathcal{Q}
-invariant), where their contribution to the partition function can be understood as free-field correlation functions.

As our Spin
(7)
theory is semi-classical, these correlation functions will correspond to tree-level scattering only.
From the equivalent LG SQM and 2d gauged LG model perspective, the
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology will be spanned by the LG
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-soliton strings defined by (
9.26
).
In turn, this means that the normalized Spin
(7)
partition function can also be regarded as a sum over tree-level scattering amplitudes of these LG
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-soliton strings.
The BPS worldsheet underlying such a tree-level scattering amplitude is shown in
Fig. 6
.
47
47

47

Here, we have exploited the topological and hence conformal invariance of the soliton string theory to replace the outgoing LG
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-soliton strings with their vertex operators on the disc, then used their coordinate-independent operator products to reduce them to a single vertex operator, before finally translating it back as a single outgoing LG
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
-soliton string.

\mathcal{E}^{I_{n_{k}+1}}_{\text{DT}}
+
\mathcal{E}^{I_{1}}_{\text{DT}}
\mathcal{E}^{I_{1}}_{\text{DT}}
-
\mathcal{E}^{I_{2}}_{\text{DT}}
\mathcal{E}^{I_{2}}_{\text{DT}}
-
\mathcal{E}^{I_{3}}_{\text{DT}}
\mathcal{E}^{I_{n_{k}}}_{\text{DT}}
-
\mathcal{E}^{I_{n_{k}+1}}_{\text{DT}}

Figure 6
:
Tree-level scattering BPS worldsheet of incoming (
-
) and outgoing (
+
) LG
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-soliton strings.

In other words, we can express the normalized Spin
(7)
partition function as

\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{\text{Spin}(7),CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}(G)=\sum_{n_{k}}\mu^{n_{k}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{6}},\qquad n_{k}\in\{1,2,\dots,k-1\}

(9.35)

where each

\boxed{\mu^{n_{k}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{6}}:\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n_{k}}\text{Hom}\left(\mathcal{E}^{I_{i}}_{\text{DT}},\mathcal{E}^{I_{i+1}}_{\text{DT}}\right)_{-}\longrightarrow\text{Hom}\left(\mathcal{E}^{I_{1}}_{\text{DT}},\mathcal{E}^{I_{n_{k}+1}}_{\text{DT}}\right)_{+}}

(9.36)

is a scattering amplitude of
n_{k}
incoming LG
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-soliton strings
\text{Hom}(\mathcal{E}^{I_{1}}_{\text{DT}},\mathcal{E}^{I_{2}}_{\text{DT}})_{-},\dots,\text{Hom}(\mathcal{E}^{I_{n_{k}}}_{\text{DT}},\mathcal{E}^{I_{n_{k}+1}}_{\text{DT}})_{-}
and a single outgoing LG
\mathfrak{A}^{\theta}_{6}
-soliton string
\text{Hom}(\mathcal{E}^{I_{1}}_{\text{DT}},\mathcal{E}^{I_{n_{k}+1}}_{\text{DT}})_{+}
with left and right boundaries as labeled, whose underlying worldsheet shown in
Fig. 6
can be regarded as a disc with
n_{k}+1
vertex operators at the boundary.
That is,
\mu^{n_{k}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{6}}
counts pseudoholomorphic discs with
n_{k}+1
punctures at the boundary that are mapped to
\mathfrak{A}_{6}
according to the BPS worldsheet equations (
9.24
).

In turn, this means that
\mu^{n_{k}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{6}}
counts the moduli of solutions to (
9.4
) (or equivalently (
9.3
)) with
n_{k}+1
boundary conditions that can be described as follows.
First, note that we can regard
\mathbb{R}^{2}
as the effective worldsheet in
Fig. 6
that we shall denote as
\Omega
, so the Spin
(7)
-manifold can be interpreted as a trivial
CY_{3}
fibration over
\Omega
.
Then, at the
n_{k}+1

\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{\theta}
-soliton strings on
\Omega
where
\tau=\pm\infty
, (
9.4
) will become (
9.16
) and (
9.17
) with
A_{t},A_{\tau}\rightarrow 0
, and over the
\mathfrak{A}^{\theta}_{6}
-soliton string boundaries on
\Omega
where
t=\pm\infty
, (
9.4
) will become (
9.19
) which defines
\theta
-deformed DT configurations on
CY_{3}
.

Note at this point that the collection of
\mu^{n_{k}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{6}}
maps in (
9.36
) can be regarded as composition maps defining an
A_{\infty}
-structure.

An FS Type
A_{\infty}
-category of DT Configurations on
CY_{3}

Altogether, this means that the normalized partition function of Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
as expressed in (
9.35
), manifests a
novel
FS type
A_{\infty}
-category defined by the
\mu^{n_{k}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{6}}
maps (

) and the one-to-one identification (
9.33
), where the
k
objects
\{\mathcal{E}^{1}_{\text{DT}},\mathcal{E}^{2}_{\text{DT}},\dots,\mathcal{E}^{k}_{\text{DT}}\}
correspond to (
\theta
-deformed) DT configurations on
CY_{3}
(with the scalar being zero)!

A Physical Proof and Generalization of Haydys’ Mathematical Conjecture

Note that the existence of an FS type
A_{\infty}
-category of holomorphic vector bundles on
CY_{3}
(i.e., DT configurations on
CY_{3}
with the scalar being zero) was conjectured by Haydys as an extension of his program of constructing an FS type
A_{\infty}
-category of three-manifolds in
[
20
]
to higher dimensions.
Thus, we have furnished a purely physical proof and generalization (when
\theta\neq 0
) of Haydys’ mathematical conjecture.

9.5
An Atiyah-Floer Type Correspondence for the FS Type
A_{\infty}
-category of DT Configurations on
CY_{3}
, and a Hom-category

Intersecting Thimbles as Intersecting Branes

Notice that the setting of this section, i.e., Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, is the same as that in

§

8.1
with
M_{1}=\mathbb{R}
, where we performed a Tyurin degeneration of
CY_{3}
along a
CY_{2}
surface.
By the same arguments that made use of (
7.7
) to lead us to (
8.3
), we can make use of (
7.12
) to get

\sum_{j}\text{HF}^{\text{Spin}(7)\text{-inst},\theta}_{d_{j}}(CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R},G)=\sum_{s}\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{d_{s}}\left(\mathcal{M}\left(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}\right),\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1}\right)\,,

(9.37)

where “
\text{Spin}(7)\text{-inst},\theta
” in the superscript of the LHS refers to the fact that it is a
\theta
-generalized Spin
(7)
instanton Floer homology class “assigned to”
CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}
, derived from Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
with the
\mathbb{R}^{2}
plane rotated by an angle
\theta
, as per our formulation in this section thus far.
This is simply a
\theta
-generalization of (
3.11
), i.e., we can write (
9.37
) as

\mathcal{Z}_{\text{Spin}(7)^{\theta},CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}(G)=\sum_{s}\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{d_{s}}\left(\mathcal{M}\left(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}\right),\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1}\right)\,.

(9.38)

In turn, from (
9.31
), this can be written as

\sum_{I\neq J=1}^{k}\sum_{p^{IJ}_{\text{DT},\pm}\in S^{IJ}_{\text{DT}}}\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{p}}(p_{\text{DT},\pm}^{IJ}(\theta))=\sum_{s}\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{d_{s}}\left(\mathcal{M}\left(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}\right),\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1}\right)\,,

(9.39)

which implies that

\text{HF}^{G}_{*}(p_{\text{DT},\pm}^{IJ}(\theta))\cong\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{*}\left(\mathcal{M}\left(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}\right),\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1}\right)\,.

(9.40)

Thus, we have a correspondence between a gauge-theoretic Floer homology generated by intersecting
thimbles
and a symplectic intersection Floer homology generated by intersecting
branes
!

An Atiyah-Floer Type Correspondence for the FS Type
A_{\infty}
-category of DT Configurations on
CY_{3}

Moreover, via (
9.33
), we would have the following one-to-one identification
48
48

48

We have restored the ‘
\theta
’ label in the following expression, as both sides of the correspondence depend on the choice of
\theta
used to rotate the
\mathbb{R}^{2}
plane.

\boxed{\text{Hom}\left(\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{DT}}(\theta),\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{DT}}(\theta)\right)_{\pm}\Longleftrightarrow\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{*}\left(\mathcal{M}\left(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}\right),\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1}\right)}

(9.41)

This means that we now have an FS type
A_{\infty}
-category defined by the
\mu^{n_{k}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{6}}
composition maps (
9.36
) and the one-to-one identification (
9.41
).
The
k
objects
\{\mathcal{E}^{1}_{\text{DT}}(\theta),\mathcal{E}^{2}_{\text{DT}}(\theta),\dots,\mathcal{E}^{k}_{\text{DT}}(\theta)\}
corresponding to
\theta
-deformed DT configurations on
CY_{3}
, are now related to intersecting A
θ
-branes
\mathcal{P}_{*}(\theta)
in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})
.

In other words, we have a
novel
Atiyah-Floer type correspondence for the FS type
A_{\infty}
-category of DT configurations on
CY_{3}
!

The Soliton as a Hom-category

At
\theta=0,\pi
,
\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{DT}}(\theta)
, corresponding to
\theta
-deformed DT configurations on
CY_{3}
, are regular DT configurations on
CY_{3}
.
Such configurations, according to

§

4
, will generate a holomorphic
G_{2}

instanton
Floer homology of
CY_{3}
.
Therefore, via the 7d-Spin
(7)
Atiyah-Floer duality of
CY_{3}
in (
8.8
), we can identify
\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{DT}}(0)
and
\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{DT}}(\pi)
with a class in a symplectic intersection Floer homology generated by intersecting isotropic-coisotropic branes
\mathcal{L}^{I}_{0}
and
\mathcal{L}^{I}_{1}
in
L\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
, i.e.,

\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{DT}}(0)\Longleftrightarrow\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{*}\left(L\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}},\mathcal{L}^{I}_{0},\mathcal{L}^{I}_{1}\right)\Longleftrightarrow\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{DT}}(\pi)\,.

(9.42)

Then, this means that for general
\theta
,
\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{DT}}(\theta)
can be identified with a class in a symplectic intersection Floer homology generated by
\theta
-deformed isotropic-coisotropic branes
\mathcal{L}^{I}_{0}(\theta)
and
\mathcal{L}^{I}_{1}(\theta)
in
L\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2},\theta}_{\text{inst}}
, i.e.,

\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{DT}}(\theta)\Longleftrightarrow\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{*}\left(L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}},\mathcal{L}^{I}_{0}(\theta),\mathcal{L}^{I}_{1}(\theta)\right)\,.

(9.43)

However, notice that the classes on the RHS of (
9.43
) correspond to open string states of the 2d A
θ
-model with branes
\mathcal{L}^{I}_{0}(\theta)
and
\mathcal{L}^{I}_{1}(\theta)
, whence we can interpret them as
\text{Hom}[\mathcal{L}^{I}_{0}(\theta),\mathcal{L}^{I}_{1}(\theta)]
, i.e.,

\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{*}\left(L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}},\mathcal{L}^{I}_{0}(\theta),\mathcal{L}^{I}_{1}(\theta)\right)\Longleftrightarrow\text{Hom}\left[\mathcal{L}^{I}_{0}(\theta),\mathcal{L}^{I}_{1}(\theta)\right]\,.

(9.44)

This must mean that the LHS of (
9.41
) can be identified as

\boxed{\text{Hom}\left(\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{DT}}(\theta),\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{DT}}(\theta)\right)_{\pm}\Longleftrightarrow\text{Hom}\left(\text{Hom}\left[\mathcal{L}^{I}_{0}(\theta),\mathcal{L}^{I}_{1}(\theta)\right],\text{Hom}\left[\mathcal{L}^{J}_{0}(\theta),\mathcal{L}^{J}_{1}(\theta)\right]\right)_{\pm}}

(9.45)

In other words, the morphisms defining an FS type
A_{\infty}
-category of
\theta
-deformed DT configurations on
CY_{3}
can be identified as a Hom-category with objects themselves being morphisms between isotropic-coisotropic branes in
L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
.

Intersecting Branes as a Hom-category

Finally, by applying (
9.45
) to (
9.41
), we would have the one-to-one identification

\boxed{\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{*}\left(\mathcal{M}\left(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}\right),\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1}\right)\Longleftrightarrow\text{Hom}\left(\text{Hom}\left[\mathcal{L}^{I}_{0}(\theta),\mathcal{L}^{I}_{1}(\theta)\right],\text{Hom}\left[\mathcal{L}^{J}_{0}(\theta),\mathcal{L}^{J}_{1}(\theta)\right]\right)_{\pm}}

(9.46)

between a symplectic intersection Floer homology of intersecting branes and a Hom-category!

This identification is indeed a consistent one as follows.
Recall that the LHS of (
9.46
) actually corresponds to open three-brane states of a 4d sigma model on
I\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
with target space
\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
.
In turn, these open three-brane states can be understood as morphisms between the open string states of two 2d sigma models on
I\times\mathbb{R}
with target space
L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
and branes
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{0}(\theta)
and
\mathcal{L}^{*}_{1}(\theta)
.
From

§

8.3
and the generalization to general
\theta
above, such open string states of 2d sigma models are given by symplectic intersection Floer homology classes generated by intersections of isotropic-coisotropic branes of
L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
.

In other words, we would have

\begin{gathered}\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{*}\left(\mathcal{M}\left(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}\right),\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1}\right)\\
\cong\text{Hom}\left(\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{*}\left(L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}},\mathcal{L}^{M}_{0}(\theta),\mathcal{L}^{M}_{1}(\theta)\right),\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{*}\left(L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}},\mathcal{L}^{N}_{0}(\theta),\mathcal{L}^{N}_{1}(\theta)\right)\right)\,.\end{gathered}

(9.47)

Then, via (
9.44
), the bottom line of (
9.47
) will become the RHS of (
9.46
), thus concluding the consistency check.

A Fukaya-Seidel Type
A_{\infty}
-category of Five-Manifolds

In this section, we will specialize to the case where
CY_{3}=CY_{2}\times S^{1}\times S^{1}
, and perform a KK dimensional reduction of Spin
(7)
theory by shrinking one of the
S^{1}
circles to be infinitesimally small.
Recasting the resulting 7d-Spin
(7)
theory as either a 2d gauged LG model on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
or a 1d LG SQM in path space, we will, via the 7d-Spin
(7)
partition function, physically realize a novel FS type
A_{\infty}
-category of
CY_{2}\times S^{1}
whose objects correspond to HW configurations on
CY_{2}\times S^{1}
.

10.1
7d-Spin

\displaystyle F_{\tau t}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\omega^{p\bar{q}}\left(\partial_{y}\mathcal{B}_{p\bar{q}}-2\mathcal{F}_{p\bar{q}}+[\Gamma,\mathcal{B}_{p\bar{q}}]\right)\,,

(10.1)

\displaystyle(D_{\tau}\mathcal{A}_{p}-\partial_{p}A_{\tau})+i(D_{t}\mathcal{A}_{p}-\partial_{p}A_{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\varepsilon_{pq}\left(\mathcal{D}^{q}\Gamma-\mathcal{D}_{\bar{s}}\mathcal{B}^{\bar{s}q}-\partial_{y}\mathcal{A}^{q}\right)\,,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\omega^{p\bar{q}}D_{\tau}\mathcal{B}_{p\bar{q}}+D_{t}\Gamma-\partial_{y}A_{t}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\text{Im}(\varepsilon_{pq}\mathcal{F}^{pq})\,,

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\text{Re}(\varepsilon_{pq}\mathcal{F}^{pq})\,.

\displaystyle F_{\tau t}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\omega^{p\bar{q}}\left(\partial_{y}\mathcal{B}_{p\bar{q}}-2\mathcal{F}_{p\bar{q}}+[\Gamma,\mathcal{B}_{p\bar{q}}]\right)\,,

\displaystyle(D_{\tau}\mathcal{A}_{p}-\partial_{p}A_{\tau})+i(D_{t}\mathcal{A}_{p}-\partial_{p}A_{t})

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\omega^{p\bar{q}}D_{\tau}\mathcal{B}_{p\bar{q}}+D_{t}\Gamma-\partial_{y}A_{t}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\text{Im}(e^{i\theta}\varepsilon_{pq}\mathcal{F}^{pq})\,,

\displaystyle D_{\tau}\Gamma-\frac{1}{2}\omega^{p\bar{q}}D_{t}\mathcal{B}_{p\bar{q}}-\partial_{y}A_{\tau}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\text{Re}(e^{i\theta}\varepsilon_{pq}\mathcal{F}^{pq})\,.

\displaystyle S_{\text{7d-Spin}(7),CY_{2}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\differential{t}\differential{\tau}\int_{CY_{2}\times S^{1}}\differential{y}\absolutevalue{\dd{z}}^{4}\Tr\Bigg(

7d-Spin(7) Theory on
CY_{2}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
as a 2d Model

After suitable rescalings, we can recast (

) as a 2d model on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
, where its action now reads
50
50

50

Just as in
footnote 38
, we have (i) omitted terms with

corresponding to the scalar
\tilde{\kappa}
of (

.

\displaystyle S_{\text{2d},\mathfrak{A}_{5}}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\differential{t}\differential{\tau}\Bigg(

\displaystyle|F_{\tau t}|^{2}+|D_{\tau}\mathcal{A}^{P}+iD_{t}\mathcal{A}^{P}+v^{P}|^{2}+|D_{\tau}\mathcal{B}^{P}+D_{t}\Gamma^{P}+r^{P}|^{2}

(10.5)

\displaystyle+|D_{\tau}\Gamma^{P}-D_{t}\mathcal{B}^{P}+\tilde{r}^{P}|^{2}+\dots\Bigg)\,,

where
(\mathcal{A}^{P},\mathcal{B}^{P},\Gamma^{P})
and
P
are holomorphic coordinates and indices on the space
\mathfrak{A}_{5}
of irreducible
(\mathcal{A}_{p},\mathcal{B}_{p\bar{q}},\Gamma)

, and

will correspond to

in (

10.3
The 2d Model on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
and an Open String Theory in
\mathfrak{A}_{5}

By following the same analysis in

§

9.3
with (
10.5
) as the action for the 2d gauged sigma model on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
with target space
\mathfrak{A}_{5}
, we find that it will define an open string theory in
\mathfrak{A}_{5}
.
We will now work out the details pertaining to the BPS worldsheets and their boundaries that are necessary to define this open string theory.

BPS Worldsheets of the 2d Model

The BPS worldsheets of the 2d gauged sigma model with target space
\mathfrak{A}_{5}
correspond to its classical trajectories.
Specifically, these are defined by setting to zero the expression within the squared terms in (
10.5
), i.e.,

\begin{gathered}F_{\tau t}=0\,,\qquad\mathfrak{H}_{5}(\tilde{\kappa})=0\,,\\
\frac{D\mathcal{A}^{P}}{D\tau}+i\frac{D\mathcal{A}^{P}}{Dt}=-v^{P}\,,\qquad\frac{D\mathcal{B}^{P}}{D\tau}+\frac{D\Gamma^{P}}{Dt}=-r^{P}\,,\qquad\frac{D\Gamma^{P}}{D\tau}-\frac{D\mathcal{B}^{P}}{Dt}=-\tilde{r}^{P}\,.\end{gathered}

(10.13)

BPS Worldsheets with Boundaries Corresponding to
\theta
-deformed HW Configurations on
CY_{2}\times S^{1}

The boundaries of the BPS worldsheets are traced out by the endpoints of the
\mathfrak{A}_{5}^{\theta}
-solitons as they propagate in
\tau
.
As we have seen at the end of

§

10.2
, these endpoints correspond to
\theta
-deformed HW configurations on
CY_{2}\times S^{1}
.
If there are ‘
l
’ such configurations
\{\mathcal{E}^{1}_{\text{HW}}(\theta),\mathcal{E}^{2}_{\text{HW}}(\theta),\dots,\mathcal{E}^{l}_{\text{HW}}(\theta)\}
, just as in

§

9.3
, we can further specify any
\mathfrak{A}_{5}^{\theta}
-soliton at
\tau=\pm\infty
as
\gamma^{IJ}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{5})
,
54
54

54

Just as in
footnote 41
, the
\tau
-invariant
\mathfrak{A}_{5}^{\theta}
-solitons can be fixed at
\tau=\pm\infty
by adding physically inconsequential
\mathcal{Q}
-exact terms to the SQM action.

where its left and right endpoints would correspond to
\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{HW}}(\theta)
and
\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{HW}}(\theta)
, respectively.

Since the
\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{HW}}(\theta)
’s are
\tau
-independent and therefore, have the same values for all
\tau
, we will have BPS worldsheets of the kind similar to
Fig. 5
.
This time, however, instead of the boundaries being

\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{HW}}(\theta)
.
And, instead of the solitons at

The 2d Model on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
and an Open String Theory in

Thus, like in

10.4
Soliton String Theory, the 7d-Spin
(7)

The 2d Model as a Gauged LG Model

Notice that we can also express (
10.13
) as

\displaystyle F_{\tau t}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\mathfrak{H}_{5}(\tilde{\kappa})

\displaystyle=0\,,

(10.14)

\displaystyle\frac{D\mathcal{A}^{P}}{D\tau}+i\frac{D\mathcal{A}^{P}}{Dt}

\displaystyle=-v^{P}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\frac{D\mathcal{C}^{P}}{D\tau}+i\frac{D\mathcal{C}^{P}}{Dt}

\displaystyle=-(\tilde{r}^{P}+ir^{P})\,.

Here,
\mathcal{A}^{P}
and
\mathcal{C}^{P}=\Gamma^{P}+i\mathcal{B}^{P}
can be interpreted as holomorphic coordinates on the space
\mathscr{A}_{5}
of irreducible
(\mathcal{A}_{p},\mathcal{C})
fields on

, where

is a scalar on
CY_{2}\times S^{1}
valued
\text{ad}(G_{\mathbb{C}})
, where

is the complexification of
G
.
In turn, this means that we can express the action of the 2d gauged sigma model with target space
\mathfrak{A}_{5}
in (

) as

\displaystyle S_{\text{LG},\mathfrak{A}_{5}}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int\differential{t}\differential{\tau}\Bigg(

\displaystyle\left|D_{\tau}\mathcal{A}^{P}+iD_{t}\mathcal{A}^{P}+ig^{P\bar{Q}}_{\mathscr{A}_{5}}\left(\frac{i\zeta}{2}\partialderivative{W_5}{\mathcal{A}^Q}\right)^{*}\right|^{2}

(10.15)

\displaystyle+\left|D_{\tau}\mathcal{C}^{P}+iD_{t}\mathcal{C}^{P}+ig^{P\bar{Q}}_{\mathscr{A}_{5}}\left(\frac{i\zeta}{2}\partialderivative{W_5}{\mathcal{C}^Q}\right)^{*}\right|^{2}+\left|F_{\tau t}\right|^{2}+\dots\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int\differential{t}\differential{\tau}\Bigg(

\displaystyle\left|D_{\sigma}\mathcal{A}^{P}\right|^{2}+\left|D_{\sigma}\mathcal{C}^{P}\right|^{2}+\left|\partialderivative{W_5}{\mathcal{A}^P}\right|^{2}+\left|\partialderivative{W_5}{\mathcal{C}^P}\right|^{2}+\left|F_{\tau t}\right|^{2}+\dots\Bigg)\,,

where
g_{\mathscr{A}_{5}}
is the metric on
\mathscr{A}_{5}
.
In other words, the 2d gauged sigma model with target space
\mathfrak{A}_{5}
can also be interpreted as a 2d gauged LG model in
\mathscr{A}_{5}
with holomorphic superpotential
W_{5}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{C})
.

By setting
d_{\tau}\mathcal{A}^{P}=0=d_{\tau}\mathcal{C}^{P}
and
A_{t},A_{\tau}\rightarrow 0
in the expression within the squared terms in (
10.15
), we can read off the LG
\mathscr{A}_{5}^{\theta}
-soliton equations corresponding to
\gamma^{IJ}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{5})
(that re-expresses (
10.7
) with

) as

\derivative{\mathcal{A}^P}{t}=-g^{P\bar{Q}}_{\mathscr{A}_{5}}\left(\frac{i\zeta}{2}\partialderivative{W_5}{\mathcal{A}^Q}\right)^{*}\,,\qquad\derivative{\mathcal{C}^P}{t}=-g^{P\bar{Q}}_{\mathscr{A}_{5}}\left(\frac{i\zeta}{2}\partialderivative{W_5}{\mathcal{C}^Q}\right)^{*}\,.

(10.16)

By setting
d_{t}\mathcal{A}^{P}=0=d_{t}\mathcal{C}^{P}
in (

), we get the LG
\mathscr{A}_{5}^{\theta}
-soliton endpoint equations corresponding to
\gamma^{IJ}(\pm\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{5})
as

g^{P\bar{Q}}_{\mathscr{A}_{5}}\left(\frac{i\zeta}{2}\partialderivative{W_5}{\mathcal{A}^Q}\right)^{*}=0\,,\qquad g^{P\bar{Q}}_{\mathscr{A}_{5}}\left(\frac{i\zeta}{2}\partialderivative{W_5}{\mathcal{C}^Q}\right)^{*}=0\,.

(10.17)

Recall from the end of

§

10.2
that we are only considering certain
CY_{2}
such that the endpoints
\gamma^{IJ}(\pm\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{5})
are isolated and non-degenerate.
Therefore, from their definitions in (
10.17
) which tell us that they are critical points of
W_{5}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{C})
, we conclude that
W_{5}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{C})
can be regarded as a holomorphic Morse function in
\mathscr{A}_{5}
.

Just as in

§

9.4
, this means that an LG
\mathscr{A}_{5}^{\theta}
-soliton defined in (
10.16
) maps to a straight line segment
[W^{I}_{5}(\theta),W^{J}_{5}(\theta)]
in the complex
W_{5}
-plane that starts and ends at the critical values
W^{I}_{5}(\theta)\coloneq W_{5}\left(\gamma^{I}(-\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{5})\right)
and
W^{J}_{5}(\theta)\coloneq W_{5}\left(\gamma^{J}(+\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{5})\right)
, respectively, where its slope depends on
\theta
(via
\zeta
).
We shall also assume that
\text{Re}\left(W^{I}_{5}(\theta)\right)<\text{Re}\left(W^{J}_{5}(\theta)\right)
.

The Gauged LG Model as an LG SQM

With suitable rescalings, we can recast (

55

55

Just as in

Morphisms from
\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{HW}}(\theta)
to
\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{HW}}(\theta)
as Floer Homology Classes of Intersecting Thimbles

Repeating here the analysis in

§

9.4
with (
10.18
) as the action for the LG SQM, we find that we can interpret the LG
\mathscr{A}_{5}^{\theta}
-soliton solutions as intersections of thimbles.
Specifically, an LG
\mathscr{A}_{5}^{\theta}
-soliton pair (corresponding to an
\mathfrak{A}_{5}
-soliton pair
\gamma^{IJ}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{5})
), whose left and right endpoints correspond to
\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{HW}}(\theta)
and
\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{HW}}(\theta)
, respectively, can be identified as a pair of transversal intersection points
p^{IJ}_{\text{HW},\pm}(\theta)\in S^{IJ}_{\text{HW}}
of a left and right thimble in the fiber space over the line segment
[W^{I}_{5}(\theta),W^{J}_{5}(\theta)]
.

This means that the LG SQM in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathscr{A}_{5})
with action (
10.18
) will physically realize a Floer homology that we shall name an
\mathscr{A}_{5}
-LG Floer homology.
The chains of the
\mathscr{A}_{5}
-LG Floer complex will be generated by LG
\mathscr{A}_{5}^{\theta}
-solitons which we can identify with
p^{**}_{\text{HW},\pm}(\theta)
, and the
\mathscr{A}_{5}
-LG Floer differential will be realized by the flow lines governed by the gradient flow equations satisfied by
\tau
-varying configurations which set the expression within the squared terms in (
10.18
) to zero.
In particular, the SQM partition function of the LG SQM in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathscr{A}_{5})
will be given by
56
56

56

Just as in
footnote 44
, the ‘
\theta
’ label is omitted in the LHS of the following expression.

\mathcal{Z}_{\text{LG SQM},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathscr{A}_{5})}(G)=\sum_{I\neq J=1}^{l}\sum_{p^{IJ}_{\text{HW},\pm}\in S^{IJ}_{\text{HW}}}\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{q}}(p^{IJ}_{\text{HW},\pm}(\theta))\,,

(10.19)

where the contribution
\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{q}}(p^{IJ}_{\text{HW},\pm}(\theta))
can be identified with a homology class in an
\mathscr{A}_{5}
-LG Floer homology generated by intersection points of thimbles.
These intersection points represent LG
\mathscr{A}_{5}^{\theta}
-solitons whose endpoints correspond to
\theta
-deformed HW configurations on
CY_{2}\times S^{1}
.
The degree of each chain in the complex is
d_{q}
, and is counted by the number of outgoing flow lines from the fixed critical points of
H_{5}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{C})
in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathscr{A}_{5})
which can also be identified as
p^{IJ}_{\text{HW},\pm}(\theta)
.

Therefore,
\mathcal{Z}_{\text{LG SQM},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathscr{A}_{5})}(G)
in (
10.19
) is a sum of LG
\mathscr{A}_{5}^{\theta}
-solitons defined by (
10.16
) with endpoints (
10.17
), or equivalently,
\gamma^{IJ}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{5})
-solitons defined by (
10.9
) and (
10.10
) with endpoints (
10.11
), whose start and end correspond to
\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{HW}}(\theta)
and
\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{HW}}(\theta)
, respectively.
In other words, we can write

\text{CF}_{\mathfrak{A}_{5}}\left(\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{HW}}(\theta),\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{HW}}(\theta)\right)_{\pm}=\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{q}}(p^{IJ}_{\text{HW},\pm}(\theta))\,,

(10.20)

where

is a vector representing a
\gamma^{IJ}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{5})
-soliton, such that
\text{Re}\left(W^{I}_{5}(\theta)\right)<\text{Re}\left(W^{J}_{5}(\theta)\right)
.
This will lead us to the one-to-one identification
57
57

57

Just as in
footnote 45
, the ‘
\theta
’ label is omitted in the following expression.

\boxed{\text{Hom}(\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{HW}},\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{HW}})_{\pm}\Longleftrightarrow\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{q}}(p^{IJ}_{\text{HW},\pm})}

(10.21)

where the RHS is proportional to the identity class when

, and zero when

since the
\mathfrak{A}_{5}^{\theta}
-soliton only moves in one direction from

to

.

Soliton String Theory from the 2d LG Model

Just as in

§

9.4
, the 2d gauged LG model in
\mathscr{A}_{5}
with action (
10.15
) can be interpreted as a soliton string theory in
\mathscr{A}_{5}
.
The dynamics of this soliton string theory in
\mathscr{A}_{5}
will be governed by the BPS worldsheet equations determined by setting to zero the expression within the squared terms in (
10.15
), where
(\mathcal{A}^{P},\mathcal{C}^{P})
are scalars on the worldsheet corresponding to the holomorphic coordinates on
\mathscr{A}_{5}
.
At an arbitrary instant in time whence
d_{\tau}\mathcal{A}^{P}=0=d_{\tau}\mathcal{C}^{P}
therein, the dynamics of
(\mathcal{A}^{P},\mathcal{C}^{P})
along
t
will be governed by the soliton equations

\derivative{\mathcal{A}^P}{t}=-[A_{t}-iA_{\tau},\mathcal{A}^{P}]-g^{P\bar{Q}}_{\mathscr{A}_{5}}\left(\frac{i\zeta}{2}\partialderivative{W_5}{\mathcal{A}^Q}\right)^{*}\,,\quad\derivative{\mathcal{C}^P}{t}=-[A_{t}-iA_{\tau},\mathcal{C}^{P}]-g^{P\bar{Q}}_{\mathscr{A}_{5}}\left(\frac{i\zeta}{2}\partialderivative{W_5}{\mathcal{C}^Q}\right)^{*}\,.

(10.22)

The Normalized 7d-Spin
(7)
Partition Function, LG
\mathscr{A}_{5}^{\theta}
-soliton String Scattering, and Maps of an
A_{\infty}
-structure

Since our 7d-Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{2}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
is semi-classical, its normalized partition function can be regarded as a sum over tree-level scattering amplitudes of the LG
\mathscr{A}_{5}^{\theta}
-soliton strings defined by (
10.16
).
The BPS worldsheet underlying such a tree-level scattering amplitude is similar to
Fig. 6
, where instead of the endpoints of each string being
\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{DT}}
, it is now understood to be
\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{HW}}
.

In other words, we can, like in (
9.35
), express the normalized 7d-Spin
(7)
partition function as

\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{\text{7d-Spin}(7),CY_{2}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}(G)=\sum_{n_{l}}\mu^{n_{l}}_{\mathscr{A}_{5}},\qquad n_{l}\in\{1,2,\dots,l-1\}

(10.23)

where each

\boxed{\mu^{n_{l}}_{\mathscr{A}_{5}}:\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n_{l}}\text{Hom}\left(\mathcal{E}^{I_{i}}_{\text{HW}},\mathcal{E}^{I_{i+1}}_{\text{HW}}\right)_{-}\longrightarrow\text{Hom}\left(\mathcal{E}^{I_{1}}_{\text{HW}},\mathcal{E}^{I_{n_{l}+1}}_{\text{HW}}\right)_{+}}

(10.24)

is a scattering amplitude of
n_{l}
incoming LG
\mathscr{A}_{5}^{\theta}
-soliton strings
\text{Hom}(\mathcal{E}^{I_{1}}_{\text{HW}},\mathcal{E}^{I_{2}}_{\text{HW}})_{-},\dots,\text{Hom}(\mathcal{E}^{I_{n_{l}}}_{\text{HW}},\mathcal{E}^{I_{n_{l}+1}}_{\text{HW}})_{-}
and a single outgoing LG
\mathscr{A}_{5}^{\theta}
-soliton string
\text{Hom}(\mathcal{E}^{I_{1}}_{\text{HW}},\mathcal{E}^{I_{n_{l}+1}}_{\text{HW}})_{+}
with left and right boundaries as labeled, whose underlying worldsheet can be regarded as a disc with
n_{l}+1
vertex operators at the boundary.
That is,
\mu^{n_{l}}_{\mathscr{A}_{5}}
counts pseudoholomorphic discs with
n_{l}+1
punctures at the boundary that are mapped to
\mathscr{A}_{5}
according to the BPS worldsheet equations (
10.14
).

Just as in

§

9.4
, the collection of
\mu^{n_{l}}_{\mathscr{A}_{5}}
maps in (
10.24
) can be regarded as composition maps defining an
A_{\infty}
-structure.

An FS Type
A_{\infty}
-category of HW Configurations on
CY_{2}\times S^{1}

Altogether, this means that the normalized partition function of 7d-Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{2}\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
as expressed in (
10.23
), manifests a
novel
FS type
A_{\infty}
-category defined by the
\mu^{n_{l}}_{\mathscr{A}_{5}}
maps (
10.24
) and the one-to-one identification (
10.21
), where the
l
objects
\{\mathcal{E}^{1}_{\text{HW}},\mathcal{E}^{2}_{\text{HW}},\dots,\mathcal{E}^{l}_{\text{HW}}\}
correspond to (
\theta
-deformed) HW configurations on
CY_{2}\times S^{1}
(with two of the three linearly-independent components of the self-dual two-form field being zero)!

A Fukaya-Seidel Type
A_{\infty}
-category of Four-Manifolds

In this section, we will perform yet another KK dimensional reduction of 7d-Spin
(7)
theory by shrinking the remaining
S^{1}
circle to be infinitesimally small.
Recasting the resulting 6d-Spin
(7)
theory as either a 2d gauged LG model on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
or a 1d LG SQM in path space, we will, via the 6d-Spin
(7)
partition function, physically realize a novel FS type
A_{\infty}
-category whose objects correspond to Vafa-Witten (VW) configurations on
CY_{2}
.

11.1
6d-Spin
(7)
Theory on
CY_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
as a 2d Model on
\mathbb{R}^{2}
or SQM in Path Space

6d-Spin
(7)
Theory on
CY_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}

First, note that KK reduction of 7d-Spin
(7)

-direction means we have to set

.
The two scalar bosons of the resulting 6d-Spin
(7)

Second, using the
(z^{2},z^{3})

(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})
.
Here, the components of

,

\mathcal{B}_{\bar{2}\bar{3}}=\frac{1}{2}(\Gamma+iC)

The conditions that minimize the 6d-Spin
(7)

are obtained by performing a KK reduction along the remaining

circle of (
10.1
), i.e.,

\displaystyle F_{\tau t}

\displaystyle=\omega^{p\bar{q}}\left(\mathcal{F}_{p\bar{q}}-\frac{1}{4}(\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{B})_{p\bar{q}}\right)\,,

(11.1)

\displaystyle(D_{\tau}\mathcal{A}_{p}-\partial_{p}A_{\tau})+i(D_{t}\mathcal{A}_{p}-\partial_{p}A_{t})

\displaystyle=-\mathcal{D}^{q}\mathcal{B}_{qp}\,,

\displaystyle D_{\tau}\mathcal{B}_{pq}-iD_{t}\mathcal{B}_{pq}

\displaystyle=2\mathcal{F}_{pq}\,.

Third, noting that we are physically free to rotate
\mathbb{R}^{2}
about the origin by an angle
\theta

11.1
) becomes

\displaystyle F_{\tau t}

\displaystyle=\omega^{p\bar{q}}\left(\mathcal{F}_{p\bar{q}}-\frac{1}{4}(\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{B})_{p\bar{q}}\right)\,,

(11.2)

\displaystyle(D_{\tau}\mathcal{A}_{p}-\partial_{p}A_{\tau})+i(D_{t}\mathcal{A}_{p}-\partial_{p}A_{t})

\displaystyle=-e^{i\theta}\mathcal{D}^{q}\mathcal{B}_{qp}\,,

\displaystyle D_{\tau}\mathcal{B}_{pq}-iD_{t}\mathcal{B}_{pq}

\displaystyle=2e^{i\theta}\mathcal{F}_{pq}\,.

This allows us to write the action of 6d-Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
as

\displaystyle S_{\text{6d-Spin}(7),CY_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}=\frac{1}{4e^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{2}}\differential{t}\differential{\tau}\int_{CY_{2}}\absolutevalue{\dd{z}}^{4}\Tr\Big(
&\left| F_{\tau t} + \hat{\kappa} \right|^2
+ 8 \left| D_\tau\mathcal{A}_p
+ i D_t \mathcal{A}_p + w_p
\right|^2 \\
& + 4 \left| D_\tau\mathcal{B}_{pq}
- i D_t \mathcal{B}_{pq}
+ s
\right|^2
+ \dots\Big)\,,

(11.3)

where

\displaystyle\hat{\kappa}

\displaystyle=-\omega^{p\bar{q}}\left(\mathcal{F}_{p\bar{q}}-\frac{1}{4}(\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{B})_{p\bar{q}}\right)\,,

(11.4)

\displaystyle w_{p}

\displaystyle=-\partial_{p}A_{\tau}-i\partial_{p}A_{t}+e^{i\theta}\mathcal{D}^{q}\mathcal{B}_{qp}\,,

\displaystyle s

\displaystyle=-2e^{i\theta}\mathcal{F}_{pq}\,.

(11.10)

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\omega^{p\bar{q}}\left(\mathcal{F}_{p\bar{q}}-\frac{1}{4}(\mathcal{B}\times\mathcal{B})_{p\bar{q}}\right)\,.

6d-Spin
(7)

\displaystyle ie^{i\theta}\mathcal{D}^{q}\mathcal{B}_{qp}

,

with the scalar and one of the linearly-independent components of the self-dual two-form field being zero.

60
60

60

At

therein satisfies

such that these undeformed HW configurations on

We can then apply the same reasoning as

In short, from the equivalent 1d SQM of 6d-Spin

11.3
The 2d Model on
\mathbb{R}^{2}

BPS Worldsheets of the 2d Model

The BPS worldsheets of the 2d gauged sigma model with target space

\displaystyle F_{\tau t}

\displaystyle=0\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\mathfrak{H}_{4}(\hat{u})

\displaystyle=0\,,

(11.13)

\displaystyle\frac{D\mathcal{A}^{M}}{D\tau}+i\frac{D\mathcal{A}^{M}}{Dt}

\displaystyle=-w^{M}\,,

\displaystyle\qquad\frac{D\mathcal{B}^{M}}{D\tau}-i\frac{D\mathcal{B}^{M}}{Dt}

\displaystyle=-s^{M}\,.

If there are ‘

, we can further specify any

-soliton at

as
\gamma^{IJ}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
,
61
61

61

Just as in

, the
\tau
-invariant

by adding physically inconsequential

-exact terms to the SQM action.

where its left and right endpoints would correspond to

and
\mathcal{E}_{\text{VW}}^{J}(\theta)
, respectively.

Since the

11.4
Soliton String Theory, the 6d-Spin
(7)
Partition Function, and an FS Type
A_{\infty}
-category of VW Configurations on
