The 2d Model as a Gauged LG Model

Notice that we can also express (
11.5
) as

\displaystyle S_{\text{LG},\mathfrak{A}_{4}}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int\differential{t}\differential{\tau}\Bigg(

\displaystyle\left|D_{\tau}\mathcal{A}^{M}+iD_{t}\mathcal{A}^{M}+ig^{M\bar{N}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}\left(\frac{i\zeta}{2}\partialderivative{W_4}{\mathcal{A}^N}\right)^{*}\right|^{2}

(11.14)

\displaystyle+\left|D_{\tau}\mathcal{B}^{M}-iD_{t}\mathcal{B}^{M}+ig^{M\bar{N}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}\left(\frac{i\zeta}{2}\partialderivative{W_4}{\mathcal{B}^N}\right)^{*}\right|^{2}+\left|F_{\tau t}\right|^{2}+\dots\Bigg)

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int\differential{t}\differential{\tau}

\displaystyle\left(\left|D_{\sigma}\mathcal{A}^{M}\right|^{2}+\left|D_{\sigma}\mathcal{B}^{M}\right|^{2}+\left|\partialderivative{W_4}{\mathcal{A}^M}\right|^{2}+\left|\partialderivative{W_4}{\mathcal{B}^M}\right|^{2}+\left|F_{\tau t}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,.

In other words, the 2d gauged sigma model with target space
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
can also be interpreted as a 2d gauged LG model in
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
with holomorphic superpotential
W_{4}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})
.

By setting
d_{\tau}\mathcal{A}^{M}=0=d_{\tau}\mathcal{B}^{M}
and
A_{t},A_{\tau}\rightarrow 0
in the expression within the squared terms in (
11.14
), we can read off the LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-soliton equations corresponding to
\gamma^{IJ}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
(that re-expresses (
11.7
) with
A_{t},A_{\tau}\rightarrow 0
) as

\derivative{\mathcal{A}^M}{t}=-g^{M\bar{N}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}\left(\frac{i\zeta}{2}\partialderivative{W_4}{\mathcal{A}^N}\right)^{*}\,,\qquad\derivative{\mathcal{B}^M}{t}=g^{M\bar{N}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}\left(\frac{i\zeta}{2}\partialderivative{W_4}{\mathcal{B}^N}\right)^{*}\,.

(11.15)

By setting
d_{t}\mathcal{A}^{M}=0=d_{t}\mathcal{B}^{M}
in (
11.15
), we get the LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-soliton endpoint equations corresponding to
\gamma^{IJ}(\pm\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
as

g^{M\bar{N}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}\left(\frac{i\zeta}{2}\partialderivative{W_4}{\mathcal{A}^N}\right)^{*}=0\,,\qquad g^{M\bar{N}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}\left(\frac{i\zeta}{2}\partialderivative{W_4}{\mathcal{B}^N}\right)^{*}=0\,.

(11.16)

Recall from the end of

§

11.2
that we are only considering certain
CY_{2}
such that the endpoints
\gamma^{IJ}(\pm\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
are isolated and non-degenerate.
Therefore, from their definitions in (
11.16
) which tell us that they are critical points of
W_{4}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})
, we conclude that
W_{4}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})
can be regarded as a holomorphic Morse function in
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
.

Just as in

§

9.4
, this means that an LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-soliton defined in (
11.15
) maps to a straight line segment
[W^{I}_{4}(\theta),W^{J}_{4}(\theta)]
in the complex
W_{4}
-plane that starts and ends at the critical values
W^{I}_{4}(\theta)\coloneq W_{4}(\gamma^{I}(-\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4}))
and
W^{J}_{4}(\theta)\coloneq W_{4}(\gamma^{J}(+\infty,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4}))
, respectively, where its slope depends on
\theta
(via
\zeta
).
We shall also assume that
\text{Re}\left(W^{I}_{4}(\theta)\right)<\text{Re}\left(W^{J}_{4}(\theta)\right)
.

The Gauged LG Model as an LG SQM

With suitable rescalings, we can recast (
11.14
) as a 1d LG SQM (that re-expresses (
11.6
)), where its action will be given by
62
62

62

Just as in
footnote 39
, we have integrated out
A_{\tau}
and omitted the term containing
A_{t}
in the resulting SQM.

S_{\text{LG SQM},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}=\frac{1}{e^{2}}\int\differential{\tau}\left(\left|\partial_{\tau}\mathcal{A}^{\alpha}+g^{\alpha\beta}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}\partialderivative{H_4}{\mathcal{A}^\beta}\right|^{2}+\left|\partial_{\tau}\mathcal{B}^{\alpha}+g^{\alpha\beta}_{\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}\partialderivative{H_4}{\mathcal{B}^\beta}\right|^{2}+\dots\right)\,,

(11.17)

where
H_{4}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})
is the
real-valued
potential in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
.

The LG SQM will localize onto configurations that
simultaneously
set to zero the LHS and RHS of the expression within the squared terms in (
11.17
).
In other words, it will localize onto
\tau
-invariant critical points of
H_{4}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})
that correspond to the LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-solitons defined by (
11.15
).
For our choice of
CY_{2}
, the LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-solitons, just like their endpoints, will be isolated and non-degenerate.
Thus,
H_{4}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})
can be regarded as a real-valued Morse functional in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
.

Morphisms from
\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{VW}}(\theta)
to
\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{VW}}(\theta)
as Floer Homology Classes of Intersecting Thimbles

Repeating here the analysis in

§

9.4
with (
11.17
) as the action for the LG SQM, we find that we can interpret the LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-soliton solutions as intersections of thimbles.
Specifically, an LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-soliton pair
\gamma^{IJ}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
, whose left and right endpoints correspond to
\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{VW}}(\theta)
and
\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{VW}}(\theta)
, respectively, can be identified as a pair of transversal intersection points
p^{IJ}_{\text{VW},\pm}(\theta)\in S^{IJ}_{\text{VW}}
of a left and right thimble in the fiber space over the line segment
[W^{I}_{4}(\theta),W^{J}_{4}(\theta)]
.

This means that the LG SQM in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
with action (
11.17
) will physically realize a Floer homology that we shall name an
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
-LG Floer homology.
The chains of the
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
-LG Floer complex will be generated by LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-solitons which we can identify with
p^{**}_{\text{VW},\pm}(\theta)
, and the
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
-LG Floer differential will be realized by the flow lines governed by the gradient flow equations satisfied by
\tau
-varying configurations which set the expression within the squared terms in (
11.17
) to zero.
In particular, the SQM partition function of the LG SQM in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
will be given by
63
63

63

Just as in
footnote 44
, the ‘
\theta
’ label is omitted in the LHS of the following expression.

\mathcal{Z}_{\text{LG SQM},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}(G)=\sum_{I\neq J=1}^{m}\sum_{p^{IJ}_{VW,\pm}\in S^{IJ}_{\text{VW}}}\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{r}}(p^{IJ}_{\text{VW},\pm}(\theta))\,,

(11.18)

where the contribution
\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{r}}(p^{IJ}_{\text{VW},\pm}(\theta))
can be identified with a homology class in an
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
-LG Floer homology generated by intersection points of thimbles.
These intersection points represent LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-solitons whose endpoints correspond to
\theta
-deformed VW configurations on
CY_{2}
.
The degree of each chain in the complex is
d_{r}
, and is counted by the number of outgoing flow lines from the fixed critical points of
H_{4}(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{B})
in
\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{4})
which can also be identified as
p^{IJ}_{\text{VW},\pm}(\theta)
.

Therefore,
\mathcal{Z}_{\text{LG SQM},\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathfrak{A}_{4})}(G)
in (
11.18
) is a sum of LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-solitons defined by (
11.15
) with endpoints (
11.16
), or equivalently,
\gamma^{IJ}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-solitons defined by (
11.9
) and (
11.10
) with endpoints (
11.11
), whose start and end correspond to
\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{VW}}(\theta)
and
\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{VW}}(\theta)
, respectively.
In other words, we can write

\text{CF}_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}\left(\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{VW}}(\theta),\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{VW}}(\theta)\right)_{\pm}=\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{r}}(p^{IJ}_{\text{VW},\pm}(\theta))\,,

(11.19)

where
\text{CF}_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}(\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{VW}}(\theta),\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{VW}}(\theta))_{\pm}
is a vector representing a
\gamma^{IJ}_{\pm}(t,\theta,\mathfrak{A}_{4})
-soliton, such that
\text{Re}\left(W^{I}_{4}(\theta)\right)<\text{Re}\left(W^{J}_{4}(\theta)\right)
.
This will lead us to the one-to-one identification
64
64

64

Once again, just as in
footnote 45
, the ‘
\theta
’ label is omitted in the following expression.

\boxed{\text{Hom}(\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{VW}},\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{VW}})_{\pm}\Longleftrightarrow\text{HF}^{G}_{d_{r}}(p^{IJ}_{\text{VW},\pm})}

(11.20)

where the RHS is proportional to the identity class when
I=J
, and zero when
I\leftrightarrow J
since the
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-soliton only moves in one direction from
\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{VW}}(\theta)
to
\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{VW}}(\theta)
.

Soliton String Theory from the 2d LG Model

Just as in

§

9.4
, the 2d gauged LG model in
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
with action (
11.14
) can be interpreted as a soliton string theory in
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
.
The dynamics of this soliton string theory in
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
will be governed by the BPS worldsheet equations determined by setting to zero the expression within the squared terms of (
11.14
), where
(\mathcal{A}^{M},\mathcal{B}^{M})
are scalars on the worldsheet corresponding to the holomorphic coordinates on
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
.
At an arbitrary instant in time whence
d_{\tau}\mathcal{A}^{M}=0=d_{\tau}\mathcal{B}^{M}
therein, the dynamics of
(\mathcal{A}^{M},\mathcal{B}^{M})
along
t
will be governed by the soliton equations

\derivative{\mathcal{A}^M}{t}=-[A_{t}-iA_{\tau},\mathcal{A}^{M}]-g^{M\bar{N}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}\left(\frac{i\zeta}{2}\partialderivative{W_4}{\mathcal{A}^N}\right)^{*}\,,\qquad\derivative{\mathcal{B}^M}{t}=-[A_{t}+iA_{\tau},\mathcal{B}^{M}]+g^{M\bar{N}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}\left(\frac{i\zeta}{2}\partialderivative{W_4}{\mathcal{B}^N}\right)^{*}\,.

(11.21)

The Normalized 6d-Spin
(7)
Partition Function, LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-soliton String Scattering, and Maps of an
A_{\infty}
-structure

Since our 6d-Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
is semi-classical, its normalized partition function can be regarded as a sum over tree-level scattering amplitudes of the LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-soliton strings defined by (
11.15
).
The BPS worldsheet underlying such a tree-level scattering amplitude is similar to
Fig. 6
, where instead the endpoints of each string being
\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{DT}}
, it is now understood to be
\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{VW}}
.

In other words, we can, like in (
9.35
), express the normalized 6d-Spin
(7)
partition function as

\widetilde{\mathcal{Z}}_{\text{6d-Spin}(7),CY_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}}(G)=\sum_{n_{m}}\mu^{n_{m}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}\,,\qquad n_{m}\in\{1,2,\dots,m-1\}

(11.22)

where each

\boxed{\mu^{n_{m}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}:\bigotimes_{i=1}^{n_{m}}\text{Hom}\left(\mathcal{E}^{I_{i}}_{\text{VW}},\mathcal{E}^{I_{i+1}}_{\text{VW}}\right)_{-}\longrightarrow\text{Hom}\left(\mathcal{E}^{I_{1}}_{\text{VW}},\mathcal{E}^{I_{n_{m}+1}}_{\text{VW}}\right)_{+}}

(11.23)

is a scattering amplitude of
n_{m}
incoming LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-soliton strings
\text{Hom}(\mathcal{E}^{I_{1}}_{\text{VW}},\mathcal{E}^{I_{2}}_{\text{VW}})_{-},\dots,\text{Hom}(\mathcal{E}^{I_{n_{m}}}_{\text{VW}},\mathcal{E}^{I_{n_{m}+1}}_{\text{VW}})_{-}
and a single outgoing LG
\mathfrak{A}_{4}^{\theta}
-soliton string
\text{Hom}(\mathcal{E}^{I_{1}}_{\text{VW}},\mathcal{E}^{I_{n_{m}+1}}_{\text{VW}})_{+}
with left and right boundaries as labeled, whose underlying worldsheet can be regarded as a disc with
n_{m}+1
vertex operators at the boundary.
That is,
\mu^{n_{m}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}
counts pseudoholomorphic discs with
n_{m}+1
punctures at the boundary that are mapped to
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
according to the BPS worldsheet equations (
11.13
).

Just as in

§

9.4
, the collection of
\mu^{n_{m}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}
maps in (
11.23
) can be regarded as composition maps defining an
A_{\infty}
-structure.

An FS Type
A_{\infty}
-category of VW Configurations on
CY_{2}

Altogether, this means that the normalized partition function of 6d-Spin
(7)
theory on
CY_{2}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
as expressed in (
11.22
), manifests a
novel
FS type
A_{\infty}
-category defined by the
\mu^{n_{m}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{4}}
maps (
11.23
) and the one-to-one identification (
11.20
), where the
m
objects
\{\mathcal{E}^{1}_{\text{VW}},\mathcal{E}^{2}_{\text{VW}},\dots,\mathcal{E}^{m}_{\text{VW}}\}
correspond to (
\theta
-deformed) VW configurations on
CY_{2}
(with the scalar and one of the linearly-independent components of the self-dual two-form field being zero)!

We may continue to repeat the procedure of these past three sections by performing a KK reduction of
CY_{2}
along an
S^{1}
circle within, to physically derive an FS type
A_{\infty}
-category of a three-manifold.
However, for a compact
CY_{2}
, that would mean we would need to specialize to the trivial case of
CY_{2}=T^{4}
to derive an FS type
A_{\infty}
-category of objects on
T^{3}
.
We will choose not to consider this here, as we have previously studied such a case more generally as an FS type
A_{\infty}
-category of Hitchin configurations on non-trivial three-manifolds in
[
15
,
\lx@sectionsign
9]
.

Topological Invariance and a Relation Amongst the Floer Homologies and
A_{\infty}
-categories

In this section, we will first exploit the topological invariance of Spin
(7)
theory to relate the Floer homologies derived in

§

3
–

§

8
to one another.
Then, we will do the same for the FS type
A_{\infty}
-categories derived in

§

9
–

§

11
.
After which, we will explain how these FS type
A_{\infty}
-categories would categorify the Floer homologies.
Finally, we will summarize all our results and obtain a web of relations and correspondences amongst the Floer homologies and FS type
A_{\infty}
-categories.
In turn, these results would serve as physical proofs and generalizations of the mathematical conjectures by Hohloch-Noetzel-Salamon
[
21
]
, Salamon
[
26
]
, and Bousseau
[
5
]
.

12.1
Topological Invariance of Spin
(7)
Theory and the Floer Homologies

Relating the Gauge-theoretic Floer Homologies of

§

3
–

§

5

Recall that the topological invariance of (the
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology of) Spin
(7)
theory in all directions means that we can relate the partition functions (
3.11
), (
4.7
), and (
5.6
) as

(12.1)

where
S^{1}
and
\widehat{S}^{1}
are circles of fixed and variable radii, respectively.

The relations in (
12.1
) are consistent, in that they have a one-to-one correspondence in their summations over ‘
j
’, ‘
k
’, and ‘
l
’.
Specifically, each ‘
j
’, ‘
k
’, and ‘
l
’ corresponds to a solution of (the simultaneous vanishing of the LHS and RHS of) (
3.8
), (
4.3
), and (
5.1
), respectively, where (
5.1
) is obtained via a KK reduction of (
4.3
), which in turn is obtained via a KK reduction of (
3.8
).

In short, we have a
novel
equivalence amongst gauge-theoretic Floer homologies of seven, six, and five-manifolds.

Relating the Floer Homologies of

§

6
and

§

7
, and a Physical Proof of Hohloch-Noetzel-Salamon’s Mathematical Conjecture

Topological invariance (of the
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology of) Spin
(7)
theory in all directions also means that we can relate the partition functions (
3.11
) and (
6.15
) as

\boxed{\hbox to335.85pt{\vbox to28.89pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 167.92183pt\lower-13.09924pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

(12.5)

where
\widehat{CY_{2}}
is a
CY_{2}
with variable size.

The relations in (
12.2
) to (
12.5
) are consistent as well, in that they have a one-to-one correspondence in their summations over ‘
j
’ and ‘
s
’.
Specifically, each ‘
j
’ and ‘
s
’ corresponds to a solution of (the simultaneous vanishing of the LHS and RHS of) (
3.8
) and (
6.11
) (on the specific
HC_{3}
’s), respectively, where (
6.11
) is obtained via a topological reduction of (
3.8
) along
CY_{2}
.

In short, we have a
novel
equivalence amongst (i) gauge-theoretic Floer homologies of various seven-manifolds, (ii) hyperkähler Floer homologies of instanton moduli spaces specified by hypercontact three-manifolds, and (iii) symplectic and symplectic intersection Floer homologies of certain spaces of instantons.

Note that the relation between hyperkähleric
\text{HHKF}_{d_{s}}(HC_{3},\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2}))
and gauge-theoretic
\text{HF}^{\text{Spin}(7)\text{-inst}}_{d_{j}}(HC_{3}\times CY_{2},G)
was conjectured by Hohloch-Noetzel-Salamon
[
21
]

[
26
,
\lx@sectionsign
5]
.
Therefore, in arriving at (
12.2
), we have furnished a physical proof of their mathematical conjecture.

In the above, just as there is a one-to-one correspondence in the summations over ‘
j
’ and ‘
s
’, for similar reasons, there would be a one-to-one correspondence in the summations over ‘
k
’ and ‘
u
’, as well as over ‘
u
’ and ‘
v
’.

In short, we have (i) a
novel
equivalence of Atiyah-Floer dualities between gauge-theoretic Floer homologies and symplectic intersection Floer homologies, and (ii) a
novel
equivalence of symplectic intersection Floer homologies of certain spaces of instantons.

Note that the relation between the hyperkähleric
\text{HHKF}_{d_{s}}(HC_{3},\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2}))
and gauge-theoretic
\text{HF}^{\text{Spin}(7)\text{-inst}}(CY_{2}\times HC_{3},G)
was conjectured by Salamon as being analogous to an Atiyah-Floer duality
[
26
,
\lx@sectionsign
5]
.
Indeed, we do find, from (
12.4
) and (
12.6
), that the two Floer homologies (with
HC_{3}=I\times T^{2}
) are related to each other by an Atiyah-Floer type duality between a symplectic intersection and a gauge-theoretic Floer homology.
Therefore, we have furnished a physical proof of his mathematical conjecture.

Relating Symplectic Floer Homologies

Because of the topological invariance of Spin
(7)
theory in all directions, we would also have a series of relations involving the symplectic Floer homologies starting with the one on the RHS of (
7.1
).

S^{1}
circles of the 4d sigma model on
T^{3}\times\mathbb{R}
with target space
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
and action (
6.12
) (with
HC_{3}=T^{3}
), we will get the following series of relations involving symplectic Floer homologies of certain spaces of instantons on
CY_{2}
:

(12.8)

where
\text{HSF}^{\text{hol}}_{d_{x}}(L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})
and
\text{HSF}^{\text{const}}_{d_{y}}(L\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})
are symplectic Floer homologies of
L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
and
L\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
, generated by time-invariant holomorphic and constant maps from
T^{2}
and
S^{1}
to
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
, respectively.

The relations in (
12.8
) are consistent, in that they have a one-to-one correspondence in their summations over ‘
s
’, ‘
x
’, and ‘
y
’, for reasons similar to that given earlier.

In short, we have a
novel
equivalence amongst symplectic Floer homologies of certain spaces of instantons.

Relating Symplectic Intersection Floer Homologies

Once again, because of the topological invariance of (the
\mathcal{Q}
-cohomology of) Spin
(7)
theory in all directions, we can derive yet another relation starting with the bottom RHS of (
12.5
).
Specifically, by KK reducing the spatial
S^{1}
circle of the 4d sigma model on
I\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
in

§

7.3
, we will obtain a 3d sigma model on
I\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
with target space
L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
.
In turn, this 3d sigma model can be recast as a 2d A-model on
I\times\mathbb{R}
with target
L\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})
, which will enable us to physically realize an intersection Floer homology of intersecting A
θ
-branes

(12.9)

where
\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{d_{r}}(\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}),P_{0},P_{1})
is a symplectic instersection Floer homology of intersecting A
θ
-branes
P_{*}(\theta)\subset\mathcal{M}(\mathbb{R},\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}})
.

The relation in (
12.9
) is consistent, in that the summations over ‘
s
’ and ‘
r
’ have a one-to-one correspondence, for reasons similar to that given earlier.

In short, we have a
novel
equivalence between symplectic intersection Floer homologies of certain spaces of instantons.

Summarizing the Relations Amongst the Floer Homologies

We can summarize the various relations amongst the Floer homologies obtained hitherto in
Fig. 7
, where the radii of the
\widehat{S}^{1}
circles and sizes of the
\widehat{CY_{2}}
’s can be varied;

dashed lines
indicate an equivalence that is due to dimensional/topological reduction;

undashed lines
indicate an equivalence that is not due to any dimensional/topological reduction;

bold rectangles
indicate a novel Floer homology that has not been conjectured before;
and
regular rectangles
indicate a Floer homology that has been conjectured.

\sum_{j}\text{HF}^{\text{Spin}(7)\text{-inst}}_{d_{j}}(G_{2},G)
\sum_{k}\text{HHF}^{G_{2}\text{-M}}_{d_{k}}(CY_{3},G)
\sum_{l}\text{HHF}^{\text{DT}}_{d_{l}}(CY_{2}\times S^{1},G)
\sum_{s}\text{HHKF}_{d_{s}}\left(HC_{3},\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})\right)
\sum_{s}\text{HHKF}_{d_{s}}\left(T^{3},\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})\right)
\sum_{s}\text{HHKF}_{d_{s}}\left(I\times T^{2},\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})\right)
\sum_{s}\text{HHKF}_{d_{s}}\left(I\times S^{1}\times\mathbb{R},\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})\right)
\sum_{s}\text{HSF}^{\text{Fuet}}_{d_{s}}\left(L^{3}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}\right)
\sum_{x}\text{HSF}^{\text{hol}}_{d_{x}}\left(L^{2}\mathcal{M}^{G,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}\right)

\sum_{s}\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{d_{s}}\left(\mathcal{M}\left(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}\right),\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1}\right)

\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{HW}}(\theta)
, and
\mathcal{E}^{*}_{\text{VW}}(\theta)
corresponds to a solution of (
9.19
), (
10.11
), and (
11.11
), respectively, where (
11.11
) is obtained via a KK reduction of (
10.11
), which in turn is obtained via a KK reduction of (
9.19
).

The identifications in (
12.11
) are also consistent, in that there is also a one-to-one correspondence amongst the various
\mu^{n_{*}}_{\mathfrak{A}_{*}}
’s.
Specifically, each ‘
n_{k}
’, ‘
n_{l}
’, and ‘
n_{m}
’, corresponds to a pseudoholomorphic disc with
n_{*}+1
punctures at the boundary whose definition is rooted in (
9.4
), (
10.1
), and (
11.1
), respectively, where (
11.1
) is obtained via KK dimensional reduction of (
10.1
), which in turn is obtained via a KK reduction of (
9.4
).

In short, we have (i) a
novel
equivalence amongst the morphisms of FS type
A_{\infty}
-categories of six, five, and four-manifolds, and (ii) a
novel
identification amongst their corresponding composition maps.

Thus, we have a
novel
correspondence amongst FS type
A_{\infty}
-categories of six, five, and four-manifolds. In particular, one can expect the existence of a mathematical functor between these FS type
A_{\infty}
-categories (which maps their objects, morphisms, and
A_{\infty}
composition maps), that has a physical interpretation as an invariance under KK dimensional reduction.

An Atiyah-Floer Type Correspondence of the FS Type
A_{\infty}
-category of

§

9

From (
9.41
) and (
9.46
), we have

\boxed{\begin{gathered}\text{Hom}\left(\mathcal{E}^{I}_{\text{DT}}(\theta),\mathcal{E}^{J}_{\text{DT}}(\theta)\right)_{\pm}\\
\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 20.00003pt\hskip 10.00002pt\;\,\displaystyle\left\Updownarrow\vphantom{\Big(}\right.\scriptstyle{CY_{3}=CY_{3}^{\prime}\bigcup_{CY_{2}}CY_{3}^{\prime\prime}}\\
\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{*}\left(\mathcal{M}\left(\mathbb{R},L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}\right),\mathcal{P}_{0},\mathcal{P}_{1}\right)\\
\displaystyle\left\Updownarrow\vphantom{\Big(}\right.\\
\text{Hom}\left(\text{Hom}\left[\mathcal{L}^{I}_{0}(\theta),\mathcal{L}^{I}_{1}(\theta)\right],\text{Hom}\left[\mathcal{L}^{J}_{0}(\theta),\mathcal{L}^{J}_{1}(\theta)\right]\right)_{\pm}\end{gathered}}

(12.12)

In short, we have a
novel
Atiyah-Floer type correspondence amongst (i) the morphisms of an FS type
A_{\infty}
-category of a six-manifold, (ii) a symplectic intersection Floer homology of the loop space of instantons, and (iii) a Hom-category of morphisms between isotropic-coisotropic branes.

Another Hom-category and a Physical Proof of Bousseau’s Mathematical Conjecture

Applying the arguments used to establish the one-to-one identification of (
9.46
) to the RHS of (
12.9
), we will have yet another one-to-one identification

\text{HSF}^{\text{Int}}_{*}\left(\mathcal{M}\left(\mathbb{R},\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}\right),P_{0},P_{1}\right)\Longleftrightarrow\text{Hom}\left(\text{Hom}\left[L^{I}_{0}(\theta),L^{I}_{1}(\theta)\right],\text{Hom}\left[L^{J}_{0}(\theta),L^{J}_{1}(\theta)\right]\right)\,.

(12.13)

This, (
9.46
), and (
12.9
), would then mean that we have the following relation between the Hom-category of morphisms between isotropic-coisotropic branes of
L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
and
\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
:

\begin{gathered}\text{Hom}\left(\text{Hom}\left[\mathcal{L}^{I}_{0}(\theta),\mathcal{L}^{I}_{1}(\theta)\right],\text{Hom}\left[\mathcal{L}^{J}_{0}(\theta),\mathcal{L}^{J}_{1}(\theta)\right]\right)_{\pm}\\
\qquad\qquad\displaystyle\left\downarrow\vphantom{\Big(}\right.S^{1}=\widehat{S}^{1}\\
\text{Hom}\left(\text{Hom}\left[L^{I}_{0}(\theta),L^{I}_{1}(\theta)\right],\text{Hom}\left[L^{J}_{0}(\theta),L^{J}_{1}(\theta)\right]\right)_{\pm}\,,\end{gathered}

(12.14)

where the spatial
S^{1}
is related to the loop in
L\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta,CY_{2}}_{\text{inst}}
.

Therefore, from (
9.45
) and the identification of
\mathcal{E}_{\text{DT}}^{*}(\theta)
as generators of the
\theta
-generalized holomorphic
G_{2}
instanton Floer homology of
CY_{3}
,
66
66

66

Recall from

§

4.2
that the
\theta
-generalized holomorphic
G_{2}
instanton Floer homology of
CY_{3}
is generated by
\theta
-deformed DT configurations on
CY_{3}
with the scalar being zero.

we have

\boxed{\begin{gathered}\text{Hom}\left(\text{Hom}\left[L^{I}_{0}(\theta),L^{I}_{1}(\theta)\right],\text{Hom}\left[L^{J}_{0}(\theta),L^{J}_{1}(\theta)\right]\right)_{\pm}\\
\displaystyle\left\Updownarrow\vphantom{\Big(}\right.\\

This is a
novel
correspondence amongst (i) a Hom-category of morphisms between Lagrangian submanifolds of
\mathcal{M}^{G,\theta}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
, (ii) an FS type
A_{\infty}
-category whose objects are
\theta

\theta

CY_{3}
with the scalar being zero), and (iii) a
\theta
-generalized holomorphic
G_{2}
instanton Floer homology of
CY_{3}
.

When
\theta=0
, we would have a correspondence amongst (i) a Hom-category of morphisms between Lagrangian submanifolds of
\mathcal{M}^{G}_{\text{inst}}(CY_{2})
(which, for
CY_{2}
a complex algebraic surface, span the space of holomorphic vector bundles
[
11
]
that can be extended to all of
CY_{3}=CY_{3}^{\prime}\bigcup_{CY_{2}}CY_{3}^{\prime\prime}
), (ii) an FS type
A_{\infty}
-category whose objects are holomorphic vector bundles on
CY_{3}
, and (iii) a holomorphic
G_{2}
instanton Floer homology of
CY_{3}
, as conjectured by Bousseau
[
5
,
\lx@sectionsign
2.8]
. Thus, we have furnished a physical proof and generalization (for general
\theta\neq 0
) of Bousseau’s mathematical conjecture.

12.3
FS Type
A_{\infty}
-categories Categorifying Floer Homologies

Introducing Higher Categorical Structures in Spin
(7)
Theory

Referring to

§

3
, we see that when we replace one of the spatial directions of the general Spin
(7)
-manifold with an
\mathbb{R}
line to consider
\text{Spin}(7)=G_{2}\times\mathbb{R}
, the Spin
(7)
partition function will be associated with a Spin
(7)
instanton Floer homology 0-category (see (
3.11
)).

Referring to

§

9
, we see that if we further replace one of the spatial directions of the general
G_{2}
-manifold with an
\mathbb{R}
line to consider
\text{Spin}(7)=CY_{3}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
, the Spin
(7)
partition function will be associated with an FS type
A_{\infty}
-category of
CY_{3}
1-category (see (
9.35
), (
9.36
), and (
9.33
)).

Clearly, one can introduce higher categorical structures in Spin
(7)
theory by replacing spatial directions of the Spin
(7)
-manifold with
\mathbb{R}
lines. The more
\mathbb{R}
lines there are, the higher the categorical structure. In fact, this scheme works for any gauge theory, not just Spin
(7)
theory (see
[
15
]
).

The FS Type
A_{\infty}
-category of

§

9
Categorifying the Gauge-theoretic Floer Homology of

§

4

A_{\infty}
-category of
CY_{3}
1-category in

§

are the endpoints of the
\mathfrak{A}_{6}^{0}
-solitons corresponding to DT configurations on
CY_{3}
with the scalar being zero. Recall also that these configurations generate the holomorphic
G_{2}
instanton Floer homology of
CY_{3}
0-category in

§

4
. In other words, the FS type
A_{\infty}
-category of
CY_{3}
categorifies the holomorphic
G_{2}
instanton Floer homology of
CY_{3}
.

A_{\infty}

are the endpoints of the

with two of the three linearly-independent components of the self-dual two-form field being zero. Recall also that these configurations generate the holomorphic DT Floer homology of
CY_{2}\times S^{1}
0-category in

5
.

The configurations which generate the holomorphic DT Floer homology of

.

In other words, the FS type

.

Summarizing the Scheme of Categorification, and a 2-category

In short, we see that to configurations on a
D
-manifold,
M_{D}
, one can associate a Floer homology of
M_{D}
0-category realized by the partition function of a gauge theory on
M_{D}\times\mathbb{R}
, which, in turn, can be categorified into an FS type
A_{\infty}
-category of
M_{D}
1-category realized by the partition function of a gauge theory on
M_{D}\times\mathbb{R}^{2}
.

By continuing this scheme of categorification, the FS type
A_{\infty}
-category of
M_{D}
1-category can be further categorified into a 2-category of
M_{D}
that is realized by the partition function of a gauge theory on
M_{D}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
.
Indeed, as we will show in a sequel paper
[
16
]
, such a 2-category would be one that is a gauge-theoretic generalization of the Fueter 2-category recently developed by Bousseau
[
5
]
and Doan-Rezchikov
[
8
]
.
